# Railhead: developer notes

Read this first every session, together with `design-doc.md` (the design) and `CLAUDE.md` (how we work).
A fresh session should also read `plans/handover.md` if it exists.

## What the project is

Railhead (working title) is an isometric, low-poly railroad game set in 1866, built for phones first.
The whole game is one file, `index.html`, using three.js r128 loaded from cdnjs.
The full design is in `design-doc.md`. The original PDF it was converted from is kept in `docs/`.

## Platform decisions (2026-11-01)

These override the original design document wherever they disagree. Details are in `design-doc.md`, section 0 (decisions D1 to D11).

- Phones first, touch first, portrait and landscape. A computer with mouse and keyboard comes second.
- One HTML file, three.js r128 from cdnjs. There is no build step, no TypeScript and no Preact.
- The camera turns in 90-degree steps. The elevation is fixed at the true isometric angle.
- Avatar game: tap to walk there; press and hold walks towards the finger (D6, D7). Walking is part of M0 piece 1.
- Piece 1 uses the final low-poly models for the character, train, track and switch stands (D8).
- Size and speed targets are scaled down for phones (see design-doc section 0).

## How to test

1. Install the test tools once: `npm install`. This only fetches a copy of three.js r128 used by the tests.
2. Run every check: `npm test`. It must pass before every push.
3. Screenshots land in `test-output/`. That folder is not saved to git.

The checks serve the project on a local web address (saving in the browser needs one) and open `index.html` in Chromium through Playwright.
They use phone size (390 x 844 portrait, 844 x 390 landscape, with touch on) and fail on any console error.
Most checks open the page with `?paused`, so the game only advances when the check steps it.

Network note: the cloud test machine cannot reach cdnjs, because its network policy blocks it.
When that happens, the test script serves the identical three.js r128 file from `node_modules/three` instead.
The page itself still points at cdnjs.

Since D24 (the station rework) a new game starts in the **hub world** (`'hub'`, see "Station rework (D24)"). The checks written before it test the M1 real start (the hub and Willow Ford, see "The real start"): `tests/browser.js` opens the page with `world=start` unless the check's query names a world itself, so they keep testing what they tested. Checks written for the M0 test yard's layout open the page with `&world=yard` (a new game, and `__railhead.newState`, then use the test yard); `check-hubworld.js` and the D24 builders' checks open `&world=hub`. `__railhead.newState(seed)` makes a game in the page's world; inside the game `newState(seed)` with no world is still the real start (the debug scenarios written for it).
Since D25 (the prototype) a new game starts in the **prototype world** (`'proto'`, drawn as pixel art; see "Prototype (D25)"); `tests/browser.js` is unchanged (it still opens `world=start` unless the query names a world), so every older check keeps testing its world. The D25 checks open `&world=proto` (and `&world=` with no value for the page's default).

Current checks:
- `check-rules.js`: no `Math.random()` in the game.
- `check-boot.js`: the page loads in both orientations with no errors and three.js r128, and the draw calls are reported. It saves screenshots at start and in a busy moment.
- `check-track.js`: the test yard passes the track validator, and the validator catches a tight curve, a sharp corner and reverse curves with no straight between them.
- `check-trains.js`: a train sent to the siding finds the way and stops on the spot. A switch under a train is locked. Switch 3 changes the path. A dead end and a switch set against the train both stop it 0.5u short. The player throws a switch. Plus regression checks from the piece 1 review: locks freed after arrival and Stop, a braking train keeps the 0.5u gap, no instant reversal, throws only within reach, camera-side approach to the train, walks beyond the map edge end.
- `check-switches.js`: manual switches (D20); see "Manual switches".
- `check-determinism.js`: two runs of 3 game days with the same commands give the same state hash.
- `check-save.js`: export and import, a real browser save surviving a page reload, and every sample save in `tests/fixtures/` still loading with its recorded hash. Damaged saves fall back to a fresh game with no console errors; the newer of the IndexedDB save and the quick save wins.
- `check-debug.js`: with `?debug=1` each overlay draws (screenshots `debug-*.png`), the debug bar opens by touch, the siding and spur scenarios load, 32x warp runs far faster than 1x, and the sim gives the same hash with debug on or off. Without it, `window.rh` is undefined and the draw calls are unchanged.
- `check-paths.js`: the player's path-finding, in the sim: from the platform in front of the depot a tap behind it walks round it without entering any footprint and without zig-zag; a tap on the far side of the stopped train walks round its end; a tap inside the depot ends at the nearest reachable spot with the "Something is in the way" message; a train running across the path makes the path change round it and the walk still arrives; a switch stand behind a train is reached round it and thrown from within reach; press and hold into a wall slides along it; a player inside a building walks out; pushing the handcart, a walk whose shortest way runs along the platform goes round it, never onto the platform or dock, and arrives; the same commands twice give the same positions and hash. Reports grid build and path search times. Screenshot `paths-overlay-portrait.png` (the Path overlay round the depot).
- `check-touch.js`: real touch events for tap to walk, hold to walk, tap a switch, tap the train (panel opens, Send to sets a route) and pinch to zoom. Since D15 the yard is 1.6 times bigger: the camera pans to the switch stand, the train and the siding when they are near the edge or out of view (as a player would), and the walk tap is 2u north of the start (1u north is behind the platform's edge as the camera sees it, so a tap there lands on the deck).
- `check-roof.js`: the hub canopy is its own see-through-capable mesh; with the player under it, it fades (opacity at most 0.2, no depth writes); far from it, it is solid; a player hidden behind it from the camera fades it; screenshots `wide-under-canopy-*` and `wide-hub-platform-*` (portrait and landscape, zoom 1.5).
- `check-buildings.js` (test yard, whose platform keeps its old 2.4 x SC depth): every station footprint keeps the section 6 clearance from every track edge (platform and dock flush at 0.9u, others 1.2u from main, 1.0u from yard), the platform top is 0.3u, the platform is 20u x 2.4u with a canopy over the middle 10u only and not past its track-side edge, no footprints overlap (except the depot's bay on the platform and the lamps standing on it), the tool parking spots stay clear of every footprint, the buildings are 2 meshes, draw calls stay under 100, and screenshots of the station.
- `check-world.js`: the ground is flat under the track and in the yard, hills exist, the drawn ground matches `terrainHeight`, noon brightness is kept, night is readable, and lamps glow. It saves screenshots at 05:30, 12:00, 19:30 and 23:00.
- `check-walk.js`: the walk animation (feet do not slide, knees close on the feet, stride matches speed, cadence, lean, ease-out), the walk speeds and no running, the work, carry, push, throw and standing poses measured on the drawn model; walk and anim screenshots.
- `check-people.js`: with `?crowd=200`, all three kits, women and children are present, draw calls (shadow pass included) stay under 100, the crowd update stays under 4 ms a frame, walkers move and freeze when paused. Frame times are reported; the 50 ms limit only applies on a machine with a real graphics chip.

- `check-handbuild.js`: hand-building track (M1 round B). Without `?debug=1`, by real touch: a drag on from the spur end and Stake it make a job and add no track (4 stakes, 2 tie bundles, 1 rail bundle, 1 spike keg for 9.6u, M1's 6u x 1.6); a tap on the stakes starts work, which stops with "Out of ties". An amber plan has Stake it disabled and `stakeJob` refused for earthwork; a second job on the same track end and a plan with a fraction are refused. Through the sim: the handbuild scenario's 32u siding (M1's 20u x 1.6) is built by one `jobWork` in the order ties, rail pair, spikes per 8u stretch; work time within 10% of the D12 figures divided by 1.6 (D15: each step is quicker by the scale so a job of the same shape takes the same real time; measured 148.5 against 145.0 game minutes, plus 33.5 of walking; 182 game minutes in all, 3.0 real minutes at 1x since D17's slower clock); the track joins, passes the validator, the job is removed and train No. 1 is routed onto the new siding and arrives. Each missing material (ties, rails, spikes, the hardware crate) stops the work with its message. A tie bundle carried and put down beside the job is delivered. A walk stops the work. Cancel mid-job keeps every piece (totals unchanged, conservation passes), and the plan staked again builds with the returned items. A 9.6u job hands back 8 ties and 32 spikes as part-used items, which count for the next job. Save mid-step: export/import and a real reload resume with the same hash. Same commands, same hash. - `check-passengers.js`: passengers (M1 package 5). In the sim, 16 game hours: travellers come at the documented rates (hub about 45 between 06:00 and 21:00, Willow Ford about 20 when its station exists, none outside those hours, 1 to 3 off each Overland visit), each wants the other station, nobody leaves the ticket line (D20 (3): nobody gives up, everyone who came is still in line after 16 hours, the give-up counts stay 0, at most 48 wait at a station), and no traveller ever stands inside a building or a train (No. 1 runs round the loop meanwhile). Fares: 40 cents hub to Willow Ford, 10 cents minimum. By real touch (`?debug=1`, `rh.scenario('passengers')`): a tap on the ticket window walks the player there and staffs it; tickets then sell every 30 game seconds on average (each within one 8 s tick), 40 cents each, each fare one ledger entry (category `fares`) with the cash up by the same and `econCheckMoney` clean; the readout and its 48 px Leave button; `stationDepartures` lists the timetable's departures to Willow Ford, soonest first, C3's station card near the window shows them, and which trains take travellers (on the timetable only for a later stop of its run); walking away stops the sales. Draw calls with 40 travellers waiting under 100, and equal with a crowd of 30 whether or not the travellers are there (shared meshes). Boarding in the sim: 5 step off first, then 12 board (the combine's seats), at 6 game seconds each through the one door; 8 are left waiting; the 5 walk to the station's edge and leave the map; nobody boards a moving train; export/import mid-boarding gives the same hash. On the real line: 6 ticketed travellers board No. 1 at the hub, it is sent to Willow Ford's platform, and all 6 step off there and walk off the map. (D13) Unstaffed Willow Ford: the hub's travellers without tickets stay behind (the hub is staffed), no fare is charged at Willow Ford to those with tickets, travellers in line at Willow Ford board without a ticket and each pays 40 cents on arrival at the hub (one `fares` ledger entry each, the cash up by the same, `econCheckMoney` clean). Same commands, same hash. A real page reload keeps the travellers. Screenshots `passengers-queue-ready-portrait.png`, `passengers-queue-portrait.png`, `passengers-boarding-portrait.png`, `passengers-reload-portrait.png`.

- `check-office.js`: the office, telegrams, alerts and Look (M1 package 7, builder D1, D13). In the sim: a new game has an empty inbox; `telegramSend` keeps a telegram with its buttons and refuses bad ones; `tgAnswer` runs the button's command once (a second answer refused); `tgRead` one or all; at most 150 kept. Over 3 game days the event table gives a telegram for each town offer, the Overland coming in with our order, a contract going late (and later cancelled), and exactly one for No. 1 waiting at Willow Ford; a low tender gives one telegram and one alert; an empty shelf a 'stock' alert. Ordering and accepting work at the office desk; `useDesk` reaches it; one service can be taken off the timetable. Same commands, same hash; export and import keep the inbox; a v10 save gets an empty one; a new game's first telegram is the tutorial's. In the page, portrait and landscape, by real touch: tap the depot wall at the desk, walk there, the Office button opens the office, the game runs with it open (ticks counted at 1x); each tab's data (offers with Accept, the market's goods with buy and sell prices and an 8-week line, the ledger's table and loans, No. 1's place, wood, water and cars, both services' stop tables); Start and Stop a service; + twice on flour and Send order ($2,500.00 to $2,492.00 on screen, money check clean); a telegram slides in under the clock, the count shows 1, a tap opens the inbox with it read, a button answers it and runs its command; the telegraph button opens and closes the inbox; an out-of-stock alert icon moves the camera to the general store; Look cards for an item (waybill), No. 1 (consist, next stop) and the general store (stock, wants), a tap elsewhere closes the card, Look is off after one look; walking away closes the office; draw calls equal with the office open and closed. In 9 states (the tutorial chip, office open, the slide-in, the inbox, alerts with carrying, Centre showing, a Look card, `?debug=1` with the debug bar closed and open) no two page elements overlap (bounding boxes) and every visible button is 48 px or more. Screenshots `office-<tab>-portrait/landscape.png`, `office-inbox-*`, `office-slide-portrait`, `office-alert-portrait`, `office-look-item/train/store-portrait`, `office-alerts-debug-*`.
- `check-ride.js`: riding our own train (D13). The real start with No. 1 on the timetable, ready at the hub's platform. A tap on the combine's door while pushing the hand truck is refused with the reason; without it, a tap on the door (real touch) walks the player there and boards in one tick (0.1 game minutes); aboard, the player is drawn (in the open combine, D15) and with the car, the riding readout and its Step off button (48 px or more) show, and walking and uncoupling are refused. Step off by touch while the train moves is refused with the reason (the readout says it is moving). Riding to Willow Ford the camera stays within 1.5u of the car; mid-line a save (export and import) resumes exactly (same hash 40 ticks later), and a real page reload too. At Willow Ford the readout says the train stands there, and Step off by touch puts the player on the platform, drawn again. In the sim: the same ride twice gives the same hash; the timetable's departure and arrival ticks are the same with or without the rider; with the player aboard, 12 of 14 ticketed travellers board the combine's 12 seats. Landscape: the readout's Step off button is on screen and at least 48 px. Screenshots `ride-riding-portrait.png`, `ride-stepoff-portrait.png`, `ride-riding-landscape.png`.
- `check-cutaway.js`: the cutaway into the combine while riding (D15); see "The cutaway into the combine".
- `check-tutorial.js`: the investor's tutorial (M1 package 8, builder D2). A bot plays a new game through commands only (no debug tools) and does what each telegram asks; every step's telegram comes in order and the tutorial ends within days 1 and 2 (with seed 1866 on day 1 at 18:55), money and item conservation hold, and each step with a place has a hint marker place. Skip tutorial ends it at once, and a day of play after it (the M1 bot) brings no tutorial telegram; a second skip is refused. A save at the tickets step loads with the same hash, resumes that step and ends with the same hash as the game played straight through; the game played twice gives the same hash. Every older sample save loads with the tutorial done and sends none. In the page, portrait and landscape: the Current task chip opens D1's inbox at the step's telegram (OK and Skip tutorial, 48 px or more, opening it marks the telegram read, OK by touch answers it, closes the inbox and moves on), a later telegram (sort) with no buttons, closed with the inbox's X, and the step's place on screen; screenshots `tutorial-first-*.png`, `tutorial-later-*.png`, `tutorial-marker-*.png`.
- `check-arrows.js`: the helper arrows (D20 (6)): at each tutorial step the arrow stands over the right place (since D21 the work board for the contract and the order, ticket counter, work board then switch, interchange then the Overland's car, bay, door), the first step's arrow never runs off the top of the screen, the plate "No. 1, yours" over No. 1 (TA's tag is gone), the edge arrow points towards an off-screen place, Skip removes them, the Arrows toggle (48 px) turns the hint arrow on (travellers in line) and off, after the tutorial nothing asks for No. 1 on the timetable (she is on both runs) and, taken off, the arrow goes to her and then her Timetable button; the arrows add at most 2 draw calls and do not change the state; screenshots `arrows-*.png`.
- `check-clarity.js` (D21): all five parts of D21; see "Clarity (D21, builder CL)". Screenshots `clarity-*.png`.
- `check-explain.js` (D22): first-time cards, warnings ahead, the schedule board on its posts, the Work board button, one top line, switch 1 straight at the start; see "Explaining as it happens (D22, builder EX)". Screenshots `explain-*.png`.
- `check-hubworld.js` (D24 foundation): the hub world's layout; see "Station rework (D24)". Screenshots `hubworld-*.png`.
- `check-proto-day.js` (D25 integrator): two days in the prototype played by `tests/proto-bot.js` through touch-level commands; see "Prototype (D25)".
- `check-proto-stall.js` (D25 fix, 2026-11-11): the three causes of the designer's stall (the page frozen with goods in hand, two trains in on top of each other, the Next job line while a track is taken); see "Prototype (D25)".
- D27 (see "D27 (the working day made visual)"): `check-d27world.js` (the foundation), `check-levers.js` (T), `check-onboard.js` (P), `check-handling.js` and `check-delivery.js` (F), `check-townsfolk.js` (W), `check-lessons.js` (U), `check-interiors.js` and `check-pixperf.js` (R), `check-atlas.js` and `check-d27-day.js` (the integrator: two days by the bot with every D27 part on; screenshots `d27-*.png`). The D25 checks open with `?d27=0` (or `&doorline=1`) where they test the D25 flows.
- D32 (see "D32 (line building)"): `check-d32world.js`, `check-worktrain.js`, `check-linerender.js` (Foundation); the other owners' checks have their lines in `run-all.js` and are skipped with a note until their files land.
- D31 (see "D31 (play-test fixes)"): `check-d31.js` (the first freight unloaded by real taps, one tap per good and the queue, the small cart, the pace, the soft see-through patch, `?d31=0`); `tests/d31-lib.js` brings a default game to the first freight for it.
- D32 (see "D32 (line building)"): `check-d32world.js`, `check-worktrain.js`, `check-linerender.js` (Foundation), `check-crew.js`, `check-camp.js` (Crew); the other owners' checks have their lines in `run-all.js` and are skipped with a note until their files land.
- D32 (see "D32 (line building)"): `check-d32world.js`, `check-worktrain.js`, `check-linerender.js` (Foundation), `check-lineplay.js`, `check-linegames.js`, `check-linelessons.js` (Play); the other owners' checks have their lines in `run-all.js` and are skipped with a note until their files land.
- D32 stage 3 (see "D32 stage 3: the bigger town (sim)"): `check-bigtown.js`, `check-towneconomy.js` (Town); `check-townsfolk.js` opens with `?tn=0` (the D27 town).
- `check-protoworld.js` (D25 foundation): the prototype's layout, walking distances, reachability (platform 2 only over the walkway), the first trains in and out with the switches set by hand, the parked systems off over a day, the day's phases, the older worlds in 3D, and the pixel renderer (whole scale and zoom steps, screen to world and back, picking a coach, frame time). Screenshots `protoworld-{start,out,trains}-{portrait,landscape}.png`.
- `check-freight.js` (D24, CG): the freight trains' goods; see "Station rework (D24)". Screenshots `freight-dock-*.png`.
- `check-rrtrains.js` (D24, TR): the railroad's trains over a day with a scripted player; `check-tickets.js` (D24, PX): tickets on board; `check-hubui.js` (D24, UI): the screen guidance (with fakes of PX's and CG's reads, and a part on the real pieces); `check-hub-day.js` (D24, integrator): three game days in the hub played by `tests/hub-bot.js`. See "Station rework (D24)".
- **Cards off in the other checks:** `tests/browser.js` opens the game with the first-time cards off (the Settings choice, `localStorage` `railhead-cards` = 'off', set before the page loads unless already set) unless `openGame(..., { cards: true })`, because a card pauses the game and stands over the middle of the screen; `check-explain.js` opens with them on.
- `tests/run-some.sh check-a check-b ...` runs a few checks one after another, each alone.
- `check-m1.js`: the M1 acceptance criteria (design-doc 22) that a script can check, played by the bot in `tests/m1-bot.js` from a new game (the real start), headless in the page: a full game week with the money rule and item conservation checked at every midnight; every import ordered arrives, goes through the freight shed's bays and on to the business doors (and Willow Ford's store by train); the starting contracts done and none late; the Willow Ford train's departures and arrivals all within 15 game minutes (all 14 runs, day 1's afternoon included since D13: the 8 starting cords; at least one cord on the handcart); fares paid on arrival are reported; the siding built by hand; tickets sold; the pressure evening (the Overland and No. 1 in within 10 game minutes, with a queue at the window, goods still in the set-out cars and No. 1 waiting for wood); the week twice gives the same hash, and a save at day 4 17:00 reloaded gives the same end. Screenshot `m1-pressure-portrait.png`. About 35 to 110 s (a week of sim about 16 to 45 s, depending on the machine). Since D15 the bot's places (piles, the stand by the water tower, the siding's 32u, the hub and Willow Ford limits) are its M1 numbers times `CFG.SCALE`; a cord's room in the tender is `CFG.CORD_U`. Measured after the scale: all 14 runs, worst 7 minutes late, none cancelled; the pressure evening on day 1 (Overland in 18:31, No. 1 in 18:20, 10 minutes apart; 9 on the later days); cash $2,479.72 at the end of the week. The tutorial criterion and the playtester gate are not scriptable yet.

Buildings (piece 3b): each world has its own layout (`BLD_LAYOUTS.yard`, `BLD_LAYOUTS.start`); `BLD_LAYOUT`, `BLD_BAYS` and `TOOL_PARK` are re-pointed to the world in use by `worldUse` (see "The real start"). In the test yard, `BLD_LAYOUT` places a platform, depot (agent's office bay), telegraph shanty, water tower, freight shed with dock, and 4 lamps inside the oval beside the bottom main line (z = 14). The platform (D12, round B) is 20u long (x -10 to 10) and 2.4u deep, top 0.3u, its edge flush at 0.9u from the main line, with a canopy over the middle 10u (stopping 0.05u short of the track-side edge) and a bench under it; lamps 1 and 2 stand on it outside the canopy. To make room, the depot and shanty moved 1u back from the track (a whole number of walk grid cells, so paths round the depot behave as before; the depot's office bay still steps onto the platform's back edge) and the water tower moved east along the track to x 14.6, past the platform's end and switch 1's stand, with lamp 3 beside it. The freight shed, its bays and the hand truck and handcart parking spots did not move. `carryNoCartRects` (player section) now reads the platform and shed sizes from `BLD_EXTENTS`, so the no-handcart areas follow the layout by themselves; the walk grid already reads `buildingFootprints()`. All are merged into one KIT_MAT mesh plus one GLOW_MAT mesh. `buildingFootprints()` gives plan rectangles as plain data (no three.js; the sizes come from `BLD_EXTENTS`, which the recipes also return), so the sim's walk grid can read them; `buildingLights()` gives lamp positions. The player walks round buildings (see Player below). Crowd walkers keep 0.3u clear of every footprint.

Sample saves: `tests/fixtures/save-v1.json` to `save-v17.json` (v17 is the first from the hub world, D24: a new hub game where the player threw switch 2, took the hand truck and picked up a sack of flour from the cargo yard; v12 adds the tutorial, builder D2: its sample is a new game with the welcome telegram unanswered; v11 adds D1's telegram inbox, and its sample has two telegrams, the first read, the second with a button; v6 adds the economy, builder C1; v7 is the first from the real start; v8 adds the timetable, builder C3; v9 adds the passengers, builder C4; v10 adds `S.town`, the placeholder towns' stock, builder C5, and its sample has a sack of flour delivered to the general store) (from v3 the sample also holds two items, one of them on the hand truck the player pushes; from v4 a job staked 6u on from the spur end, with a tie bundle on site; v5 adds the interchange track, the Overland (away) and its set-out cars, builder B4). When the save format changes, bump `SCHEMA_VERSION`, add a step to `migrate()`, and run `node tests/make-fixture.js` to add the new version's sample. Keep the old samples.

## Cargo (M1 package 2)

- **Items** (section "cargo (items and waybills)", after "trains"): `S.items` holds every freight item as columns indexed by item id (`waybill`, `where`, `alive`), plus `next` and the consumption log `used`. Ids are never reused. The API comment block at the top of the section lists the functions: `itemCreate`, `itemMove`, `itemCanMove`, `itemsAt`, `itemConsume`, `itemCheckConservation`. Other code must change items only through them. Each returns `{ ok, why }` and adds an event (`itemCreated`, `itemMoved`, `itemConsumed`, or `itemRefused` with the reason).
- **Places** (`where`): `CAR {stockId}`, `BAY {buildingId, bay}`, `CARRIED {actorId, carrier: HAND | HANDTRUCK | HANDCART, slot}`, `GROUND {x, z}` in hundredths, `STOCKPILE {jobId}`, `BUSINESS {businessId}`. The player's actor id is `'player'`. Cars now have stock ids (`consist[i].id`).
- **Limits**: a bay holds 8; by hand 1, hand truck 3, handcart 6; one way of carrying per actor at a time. Long bundles go by hand only, except cordwood, which may also go on the handcart (D13, 2026-11-02; never on the hand truck). The rule lives in one place, `CARGO_CART_LONG` / `cargoLongCarrierOk` at the top of `cargoRoomProblem`, which the conservation check reads too. A boxcar has 16 slots and a combine 8 (no long bundles). A flatcar takes long bundles, crates, pallets and machinery only (12 long or 8 crates: units of 2 and 3 out of 24). Bulk goods (logs, coal, ore) are not items.
- **Conservation**: `itemCheckConservation(S)` lists problems. Every item ever created is either alive in exactly one real place or logged once as consumed. Every place is within its limits.
- **Commodities**: `COMMODITIES` is the section 21 table (37 rows, prices in whole cents, null where it is not traded).
- **Destination tags**: `CARGO_TAGS` are WILLOW (W), HUB (T), HUBWH (H), RAILHEAD (R), OUTSIDE (O) and ANY (bays only). Each has a colour (colourblind-safe Okabe-Ito colours), a letter and a shape. `itemTag(waybill)` gives an item's tag.
- **Bays**: `BLD_BAYS` in "buildings" holds the bay positions as plain numbers, one bay in front of each of the freight shed's 4 doors. `S.bayTags.freightshed` holds the tags (default Willow Ford, Hub, Outside, Any since D4; before that the second bay was Hub warehouse, which saves from before cargo still get from the 1 -> 2 step, `CARGO_OLD_BAY_TAGS`). Change a tag with the command `{ t: 'setBayTag', b: 'freightshed', bay, tag }`. A non-matching bay is allowed and the move event says `mismatch: true`.
- **Drawing**: `itemPropShapes()` in "models" makes one low-poly shape per item type. "World rendering" draws them with one InstancedMesh per shape and one more for all the tag cards. `cargoDraw` in "drawing each frame" rebuilds the bays and the ground only when an item event happens (`cargoView.dirty`), when there is a new state, or when the camera turns. Carried items follow their actor every frame, through `cargoCarriedPose`, which builder W3 may replace. Items in a car, a stockpile or a business are not drawn. Code that changes items outside a tick calls `__railhead.cargo.redraw()`.
- **Testing**: `?debug=1`, then `rh.scenario('cargo')` or `?scenario=cargo`, gives 37 items (19 in bays, 15 on the ground, 2 in the boxcar, 1 in hand). A normal game starts with none; the Overland brings the first at 06:00 (see "Interchange and the Overland"). Test hooks are in `__railhead.cargo`.

### Provisional choices for the lead (not in the design doc)
- The tag letters and shapes are W Willow Ford (circle), T Hub town (square), H Hub warehouse (hexagon), R Railhead (triangle), O Outside (diamond) and A Any (ring). The colours are Okabe-Ito colours.
- A long bundle is refused on a handcart as well as on a hand truck. Section 9 says it goes "by hand" only. (Changed by D13: cordwood may go on the handcart.)
- Flatcar mixing uses units: a long bundle is 2 and a crate or pallet is 3, out of 24. Machinery is 9 units and is refused in bays and for carrying.
- The placeholder towns are id 0 Hub and id 1 Willow Ford (`CARGO_TOWNS`), until real towns exist.
- An item's tag comes from its waybill. Outside goes to OUTSIDE. Construction or a camp goes to RAILHEAD. A warehouse goes to HUBWH. Town 1 goes to WILLOW, and anything else to HUB.
- The three loose crates and the barrel that stood on the freight dock in M0 are removed, because real items now stand in those bays.
- The per-item event history (`history` in section 20) is not kept, to keep saves small. The move events and the consumption log are the record. There are no free lists, because ids are never reused (that keeps the conservation check simple).
- **Wrong bay mark (D15, builder G):** an item in a bay whose tag does not match it (the move event's `mismatch`) gets a warning mark above its stack: a yellow triangle with a black edge and "!", standing upright facing the camera, 1.7 times a tag's size. It is the tag atlas's last cell (`CARGO_WARN_CELL`) drawn as an extra instance of the one tag mesh, so it costs no draw call. `cargoWarnMarks` makes the list (`cargoView.warn`) when the bays are rebuilt; `bayTagSet` now also marks the props for a redraw.

## Economy (M1 round C, builder C1)

- **Where the code is:** section "economy" (after "construction jobs"; pure sim). The comment block at its top lists `S.econ`, the API and the commands. Commands `useTelegraph`, `econOrder`, `contractAccept`, `contractDecline`, `creditDraw`, `creditRepay` in "commands"; `econTick` is step 8 of `simTick` (after the actors; `econTelBefore` notes a walk to the key before them). Page side: the telegraph panel (`telUi`, `telPanelUpdate`, `econOnEvent`) and the cash readout (`cashHud`) in "HUD and panels"; `econTelTap` in "input".
- **Money:** whole cents. `econPost` is the only thing that changes `S.econ.cash`; every change is a ledger entry `{ t, amount, category, counterparty, ref }` (categories in `ECON_CATEGORIES`). `econCheckMoney(S)` is the money rule (cash = starting cash + the ledger; credit owed = the credit entries; whole cents; known categories; time order), like `itemCheckConservation`.
- **Market:** `S.econ.market` buy and sell per commodity (section 21), with a per-commodity random walk (permille) moved every Monday 00:00 by up to 5% of base from the game's seeded numbers (`S.rng`, in commodity order), times the season factor, kept within 70% to 140% of base; 8 weeks of buy prices in `history`.
- **Telegraph key:** `econTelegraphKey()` is the hub depot's 'telegraph' use spot from `buildingSpots()` when the buildings section has it (C2), else worked out from the depot's layout entry (in front of the office bay window). Tap the bay window: `useTelegraph` walks there; on arrival `reachedTelegraph` opens the panel. Every economy command is refused (`econRefused`) unless the player stands within 3u of the key. The panel (car panel look, 48 px buttons) has three tabs: Order (- and + per good, Send order), Contracts (Accept, Decline; an open contract has a button to order what it still needs), Ledger (Borrow and Repay $500, the latest 12 entries). It closes beyond 3u. While paused, a panel button runs one tick so the money changes at once (as Lay track does).
- **Orders:** paid on order (`imports`). An order rides the first 06:00 or 18:00 Overland at least 6 game hours away. `overlandTick` calls `econLoadOverland` as the Overland comes in (unless it has cars held aboard): every order due, oldest first, while they fit in 6 cars; long bundles and machinery on flatcars, everything else in boxcars (first fit, `econPack`). Waybills: an order for a contract goes to its business with its `contractId`; ties, rails, spikes and hardware go to CONSTRUCTION 0; everything else to WAREHOUSE 0 (the hub warehouse). Events `orderPlaced`, `orderDelivered`.
- **The fixed test delivery** (`OVERLAND_DELIVERY`) now only comes when `S.overland.testDelivery` is set: the `interchange` debug scenario and `check-interchange.js` (`__railhead.interchange.withTestDelivery(st)`). Normal play brings only what was ordered.
- **Selling Outside:** goods consumed `soldOutside` on the Overland's pick-up are paid at the sell price (`econSoldOutside`, one `exports` entry per item; a part-used bundle pays for its pieces); `overlandPickedUp` carries `paid`.
- **Contracts:** `S.econ.contracts` (index = id), section 20 fields plus `period`, `owed`, `items`, `offered`. Businesses in `ECON_BUSINESSES`, which is the town section's `TOWN_BUSINESSES` (0 Hub General Store, 1 Willow Ford General Store, 2 Hub Developer's Yard, 3 Hub Saloon, 4 Hub Boarding House). A new game offers the section 21 first contracts (one commodity each: 6 flour and 4 beans by day 2, 8 lumber and 2 nails by day 4) and a standing one (2 coffee a week for 4 weeks to Willow Ford). `contractOffer(S, spec, ev)` adds offers (for C5). `contractDeliver(S, itemId, ev)` is for C5's local delivery: call it once the item is consumed with reason `business` (or while it is at `BUSINESS {businessId}`); it pays the contract its waybill names, or the oldest open one of that business for that commodity. Delivery contracts pay at once; standing contracts owe and pay on Saturday 18:00. Late: 10% a day off, up to 50%; reputation +2 on time, -4 late, -10 cancelled (5 days late). Offers not answered by their due time expire.
- **Credit line:** up to $5,000 from the Eastern investors, 1% a week charged on Monday 00:00; borrow and repay by telegraph. Cash may go to -$500 (orders beyond that are refused). **Lot fees:** `econLotFee` stub ($25 house, $60 business), called by nothing yet.
- **Cash on screen:** `#cash`, beside the speed buttons under the clock on a narrow phone, beside the clock when the screen is 600 px wide or more; red when negative.
- **Saving:** `S.econ` in the save; the migrate step gives an old save the starting cash and market, and the starting offers dated from its own day.

### Provisional choices for the designer (economy)
- Order cutoff: an order rides the first Overland at least 6 game hours away (orders before 00:00 for the 06:00, before 12:00 for the 18:00), so the first order of a new game comes on day 1's evening train (section 21 tutorial). Section 10 says "1 or 2 days later".
- At most 6 cars of orders per visit; the rest wait for the next visit.
- Willow Ford is 12 miles from the hub for the delivered price (16u a mile; the real map may change it). Scarcity pricing is applied by the town section to the offers it makes (up to +30% when the business has none, C5).
- "By day N" means by the end of day N. Contracts have one commodity each, so the general store's and developer's first contracts are two each.
- Who owns ordered goods: track materials go to construction, other goods to the hub warehouse, goods ordered for a contract to that business.
- Standing contracts pay on Saturday 18:00 (receivables, section 10); delivery contracts pay when delivered. A late contract is cancelled 5 days after its due time (section 10 says "may cancel").
- Contract statuses DECLINED and EXPIRED added to section 20's list.
- The telegraph key is tapped at the depot's office bay window; there is no separate marker on it yet.
- Panel actions while paused run one game tick (as Lay track does).

## Town (M1 package 4, builder C5): placeholder towns

M2 replaces this with real citizens. Design-doc 9 "Local delivery", 11 "Consumption per person per week", 12 "Town businesses", 15 "Starting hub", 21 "Starting world".

- **Where the code is:** section "town" (between "construction jobs" and "economy"; pure sim). The comment block at its top lists the state, rules, commands and events. `townTick` is step 8b of `simTick` (after `econTick`). The buildings' recipes (`TOWN_RECIPES`, `townBuildingsDraw`) are at the end of "buildings"; `buildBuildings` adds their meshes (one per town) and `buildingFootprints()` adds `townFootprints()`. Page side: `townTap` in "input" (before `carryTap`), the door labels (`townSignHud`, `townSignText`) and messages (`townOnEvent`) in "HUD and panels". Small calls in carrying (player section): `carrySpot` knows a BUSINESS place (its door), `carryPutCmd` takes `{ biz }`, `carryArrive` handles `use.k === 'biz'`, `carryFinish` checks the door again, and `carryActorTick` calls `townAfterCarry` after `jobAfterCarry`.
- **One list of businesses:** `TOWN_BUSINESSES` (id, name, short name, town, building kind, weekly rates). The economy's `ECON_BUSINESSES` is the same array. Ids are unchanged from C1: 0 Hub General Store, 1 Willow Ford General Store, 2 Hub Developer's Yard, 3 Hub Saloon, 4 Hub Boarding House.
- **Stock and use:** `S.town.biz[id]` has `shelf` (opening stock, counted but not items, because a new game has no items), `acc` (use since the last whole unit), `short` (units wanted with nothing in stock, for M2) and `offered` (when it last offered a contract per good). Delivered goods are real items at `{ kind: 'BUSINESS', businessId }` until used. Every game hour each rate (milli-units a week) is added to `acc`; each 168,000 is one unit used: the shelf first, then the oldest delivered item (`itemConsume`, reason `business`, logged), else counted as short. Exact whole numbers, so 7 days use exactly `floor(rate / 1000)` units.
- **Offers:** every day at 07:00, a business whose stock plus what its open contracts and offers still bring is under 4 days of use offers a delivery contract by telegraph (`contractOffer`): enough for 2 weeks, due by the end of the 4th day after, at the delivered price plus up to 30% scaled by how far stock is under the low mark (section 10 scarcity; +30% when it has none). Not while an offer for that good is open, and at most every 3 days per good.
- **Delivery at the door:** tap a business (its walls, roof or door spot): carrying something, `carryPut { biz }` walks to the door spot and puts the top item down there in 0.2 game minutes, by hand, hand truck or handcart; carrying nothing, `useBiz { biz }` walks to the door and shows what it wants (`reachedBiz`). Only items whose waybill names that business are taken (`townDeliverWhy`, with the reason in words: another business, the hub warehouse, our own track work, Outside). The item moves to BUSINESS, `contractDeliver` pays for it under its contract (a delivery contract at once, a standing one on Saturday), and `bizDelivered` gives the message. More items for the same business on a hand truck or handcart are put down one after another. An item for that business with no open contract is still taken into stock, unpaid.
- **Buildings:** `TOWN_KINDS` (plan extent, door, name board) and `TOWN_LOTS`, placed by `townAnchor` from the depot of the world in use (`worldUse` points `BLD_LAYOUT` at it; `townPlaced` caches per world). Real start: the hub's Main Street (dirt, 1.2 cm up) runs behind the depot at z about -14, with the developer's yard (x -7), boarding house (x -0.4), general store (x 14.5) and saloon (x 19.6) on its far side facing south towards it, 4 shanty homes on a back lane behind them (z about -22), all inside the hub boundary; Willow Ford's general store (a canvas tent store) stands west of its depot and platform (x 201.5, z -40), beside the settlers' cabins, which stay scenery. Test yard (old saves, debug scenarios): the same lots from the yard's depot (Main Street at z about 3.5), and Willow Ford's store at a spot inside the east loop (x 23.4, z -2). Kit parts as the station (false fronts with blank name boards, porches, GLOW_MAT windows), one mesh plus one glow mesh per town (culled when out of view). Each building is a footprint the walk grid blocks; the door spot is 0.6u out from the front, outside the block.
- **Door labels:** a card above the name board (the job sign style): the name and "Wants 6 flour" (open contracts), "Low on coffee: an offer waits at the telegraph", "Stocked up", or "Tap the door to deliver the flour" while carrying goods for it. Shown within 9u of the player or while carrying goods for that business; nearest first, a card that would overlap a nearer one or any button or panel is hidden.
- **Testing:** `?debug=1&scenario=town` or `rh.scenario('town')`: the store's flour contract and a saloon whiskey contract accepted, flour in hand beside the store, two barrels of whiskey on the ground by the saloon. Hooks in `__railhead.town`.

### Provisional choices for the designer (towns)
- Who each business serves: hub 25 people in 9 households with 8 boarders. The general store serves the other 17 people and 8 households at section 11's rates (flour 4.25, beans 2.55, meat 1.7, coffee 0.85, salt 0.85, clothing 0.51, lamp oil 0.8 a week; cordwood 4 in winter). The boarding house feeds its 8 boarders at section 12's rate (0.67 flour, 0.67 beans, 0.33 meat, 0.13 coffee) plus 0.1 lamp oil and 0.5 cordwood in winter. The saloon 0.83 whiskey (1 per 30 people). Willow Ford's store serves 10 people in 4 households (flour 2.5, beans 1.5, meat 1, coffee 0.5, salt 0.5, clothing 0.3, lamp oil 0.4, cordwood 2 in winter).
- The developer's yard uses 2 lumber and 0.5 nails a week as a placeholder until it builds real projects in M2.
- Opening stock: about 5 days of each year-round good, at least 1 (`TOWN.OPEN_DAYS`; was 10 until D4, so no offers came in the first week), so the first town offers come on day 3 and several on day 4 with nobody playing (the bot's week: 3 offers, all done). Provisional tuning. Saves from before the towns (9 -> 10 step) still get the old 10 days. Low mark 4 days of use; offers for 2 weeks' worth, due in 4 days; one offer per good at a time, at most every 3 days.
- Willow Ford's general store is a canvas tent store (section 21 "a general store tent") beside its depot; C2's 4 cabins stay scenery (homes), standing in for its shanties.
- Goods addressed to the hub warehouse (ordered without a contract) are refused at a business door; to sell them, order for the contract (its button on the telegraph's Contracts tab addresses them to the business).
- Several items for the same business are put down one after another from one tap.
- The door labels show only near the player (9u) or for goods being carried, so the screen is not filled with cards.

## Construction jobs (M1 round B, hand-building track, D10 and D12)

- **Where the code is:** section "construction jobs" (after "cargo"; pure sim): the job store, the rules, the commands' code and `jobDebugScenario`. The comment block at its top describes every job field. Commands `stakeJob`, `jobWork`, `jobCancel` in "commands"; `trackLayPlan` there is the one way track joins the network (the debug instant build and a finished job both use it). `jobActorTick` wraps `carryActorTick` in the sim tick; `carryActorTick` calls `jobAfterCarry` after a put-down. Page side (end of "build tool"): the Stake it button, `jobOnEvent` (messages) and `jobTap` (a tap within 1u of a job's centre line sends `jobWork`). Drawing jobs is builder B2's.
- **Stake it:** without `?debug=1` the build tool's button says Stake it and sends `{ t: 'stakeJob', ...the buildTrack fields }`. The sim checks the plan again (`buildPlan`), refuses amber plans (earthwork comes later), plans with fractions, and a job that shares a track end or the switch's track with another job or comes within 1.6u of one. The job keeps its plan, its own copy of the new track (`geo`, never in `S.track`, so trains cannot use it), stakes every 2.5u, piece counts (a tie every 0.5u, a rail pair per 5u, 4 spikes per tie) and materials (per job, rounded up: tie bundles of 10, rail bundles of 4 rails, spike kegs of 80, and a hardware crate for a switch). With `?debug=1` the button stays Lay track (the parked instant build).
- **Materials on site:** a tie bundle, rail bundle, spike keg or hardware crate put down within 3u of a stake (carrying's put-down, or lying there when the job is staked) moves to `STOCKPILE {jobId}` through `itemMove`; `job.site` remembers where it was put down. A step opens a bundle or keg when it needs pieces: the item is consumed (`itemConsume`, reason `construction`) and its pieces go to `job.loose`. **Part-used bundles:** a waybill may carry `pieces` (fewer than a full bundle); they are made only when a job hands back pieces that do not fill a bundle (a cancel, or the round-up left over when a job finishes), and they open like full ones. So pieces are never lost, and the item conservation check holds unchanged.
- **Work:** `jobWork` walks the player beside the job (the side with more room, 0.9u out, 0.9u ahead of the next piece) and works while the next piece is within reach (1.5u), so the player walks every couple of units. Order, stretch by stretch (5u): that stretch's ties, its rail pair, then its spikes (`jobStepAt`). Times: tie 30, rail pair 60, spike 78 game seconds; the seconds left over from the tick that ends a step go into the next step, so the total is exact. Work stops when a material runs out (`jobStopped` with `missing` and a message), when the work cannot be reached, or when any other command replaces `P.use` / `P.act`. A switch job fits its hardware crate at its first rail pair.
- **Switches beside a standing train (D4):** `bStart` refuses a switch on a track only while a train moves on it ("A train is moving on that track") or stands over the switch's own 5u (its through leg, beside which the turnout's diverging leg runs) plus 1.5u at each end (`BUILD.SW_CLEAR`; "A train is standing where the switch would go"); until D4 any train anywhere on the edge refused it. When the switch splits the edge, `trackLayPlan` moves a train standing beyond the first cut onto the new piece it stands on (`bPlaceSwitch` returns the cuts and pieces), so it keeps its place exactly. Switches under trains stay locked as before (`occupiedSwitches`). So a siding can be staked off the platform track while No. 1 is home.
- **Finish and cancel:** after the last spike the plan is checked again and laid with `trackLayPlan` (same as `buildTrack`: the track index and walk grid caches are cleared, `trackBuilt` fires and the page redraws), unused items and loose pieces go back on the ground beside the job, the job is removed and `jobDone` fires. If the plan no longer fits (a train on the switch's track, say), the job stays finished-but-waiting and a later `jobWork` tries again. `jobCancel` returns every stockpile item to where it was put down and every placed or loose piece as items (whole bundles, plus one part-used one per material for a remainder).
- **Timing:** the handbuild scenario's 20u siding (with a switch) takes 1,884 ticks of work and walking: 251 game minutes, about 3.1 real minutes at 1x (4.2 at D17's slower clock). D12 said "about 5 real minutes", but its own step times give 232 game minutes (2.9 real minutes at the old clock) of work for 40 ties, 4 rail pairs and 160 spikes, so the step times were kept as approved.
- **Testing:** `?debug=1&scenario=handbuild` or `rh.scenario('handbuild')`: a 20u siding staked off the north main line (switch at x 18, facing west), with all 9 materials on site and the player beside its start. Then tap the stakes (or `rh.send({ t: 'jobWork', id: 0 })`). Test hooks in `__railhead.jobs`.
- **Taking materials back (D15, builder G):** a stockpile item can be picked up like any loose item (a tap on it, or `carryPick`): the player walks to where it was put down (`jobSiteSpot`), it leaves the stockpile, the job forgets its spot and says `jobTaken`, and the job's needs go up again. Put down within 3u of the stakes it is on site again. Opened bundles and placed ties, rails and spikes are not items, so they stay put. A tap near a stockpile item now picks it up rather than starting work (carrying's taps come first); tap the stakes away from the pile to work.
- **Not built yet:** fetching materials from the pile during work (the player works from the stockpile wherever it is within the 3u of the stakes). The Cancel button is on the job's sign label (drawing, below).

### Drawing hand-building (builder B2)

- **Where the code is:** `JOB_LOOK`, `jobPieceGeos`, `modelMaul`, `jobStaticKit` and `jobSignSpot` at the end of "models"; `jobView`, `jobGeom`, `jobFill`, `jobAnimate`, `jobPose` and `jobDraw` at the end of "world rendering" (called from `render()` right after `carryDraw`, before `cargoDraw`); the sign label (`jobSignText`, `jobSignHud`, CSS `.jobSign`) in "HUD and panels". Drawing only reads `S.jobs` and `S.player`; it never changes the game (the check compares the hash with the same ticks run on a copy).
- **What shows:** a staked job gets a dirt bed (2 cm up, under the laid ballast's 3 cm), a stake pair every 2.5u (at `job.stakes`, 0.8u either side, just outside the ties) with a red-orange flag at the tip, string between neighbouring stakes along both sides, and a sign board beside its start on the work side. Stockpile items are drawn by the cargo instanced meshes where they were put down (`job.site`; a STOCKPILE branch in `cargoPoses`). Opened bundles: loose ties as a small stack where the bundle stood, loose rails laid in pairs on the ground beside the next stretch to rail (on the work side, 1.3u out), an opened spike keg (a cargo keg drawn through `jobView.extras`) with spike heads on top. Where a bundle stood is remembered by the drawing (from the site it saw the frame before); after a reload the piles go to a fixed spot beside the job's start.
- **Pieces:** ties `0..done.ties-1` at `jobTieAt` (same size, colour and height as the laid track's ties, the switch's diverging leg 4 mm higher like the laid track), rail pairs `0..done.rails-1` as pieces of about 0.5u end to end along the curve, spikes `0..done.spikes-1` as small iron heads, 4 per tie, 2 each side of each rail, staggered. One InstancedMesh each for ties, rail pieces and spikes over all jobs, sharing KIT_MAT (vertex colours; all-white instance colours because r128 shares one KIT_MAT program with the cargo meshes, which use them), starting maximum counts 512, 1024 and 2048 (doubled only if ever exceeded). They are filled again only when a job's done or loose counts, its site or the job list change; the piece in the player's hands is the last instance and only it is moved each frame (a partial upload). When the last spike goes in, the same tick removes the job and lays the track (`trackBuilt` rebuilds the world), so the next frame draws the laid track and no job pieces. The laid track then spaces its ties 0.55u with its own jitter, so the ties shift slightly at that moment (the job's are every 0.5u, the counts B1's rules use).
- **Work poses** (from `act.step` and how far through it, `(total - left) / total`, blended within the tick): a tie rides on the right shoulder (right arm up) while walking to the next spot and for the first 55% of the tie step, then both arms set it down into its place; a rail pair is held up 0.32u with both arms for 55% of the step, then dropped onto the ties; a spike step is 3 overhead blows with a spike maul (an extra mesh on the right arm, shown while spiking), each raised over 60% of the blow and struck fast with a small lean, and the spike sinks a third with each blow. Judgement call: B1's player does not walk to the pile for each tie (it works everything within reach), so the "carry from the stack" is shown as the tie on the shoulder between spots rather than a walk to the stack.
- **The sign label:** a card standing on the sign board: "Job 1: ready to build / Tap the stakes to work", "Job 1 needs / 1 tie bundle and 2 spike kegs" (jobNeeds through B1's `jobNeedsText`), or "Job 1: building (21%)" (done work weighted by the D12 step times), with "Still to bring: ..." if more is needed. While the player works that job the card sits below the sign's foot, so it covers less of the work. It keeps inside the screen sideways and hides when it would overlap a button or panel (it sits below them, z-index 1). Its **Cancel job** button (48 px) needs a second tap within 4 seconds ("Tap again to cancel"), then sends `jobCancel` (one tick at once when paused, like Lay track).
- **Testing:** `check-handbuild-look.js`; screenshots `handbuild-look-staked-*`, `-needs-portrait`, `-mid-*`, `-done-*`, `-cancel-ask-portrait`, `-cancelled-portrait`. Hooks: `__railhead.jobsView` (meshes, counts, `placed` per job), `jobSignText`, `jobSignSpot`, `jobGeom`, `trackTieCount`.

## How the game works (piece 1)

- **Simulation and drawing are kept apart.** The simulation advances in ticks of 8 game seconds, 7.5 ticks per real second at 1x (D17, 2026-11-02; was 10): one game minute is 1.0 real second, a game day 24 real minutes. 2x and 4x are 15 and 30 ticks a second; the debug warp's 32x is 240. `CFG.TICKS_PER_REAL_S` is the only place real time becomes game time: the main loop's tick accumulator, and the drawing-side game-minute figures of the walk (player, crowd and travellers) are worked out from it. Real-time things stay real time: the camera's follow lag and turn, telegram slide-ins, the roof fade, the idle breathing. The simulation itself does not read it, so ticks, results and save hashes are unchanged. Drawing reads the state and blends between the last two ticks.
- **Commands.** Every action is a command (`walkTo`, `walkDir`, `useSwitch`, `useTrain`, `throwSwitch`, `trainRun`, `trainStop`, `trainSendTo`), applied at the start of the next tick. Commands carry whole numbers only (positions in hundredths), so replays match exactly.
- **Track.** The track is a graph of nodes and edges. A position is `{e, s, d}`: edge, distance from node A, and facing (+1 towards B, -1 towards A). `trackWalk` follows the switches as set. `trackScan` looks ahead for the stopping point and speed limits. A train facing a switch set against it stops 0.5u before the switch's block, not at the switch point.
- **Trains.** A train stores only its front position. Each car's position is found by walking back along the track. This is exact because switches under a train are locked.
- **Routes.** A* over directed edges (length plus 20u per diverging switch), trying both directions of travel. Since D20 a route sets and locks no switch: it keeps the switches it needs and the train waits short of one set against it (see "Manual switches").
- **Player.** Tap to walk (`walkTo`, `useSwitch`, `useTrain`) follows a path from the walk grid (section "walk grid and path-finding", design-doc 11): one 0.5u grid over the whole map (the test yard 384 x 384 cells; the real start 1280 x 640 since D15, see "Scale (D15), sim side"). Blocked cells: building footprints grown by the player's radius (0.25u), any cell not flat at its corners, edge midpoints and centre (hills), and the cells under trains, which are stamped in at each query so the grid itself stays static. Costs: grass 1.4, platform and freight dock 1.0. While the player pushes the handcart, a second cached grid (made from the first on first use) also blocks the platform and the freight shed with its dock (`carryNoCartRects`, they have steps), so the path goes round them. The grid is cached per track and rebuilt only when the track changes (`walkGridSig`, and `buildTrack` clears it). A* is deterministic (whole-number costs, fixed neighbour order, ties by cell index), then the path is string-pulled (smoothed along clear straight lines). The path is never saved: it is worked out again every tick from the saved state (a memo keyed by start cell, target cell and train cells skips repeat work), so a moving train simply gives a new path round it, and a save loads to exactly the same walk with no new saved fields. A target inside a building, on a hill or in an area that cannot be reached is moved to the nearest cell that can be; the walk then ends there with the "Something is in the way" message (a train that stays in the way leaves the player standing beside it). A player inside a blocked cell (old save, teleport) first steps out to the nearest open cell. Press and hold (`walkDir`) is direct steering: a move into a train, hill or building tries its x-only and then z-only part, so the player slides along walls. A switch throw takes 0.5 game minutes at the stand, and only starts within 1.5u of it when the walk ended short. A tap on the train walks to within 1.5u of it (camera side preferred when both sides can be walked to straight; a side inside a building or on a hill gives way to the other side, reached round the train's end), then opens its panel.
- **Track and walking (provisional, for the lead):** design-doc 11 says track is impassable except at grade crossings and platform-end crossings. The M0 game lets the player cross track anywhere and throw switches from beside it, so for now track cells are walkable at the grass cost. Making track impassable needs crossings to exist first.
- **Path speed (test machine, software drawing, desktop CPU):** grid build about 3 ms; a typical path search (round the depot) about 0.01 ms; a long one across the yard about 2 ms (6,600 cells); the worst case (a goal that cannot be reached, so the whole connected area of 21,600 cells is searched, as when a train cuts it off) about 5 ms. A sim tick while walking about 0.2 ms. The worst case can repeat every tick only while a train keeps a target cut off.
- **Models.** Built in code from the style test page shapes. Each vehicle or body part is merged into one mesh with vertex colours and one shared material.
- **Build tool (piece 2, M0 instant build).** The Build button (top right, or the B key) turns on build mode: one finger draws track instead of walking; pinch and two-finger pan still work. A drag that starts on a track end (brass ring) continues it; one that starts on a straight places a switch there (5u through leg, radius 10u 30-degree diverging leg, on the side the finger moves to) and carries on from the diverging leg; one that starts on open ground starts a free piece heading the first drag direction (snapped to 15 degrees). The plan follows section 6: straight within 1 degree, else arc then straight (largest radius, 15-degree straights, nearby straight track's heading first, ends snapped to 1.6u parallel offsets); an end within 0.5u of a track end joins it with a straight, arc, arc + straight, straight + arc or S-curve. The ghost is green (valid), amber (ground more than 0.3u off the bed: needs earthwork) or red with the reason in the label. Red covers: radius by class, reverse curves without the straight, closer than 1.6u to other track or crossing it, building clearance (if `buildingFootprints()` exists), off the map, a train on the track, too little straight for a switch, and anything `trackValidate` finds. On release, Lay track / Cancel appear; Lay track sends `{t:'buildTrack', ...}` (whole numbers only), which re-checks and applies or refuses with an event. If the game is paused, Lay track runs one tick so the track appears at once. Splitting an edge keeps its id (it is shortened) and appends the rest.
- **Instant build parked behind `?debug=1` (D10).** The designer cut the M0 instant build; its code stays but only works with `?debug=1`. Without it the Build button and planning (ghost, green/amber/red, label) still work, but after release the button beside Cancel is Stake it (a construction job, see "Construction jobs" below) instead of Lay track. `send()` (the page's command intake, so also `__railhead.send` and `rh.send`) asks `buildIntakeRefusal()` in the build tool section first: without `?debug=1` a `buildTrack` command never reaches the queue and a `buildRefused` event (with `debugOnly: true`, recorded in `buildTool.refused`) shows "Cannot lay that track: instant track laying is switched off ...". Why there: the sim tick must never read page flags, or the same commands could give different results depending on the address; gating at the door where the page hands commands to the sim keeps `simTick`/`simRun` identical with debug on or off (the sim-only checks still build track through `simTick` directly). `buildTool.instant` says which mode the page is in.
- **Test hooks.** `window.__railhead` gives access to the state, `step(n)`, `send(cmd)`, `newState`, `simRun`, `stateHash`, `exportSave`/`importSave` and `toScreen`.

## Debug tools (`?debug=1`)

- Off unless the address has `?debug=1`: no extra page elements, meshes or work per frame.
- A DBG button (bottom right, above Centre) opens a bar: overlay toggles (Blocks, Locks, Routes, Path) and 8x, 16x, 32x, 1x speeds. The train panel covers the bar when open.
- Overlays: blocks coloured per block (occupied red, reserved by a route orange); switch markers (green free, orange locked by a route, red occupied) with a stub along the set leg; each train's remaining route (cyan); the player's target and path (pink: the smoothed walk-grid path for a tap to walk, with a small diamond where the walk really ends if that is short of the target; a straight line for press and hold). One merged mesh each (one draw call each), drawn on top without depth test, rebuilt only when what they show changes (checked every 3 frames).
- Console `window.rh`: `send(cmd)`, `state`, `hash()`, `step(n)`, `setTime(seconds since 05:00 on day 1)`, `warp(0..32)`, `teleport(x, z)`, `scenario('yard' | 'siding' | 'spur' | 'cargo' | 'interchange' | 'handbuild' | 'start' | 'willow')` (interchange: 05:50 on day 1, the player beside the interchange's dead end), `overlay(name, on)`, `drawCalls`, `help()`.
- `?scenario=<name>` (with `?debug=1`) starts from a named scenario. Saving is switched off for the rest of that page visit (`savingOff`, which also stops the quick save), so a scenario never overwrites the player's save.
- Warp above 4x runs up to 640 ticks of backlog, time-boxed to about 8 ms per frame; the rest carries over. It only runs more ticks, so results stay the same.

## Carrying (M1 package 1)

- **Where the code is:** the end of the "player" section (`carryActorTick` and the `carry*` functions, after `playerTick`), the commands `carryPick`, `carryPut` and `carryTool` in "commands", `modelHandTruck`, `modelHandcart` and `TOOL_STACK` in "models", `carryDraw` and `carryItemPose` at the end of "world rendering", the readout (`carryHud`) in "HUD and panels", and `carryTap` in "input". The item store itself is the cargo section's (builder W2).
- **How it runs:** `carryActorTick` replaces the direct `playerTick` call in `simTick`. A pick-up or put-down in progress (`P.act.k` is `pick` or `put`) is counted down there; otherwise `playerTick` walks as before, and afterwards the carry code notices arriving at an item, bay or tool spot (`P.use.k` is `pick`, `put` or `tool`) and starts the 0.2-minute action if within 1.5u. The walking code is untouched, so path-finding can change underneath it.
- **Cordwood on the handcart (D13, builder D6):** up to 6 cords; other long bundles (ties, rails, lumber, poles) are refused ("only cordwood goes on the handcart"), and every long bundle on the hand truck. Cords go from the handcart onto the tender one at a time (`fuelTender` takes the top carried cord, as by hand); pushing the handcart, the walk to the tender goes to the side away from the platform (`ttWalkBeside`). Drawn lying along the handcart's bed, two side by side, up to three layers (`carryItemPose` with the item id).
- **Carried items** are at `{ kind: 'CARRIED', actorId: 'player', carrier, slot }`. `carrier` is `HAND`, `HANDTRUCK` or `HANDCART`, from the tool pushed (`S.player.tool`, null or an index into `S.tools`). Slot 0 fills first; Put down takes the top slot. Taking a tool moves a hand-held item onto it (refused for a long bundle). A loaded tool cannot be left. Tools are taken and left only at their parking spots (`TOOL_PARK`, east of the freight shed, on the side away from the track), saved in `S.tools`.
- **Handcart:** may not go on the platform or the freight shed and its dock (`carryNoCartRects`, from the building layout plus 0.2u); a step that would enter is undone and the walk stops with a message. It can still put items into the bays from the track side of the dock (within reach). Known gap: the walk grid does not know about the handcart, and the platform and dock are its cheapest cells, so a path may lead onto them and the handcart stops at the edge; the walk grid should block those cells while a handcart is pushed (a follow-up for the path-finding owner).
- **Taps:** a tool spot takes or leaves that tool; an item picks it up if there is room; while carrying, a bay or the dock puts the top item in that bay (dock taps go to the nearest bay, as the cargo section draws ground items at ground height); by hand, the ground puts the item there. With a tool, a ground tap just walks; the readout's Put down button (or F) puts an item down beside the player.
- **Drawing:** the pushed tool stands ahead of the player (`CARRY_PUSH`); the hand truck leans back round its axle. Arms are forward 70 degrees carrying by hand, 45 degrees pushing, and dip during an action. A brass progress ring above the head shows any timed action, switch throws included.
- **Feet on the decks (D15, builder F):** `bldStandY(x, z)` (buildings section, after `buildingFootprints`) is the one height lookup for people: the deck top (`BLD_PLATFORM_TOP`) on the flush footprints (the platforms and the freight dock, cached per `BLD_LAYOUT`), the ground elsewhere, eased over `BLD_STEP_R` (0.12u) either side of a deck's edge so walking on or off is a short smooth step. It follows the layout's numbers, so scaling or a new deck height needs no change here. The player, the pushed or parked tool, the item in hand, the tie on the shoulder, the progress ring and travellers (`paxStandY`) all use it; taps for walking and putting down use `deckGroundAt` (the deck's top where the ray meets it inside a deck, else the ground), and the tap marker sits 0.05u above that surface. Drawing only: the sim has no height, so saves and the state hash are unchanged.

## Passengers (M1 package 5, builder C4)

- **Where the code is:** section "passengers" (after "player", before "sim tick"; pure sim): `PAX` (all the numbers), the store `S.pax` (its comment block lists every field), stations (`paxStations`), the queue, ticket sales, boarding, the commands' code and `paxDebugScenario`. `paxTick` is step 8 of `simTick`. Commands `staffWindow { st }` and `leaveWindow` in "commands". Drawing: the end of "people (crowd)" (`paxView`, `paxLook`, `paxDraw`, called from `render()` after `updateCrowd`). Page side: `paxTap` in "input" (a tap within 34 px of a ticket window), and in "HUD and panels" the staffing readout (`#paxDesk`, with Leave) and the window's "Tickets (n)" button (`.paxWin`), in `paxHud`.
- **Stations:** read from data, never hard-coded. With C2's station table (`stationsOf(T)`) each station names its platform and depot, and the depot's `ticket` use spot (`buildingSpots()`) is where the player stands to sell; the window is 0.67u behind it. Without it (the M0 test yard, which has no table) each platform footprint is a station (the first is the hub) with its nearest depot, and the window is on the depot's track-side wall 0.35 of its width from its middle (where the recipe draws it). Cached per world. A station's edges are 0.8u past its platform's ends.
- **Travellers:** whole-number positions (hundredths), saved with their path (the way worked out on the walk grid, round buildings, hills and standing trains, when they set off somewhere). They walk 27 hundredths a tick (2.0 u per game minute). A step into a train waits; after 4 ticks the way is worked out again. States: `queue` (in line), `wait` (ticketed, on the platform), `board` (to a door), `aboard` (in a car, not drawn), `off` and `leave` (walking to the station's edge, then gone). Their own random numbers (`S.pax.rng`), so other systems are untouched.
- **Rates:** hub one traveller every 20 game minutes on average, Willow Ford every 45 (each gap 0.5 to 1.5 times that, in whole ticks), only 06:00 to 21:00, appearing at a random edge of their station and wanting the other station. At most 48 waiting per station (queue, platform and walking to a door; 24 until D20, when nobody gives up any more); while that many wait, no more come (the queue's 48 places fill about 2.5 rows along the back of the platform). Plus 1 to 3 off each Overland visit (when it sets out its cars), appearing beside its engine and walking to the hub's queue, for Willow Ford.
- **Ticket window:** tap the window (or its "Tickets (n)" button, shown while people queue): the player walks to the staff spot; arriving there staffs it (`deskStaffed`), facing the queue. While staffed and the first in line is within 1.5u, a ticket takes 30 game seconds (counted in 8-second ticks with the remainder kept, so the average is exact). Any walk or action, or Leave, stops it. Fare: 4 cents a mile, at least 10 cents; the hub to Willow Ford is 10 miles (160u, section 15) so 40 cents. Each fare is posted with C1's `econPost(S, cents, 'fares', 'Ticket, Hub to Willow Ford', { kind: 'TICKET', id }, ev)`: one ledger entry per ticket, and the cash goes up. Counts per station in `S.pax.sold` and `S.pax.cents`.
- **The queue:** places 0.6u apart along the platform from beside the staff spot, then back in a second row, on open walk-grid cells inside the platform (24 places).
- **Unstaffed stations (D13, builder D6):** a station with `staffed: false` in `STATIONS` (Willow Ford; `paxUnstaffed`) has nobody selling tickets unless the player stands at its window. Travellers still come and wait in line by the window (and still go home after 60 game minutes with no train). When a passenger car of a train that serves them stands at the platform, those in line board too, after the ticketed ones, without a ticket. Stepping off, each pays the fare (`paxFare` of the two stations, 40 cents): one ledger entry through `econPost` (category `fares`, counterparty "Fare paid on arrival, Willow Ford to Hub", ref `{ kind: 'FARE', id }`) and an event `farePaid`. A car that moves off sends them back into the line. The hub is staffed: without a ticket nobody boards there, even while the player is away from the window. Fares paid on arrival are not counted in `S.pax.sold`/`cents` (the ledger has them), so the save shape is unchanged. The test yard has no station table, so its stations count as staffed.
- **Giving up (parked since D20 (3), "people should wait in line as long as needed"):** nobody leaves the ticket line (`PAX.GIVE_UP_S` is 0; the M1 rule, home after 60 game minutes with the count `S.pax.rep[station]` going up, stays in the code switched off, and its toast with it). `S.pax.rep` stays in the save at 0. Willow Ford still lets those in line board without a ticket and pay on arrival.
- **Boarding:** a passenger car (`PAX.SEATS`: combine 12, coach 30) of a train of ours standing still (not running, routed or turning) with its centre beside the platform's track edge. Its doors (`PAX.DOORS`: the combine's one passenger door at its passenger end, 1.25u from its centre; a coach two) have boarding spots 1.3u out on the platform; a door up to 0.5u past the platform's end still serves it, its spot pulled 0.3u inside the end (No. 1 stopped mid-span at Willow Ford leaves the combine's door just past the 16u platform). At each door, one traveller every 6 game seconds (0.1 minutes): those for this station step off first, then ticketed travellers board in id order, never past the seats. A train that moves off sends the walkers back to waiting. The cars a timetable train's engine is running round stand at the platform as part of that train (D7), so travellers step off and board them meanwhile. A timetable train waits at the platform for its travellers (see "Platform work" in the timetable section). Which trains take travellers (`paxTrainServes`): a train on the timetable (C3's `S.tt` services) only while its run is at this station and calls at the traveller's station later on; a train off the timetable (driven by the player) always.
- **Departure board:** one board per station, C3's station card (`ttCardHud`, shown while the player is within 12u of the station's order board), now listing the next 4 departures (`ttBoard` showed 3; changed by C4). For other code, `stationDepartures(S, stationId)` gives the next 4 departures as data `[{ time, train, to, platform, state }]` from C3's services (`S.tt.services`, days and stop times), soonest first within the next 24 game hours; a service that already left today gives tomorrow's run; state is On time, Late or No engine. Platform is always 1.
- **Drawing:** travellers use the crowd's 15 instanced meshes (room for 96 per kit after the walkers), so they add no draw calls when a crowd is shown, and at most the crowd's 30 (with shadows) when it is not. Looks from their id (`crowdDescribe`, never railroad staff or a conductor). On a platform or the dock they stand on its top. In line they face the window; waiting, the track.
- **Saving:** schema 9 (C1 took 6, C2 7, C3 8): the 8 -> 9 step adds an empty `S.pax` (`paxNewStore`); `paxCheckSaveShape` checks it on load. The sample `save-v9.json` holds a traveller in line and one with a ticket.
- **Testing:** `?debug=1&scenario=passengers` or `rh.scenario('passengers')` (the real start): 08:00, 20 in line at the hub's window and 20 ticketed on the platform, the player near the window. Hooks in `__railhead.pax`.

### Provisional choices (passengers, for the lead)
- Rates: hub every 20 game minutes, Willow Ford every 45, from 06:00 to 21:00; 1 to 3 off each Overland visit; at most 24 waiting per station.
- The ticket window is staffed from outside, standing at it on the platform (the depot's inside is not walkable); the queue runs along the platform from there.
- One passenger door on the combine, at its passenger (windowed) end; travellers step off before others board.
- A train off the timetable takes travellers at any platform, and they stay aboard until it stands at their destination's platform; a timetabled train only for its later stops.
- Alighting takes the same 0.1 game minutes per door as boarding.
- Travellers who stepped off walk to the nearer platform end and vanish there (no town yet).
- The departure board is C3's station card (a page card near the order board); it now lists 4 departures. Since D15 there is also a painted board on each depot (see "Departure boards in the world and the ticket booth").

## Riding our own train (D13, builder D5)

- **Where the code is:** the end of section "passengers" (after `paxDebugScenario`, before "sim tick"; pure sim): `RIDE`, `rideCarOf`, `rideAtPlatform`, `rideBoardWhy`, the commands' code (`rideBoardCmd`, `rideStepOffCmd`), `rideOffSpot`, `rideActorTick` and `rideCheckSaveShape`. `rideActorTick` is now step 7 of `simTick` (it calls `ttActorTick` when the player is not riding or boarding). Commands `boardCar { stock }` and `stepOff` in "commands"; the refusal of walking and using commands while aboard is at the top of `applyCommand` (`RIDE.BLOCKED`). One line in `ttCrewAt` (a rider is never crew at the train). Page side: `rideTap` in "input" (also `sendDir` sends no walking while aboard), and in "HUD and panels" after the passengers' part the riding readout `#ride` with its Step off button, the Board button `#rideBoard`, `rideHud` and `rideOnEvent`. Drawing: `render()` hides the player while aboard (`pl.visible`), and `cargoDraw` skips items carried by hand. Hooks in `__railhead.ride`.
- **State:** `S.player.ride` is null, or `{ stock }` (the car's stock id; the car is found every tick, so a split train keeps its rider). While aboard `P.mode` is `'ride'`, and `P.x`, `P.z` are the car's centre every tick, so the camera (which follows the player) follows the car with no camera code of its own.
- **Boarding:** a passenger car (combine or coach) of our own train that stands (`ttStanding`): at a platform the player uses the door's boarding spot on the platform (the same test travellers use, `paxCarsAt`), elsewhere the ground beside the door (`rideDoorSpot`, the open side nearer the player). Tap its door (within 30 px, and nearer than any coupling, which the uncouple tap would otherwise take) or the Board button over the door (shown while the player is within 8u of the door). The player walks to the door's boarding spot on the platform and boards in 0.1 game minutes (one tick). Refused with the reason: pushing a hand truck or handcart ("The hand truck cannot go aboard: leave it at its spot first"), a moving train, a cut without an engine, the Overland's cars. Items carried by hand ride along (not drawn).
- **While aboard:** walking, switches, the train's and cars' doors, carrying, job work, uncoupling, wood, water, the telegraph, business doors, the ticket window and boarding are refused ("You are aboard No. 1: step off first"; uncoupling: "You are riding the train: step off first to uncouple"). Driving commands from the train panel still work (a tap on our train opens its panel without walking), as do Timetable and Highball. A tap anywhere else says how to get down.
- **Step off:** the readout's Step off button (48 px). Allowed only while the train stands (`ttStanding`: speed 0, idle or stopped, not coupling up); refused while it moves ("Wait until the train stands ..."), and if it starts during the 0.1 minutes the player stays aboard. At a platform the player stands on the door's boarding spot; elsewhere beside the door on the first open side clear of every train.
- **Timetable:** nothing in the timetable knows about the rider: departures never wait for the player, and C3's station work, run-around and water are unchanged (the rider cannot uncouple; they step off first). `ttCrewAt` ignores a rider, so riding in with a cord in hand does not hold the train at the spout.
- **Saving:** schema 13 (D1's telegrams took 11, D2's tutorial 12). The 12 -> 13 step sets `player.ride = null`; `rideCheckSaveShape` checks it (a rider's car must exist and the mode must be `ride`). A save made while riding loads aboard and runs on exactly as before (`check-ride.js`).
- **Testing:** `tests/check-ride.js`.

### Provisional choices (riding, for the designer)
- The player does not take a traveller's seat: they ride as railroad staff in the combine's baggage end, so all 12 seats stay for paying travellers (the check boards 14 ticketed travellers with the player aboard: 12 get seats).
