- The player is hidden while aboard (not shown at a window). **Replaced by D14/D15 (2026-11-02):** the player is shown inside the open combine, see "The cutaway into the combine".
- Boarding and stepping off wherever the train stands, not only at a platform: the timetable's departures often left from the water tower's spout (Willow Ford always, after the run-around; the hub when the run had to move to the spout; since D7 Willow Ford's leave from the platform), where the combine is beyond the platform. With boarding at platforms only, the bot could ride only 8 of 26 legs in the week.
- A hand truck or handcart is refused at the door, with the reason (not left on the platform).

## Interchange and the Overland (M1 round B, builder B4)

- **Track:** `interchangeAdd` in "test yard layout" adds one straight edge (`ICH`): a dead end at (2, 20), east of the station and south of the passing siding, running east past the map edge (x = 60) to x = 140, so the Overland can stand off the map. Positions along it are s = x - 2. Speed limit 2.0 u/min (`CFG.LIM.interchange`). It is not joined to No. 1's track yet (coupling and moving set-out cars come in round C), and its edge has `fixed: true`: the build tool's `bStart` refuses track from its end or a switch on it ("That is the Overland's interchange track: it cannot be changed"), `bGeom` never snaps a plan onto its ends, and its ends get no brass rings. Staked jobs (B1) go through the same plan check, so they are refused too. `trainSendTo` onto it is refused with a reason.
- **Trains:** two more entries in `S.trains` (ids stay equal to the index): the Overland (`owner: 'overland'`, id 1) and its set-out cars (`owner: 'interchange'`, id 2, a train with no engine, possibly with no cars). `trainOff(tr)` (away, or no cars) makes `trainPoses` and `trainSegs` return nothing, so a train off the map is not drawn and blocks nothing; standing cars are an ordinary train to the walk grid, so they block walking like any train. Commands that drive a train (`useTrain`, `trainRun`, `trainStop`, `trainSendTo`) are refused for both with a `notYourTrain` event.
- **Visits** (`overlandTick` in "trains", step 3 of `simTick`, ticks only): at 06:00 and 18:00 the Overland appears just beyond the east edge (it comes in at that time and stands at the interchange about 30 game minutes later), pushing its cars ahead, and runs to the dead end or to the standing cars. With cars standing there it couples them (1.0 game minute). If any of them is empty or holds only goods for Outside it takes them: it pulls the whole string off the map, drops those cars (their goods are consumed as `soldOutside`; money comes in round C), and pushes the rest back in. Then it uncouples (0.5 game minutes) every car behind the engine that fits (the set-out cars must end by x = 48; the rest stay aboard, and while any stay aboard it brings no new delivery), and leaves east. Phases in `S.overland.phase`: away, in, couple, out, back, setout, leave. A phase of `off` (debug scenarios and checks only, never in play) stops the visits: B1's handbuild scenario and one part of `check-handbuild.js` use it, because they run past 06:00 and count every item in the game. A visit with no pick-up ends about 07:00; one with a pick-up about an hour later. Events `overlandArriving`, `overlandCoupled`, `overlandPickedUp`, `overlandSetOut`, `overlandLeft` show short messages.
- **Test delivery** (`OVERLAND_DELIVERY`, until import orders exist in round C): every morning a flatcar with 4 tie bundles, 2 rail bundles and a hardware crate (consignee CONSTRUCTION, id 0) and a boxcar with 2 spike kegs (CONSTRUCTION), 2 flour and a coffee for Willow Ford and tinned goods, lamp oil and clothing for the hub; every evening a boxcar with 2 flour (hub), beans, salt and the mail (Willow Ford) and whiskey (hub). Stock ids start at 100 (the Overland's engine) and count up in `S.overland.nextStock`.
- **Loading and unloading by hand** (end of "player", after the carrying code): a car is worked from beside it, at the door of a boxcar (middle of its side), the baggage door of a combine, or the nearest point of a flatcar's side (`carryCarDoor`), within 1.5u, 0.2 game minutes per item. `carryPick { id }` takes an item from a car, `carryPut { stock }` puts the top carried item into one, `useCar { stock }` walks to the door and gives `reachedCar`. All moves go through `itemMove`, so the cargo rules hold (no long bundles in a boxcar, long bundles by hand only, car capacities). Only standing cars that are not the Overland's own can be worked; a pick-up that finishes after the car has moved or been taken is refused.
- **Touch rule:** tap a set-out car: the player walks to its door and the car panel opens, listing what is in it (one button per item, 48 px high). Tap an item to take it; Put in loads the top carried item. The panel closes when the player walks more than 3u away, or the car goes. Tapping the Overland's engine or its cars while it is in shows "That is the Overland's train: its own crew drives it". No. 1 is unchanged: a tap opens its train panel (loading No. 1's own boxcar or combine works in the sim but has no button yet).
- **Drawing:** a flatcar model (`modelFlatcar`); the Overland's engine is the 4-4-0 with a blue-grey cab and wheels (`modelLoco('overland')`). The meshes are rebuilt when any train's cars change (`trainViewSig`). Items on a flatcar are drawn (long bundles along the deck, two across and two along per layer; crates at the deck ends) through the cargo instanced meshes, following the car every frame (`cargoFlatPoses`); a boxcar's load is inside and not drawn.
- **Saving:** schema 5. The 4 -> 5 step (`overlandMigrate`) adds the interchange edge to the saved track (old saves keep all their own track; a player's debug-built track lying where the interchange goes is not checked, it would simply overlap), adds the Overland (away) and empty set-out cars, and starts the visit state fresh. `overlandCheckSaveShape` checks it on load.

## Timetable, coupling, wood and water (M1 package 6, builder C3)

- **Where the code is:** coupling, uncoupling and engine state at the end of the trains part of "trains" (before the Overland); the new section "timetable" (after "trains"): stations, services, runs, line reservations, the run-around, person tasks at a train (uncouple, wood on the tender, water), the order board's data, saving and the debug scenario. The comment block at its top describes every field. `findRoute` (routes) takes an optional set of edges to avoid and returns its path and arrival facing. Page side: the order boards (`ttBuildBoards`, `ttDrawBoards`, end of "world rendering"), the panel's wood and water line, Timetable and Highball buttons and the order board card (`ttPanelUpdate`, `ttCardHud`, `ttOnEvent` in "HUD and panels"), and the train taps (`ttTap` in "input").
- **Vehicles:** `consist` is front to back; a vehicle turned round has `rev: true` (drawn turned by `trainPoses`). An engine's smokebox faces the train's front unless `rev`; `tenderFirst(tr, m)` replaces the old `m < 0` test, so routes and speeds handle an engine at either end. Our engines carry `fuel` and `water` (u of range left). Wood and water burn with the weight hauled: 1u per u run at 6 tons, so No. 1 with its two cars burns 72% and a light engine 50% (provisional). A tender holds 4 cords of 120u (30 miles); the tank 320u (20 miles), as section 7. Out of either, an engine creeps at yard speed.
- **Coupling:** a train moving by Forward or Back (mode `run`), or a route marked `couple` (Send to a standing car, or the run-around's last move), runs up to a standing cut (no engine, not the Overland's) and couples on contact: the cut's cars join in order along the track (turned ones get `rev`), the mover keeps its id, the cut's entry stays with no cars, and the train stands "Coupling up" for 1 game minute (`tr.cw`). Any other move stops 0.5u short of another train (`trainObstacle`); the Overland runs right up to its own set-out cars as before, and stops 0.5u short of anything else. **Uncoupling:** tap the gap between two vehicles: the player walks beside the joint and works 0.5 game minutes with the train standing (`uncouple { tr, at }`); the part with the engine keeps the id; the other becomes the interchange's cut entry (if on the interchange and that entry is empty), an empty `cut` entry, or a new one. A tap on a cut's car opens the car panel like a set-out car.
- **Stations:** `ttStations(S)` resolves station data onto the track. In the real start it reads C2's `stationsOf(T)` through `ttFromWorld` (stopping track = the first of `stops`; other tracks except the headshunt and interchange are alternates, which is how trains meet at Willow Ford's loop; water tower and wood stage = the world's buildings of those kinds near the station; run-around ends from `runAround`). The test yard has no stations of its own, so `TT_TEST_STATIONS` puts "Hub (test)" on the north main line and "Willow Ford (test)" on the south one (with the passing siding and water tower).
- **Stopping tracks across joints (D15, builder G):** a station's stopping track (`trk`, and each of `trks`) is resolved by `ttSpanOf` into pieces: `{ segs: [[edge, s0, s1]], len, ends: [{ e, s, d }], mid }` (plus the old `e, s0, s1`, the longest piece). On one edge it is exactly the old span. When a switch has been built inside it (which splits its edge), the span is the shortest way between its two ends, kept only if it is about as long as the straight line between them (so it never runs onto a diverging leg or round a loop). `ttPlanStop`, `ttAtTrack`, `ttLegU`, `ttWoodNeedU`, the move back to the platform and `ttDebugScenario` all read the pieces, so a switch inside a platform no longer cancels that station's runs. In the real start the platforms and the parallel tracks leave no room for such a switch; in the M0 test yard the hub's stopping track has room.
- **Services and dispatch:** two default services, Willow Ford Mixed morning (hub 08:00, Willow Ford 09:30 to 10:50, hub 13:00) and afternoon (14:00, 15:30 to 16:20, 18:30), timed from runs in the real start. Since D21 a new game in the real start has No. 1 on both (`newState` sets `train: 0` after `ttNewState`; the test yard keeps none; loading a save never comes through `newState`, so saves keep what they have); the player takes her off or puts her on with Timetable in her panel or the office's Timetable tab (`ttAssign`, optionally per service). (Until D21 they started with no engine.) (Until D4: Willow Ford 09:20, hub 12:50; afternoon 13:30, 14:50 to 16:20, 18:20, and no water on the way in; saves from before the timetable still get those, `ttDefaultServices(true)`; later saves keep the services they have.) While a train is on the timetable it drives itself and Forward, Back, Stop and Send to are refused. A run starts 60 game minutes before its first departure (`TT.START_EARLY_S`, 30 until D4) and goes stop to stop. At a stop it waits for its time, the stop's actions (work: no goods for this station aboard and none for its later stops in the station's area, which at the hub takes in the freight shed's bays; water: the train first moves (at most 40u) so a tender stands under the spout, then waits for the player to take water, and a tank short by no more than a 40u move burns counts as full; wood: enough for the route to the next station with a wood stage (from where the train stands, the legs after, twice each run-around, plus 2 miles), or no room for another cord, and once met at a stop it stays met there, so the short move to the spout cannot bring it back) and for wood and water for the next leg plus 2 miles.
- **Water on the way in (D4):** a run's last stop with `water` in its acts (both default services' return to the hub) does not take water at the stop: leaving the stop before it, the train is routed to the station's spout first (`ttPlanSpout` with no distance limit), stops with its tender under it and waits up to 10 game minutes (`TT.IN_WATER_S`) for the player to take water (or, once watered, while the player carries a cord of wood within 12u of a tender with room, so wood from the wood stage beside the tower can go on there too); working at the train (water or wood, or walking up to it from within 6u) restarts the 10 minutes (`ttCrewAt`). Someone standing by with a cord does not hold it beyond that. Then it runs on to the platform. The next departure then needs no move: it leaves from the platform (where passengers can board) with a full tank. If the player missed it, the departing run moves to the spout as before, now with about 35 minutes in hand (60 minutes early, about 25 minutes' crawl). A run starting with its train already under the stop's spout counts as at the stop. Highball (panel, at a stop) waves it off without its actions. Every move asks for a reservation of the blocks on its route; it is granted only if no other train holds or stands on any of them, released block by block as the tail clears (`S.tt.res`). Runs more than 6 game hours late are cancelled. Statuses say what a train waits for ("Waiting: take water at the tower", "Hold for orders: the line is not clear").
- **Platform work (D7):** at every timetabled stop a train with a passenger car does its platform work at the platform. It stands on the stopping track at least 5 game minutes (`TT.DWELL_S`) before it moves on (to the spout or away), and longer while a traveller for this station is still aboard one of its cars beside the platform (they always step off here) or a traveller walks up to board (that part up to 15 minutes after it came in, `TT.PLAT_MAX_S`); status "Waiting: passengers stepping off and boarding" (`ttPlatformHold`, `run.pT` = when it came to stand there). Then it moves to the spout for water if it still needs it. After water at a spout off the stopping track (Willow Ford's, beyond the platform's east end, when the engine came in pushing and needs no run-around) it stops at the platform again on its way out, if the platform is on its way (the departure route runs over the stopping track) and at least 15 minutes (`TT.BACK_S`) are left before the departure (status "Running back to the platform", tried once per stop, `run.back`), so it leaves from the platform and travellers who came meanwhile board too. A hub departure that had to move to the spout (water missed on the way in) still leaves from there: the platform is behind it. During a run-around the cars left standing at the platform count as the train for travellers (`paxCarsAt`). Highball skips all of this. No save change: `pT` and `back` are new optional fields of a run (an old save mid-stop just starts the dwell when loaded).
- **Run-around:** at a stop marked `turn: 'runaround'` with an engine that came in at the head of its train (one that came in pushing is already at the right end): the player uncouples the engine (status "Waiting: uncouple the engine to run around"); the engine runs clear beyond one end (stopping under the spout first if the stop takes water), back through the loop (track other trains stand on is avoided), and forward onto its cars, coupling on contact. It still faces the old way, so the next leg is tender first (60%, 2.4 u/min).
- **Wood and water by hand:** carry a cord of wood (a long bundle, by hand) and tap the engine: the player walks beside the tender and puts it on (0.2 game minutes; the item is consumed as `fuel`). Tap a water tower's spout with an engine's tender under it: the player works the spout 2 game minutes and the tank is full.
- **Order boards:** a post with a board and a lamp at each station: red (hold) or green while a timetable train is leaving on its reservation. Within 12u of a board a small card above it lists the next 3 departures with what each waits for (hidden where it would cover a button). Data: `ttBoard(S, id)`.
- **Saving:** schema 8 for now (C1's economy is 6, C2's world change 7; renumber at merge). The step adds `S.tt` (default services, no engine) and full wood and water to our engines. `ttCheckSaveShape` checks it on load.
- **Testing:** `?debug=1&scenario=timetable` (M0 test yard, 07:20, No. 1 on the timetable at the test hub, 6 cords at its wood stage). Hooks in `__railhead.tt`.
- **Known gaps:** no deadlock alert yet (two single-track stations each holding the other's train wait for ever); (since D13 the player can ride the train, see "Riding our own train"); a player's cars left standing on the interchange beside the Overland's own cut are not known to the Overland.

### Provisional choices (timetable, for the designer)
- Coupling happens on contact at the end of a slow move, by the train crew, with no tap (the task asked for this); uncoupling needs the player at the joint. Section 7 says a person does both in the link-and-pin era.
- Times: uncouple 0.5 and couple 1.0 game minutes (section 7), water 2 game minutes, a cord onto the tender 0.2 (as any item).
- A tender holds 4 cords of 7.5 miles each; burn scales with weight (6 tons = 1u per u). With section 7's ranges taken flat, a round trip to Willow Ford with a run-around did not fit, so this keeps the doc's ranges for a normal train.
- The default services and their times; (D21, designer) No. 1 on both in a new game (was: no engine, the player turned the timetable on); no run-around at the hub, so the afternoon train leaves the hub with the engine pushing.
- (D4, decided by the lead, times provisional) Water on the way in at the hub; the afternoon departure 14:00 (an hour after the morning train is due back, 13:00); runs start 60 minutes early; the train waits up to 10 game minutes at the spout on the way in; the wood need at the hub judged by the route to the next wood stage plus 2 miles (in practice still "fill the tender": the round trip with a run-around needs more than a tender within a cord of full).
- (D7) Platform dwell 5 game minutes: 12 travellers stepping off one door take 1.2 minutes, and a walk from Willow Ford's line to the combine's door about 3; up to 15 minutes while someone is still walking up to board; the stop at the platform on the way out after water needs 15 minutes in hand (the move back from Willow Ford's spout takes about 9 minutes and the dwell 5). Default times unchanged: arrivals stay early (the hub about 10 minutes early in the bot's week, as the train no longer leaves Willow Ford from the spout 20u further east).
- "Station work" is judged from the goods: none for the station left aboard, none waiting in the station's area for a later stop. Highball overrides.
- The order board is a post in the world plus a card shown within 12u (not a board readable when zoomed in).
- A timetable train refuses the player's driving commands until taken off the timetable; a player's Send to is refused through blocks a timetable train has reserved; a player's own moves take no reservation (they are stopped 0.5u short of other trains).
- Cordwood in normal play: ordered by telegraph (C1), and since D13 a new game in the real start begins with 8 cords on the ground at the hub's wood stage (`ttStartingWood`, called by `newState`; two rows of 4 just beyond the stage's open east end, 0.7u clear of it, waybill as the timetable scenario's: CONSTRUCTION 0). They are ordinary items, so conservation counts them; loading a save never comes through `newState`, so old saves get none. The test yard starts with none.

## The real start (M1 package 9, builder C2)

- **Worlds.** A game is in one of two worlds, saved on its track: `S.track.world` is `'start'` (every new game) or `'yard'` (the M0 test yard: debug scenarios, `?world=yard`, and every save from before schema 7). `WORLDS` (section "starting world layout") holds per world: the track builder, the terrain grid, the walk grid, the map bounds (player, items, plans, camera), the flat areas, the hub boundary, the crowd area, and where the player and No. 1 start. `worldOf(T)` reads it from a track. `worldUse(T)` points the plain layout tables (`BLD_LAYOUT`, `BLD_BAYS`, `TOOL_PARK`, `CROWD.AREA`, the bounds `WB` used by `clampX`/`clampZ`) at that world; it is called at every entry point (`newState`, `simTick`, `walkGrid`, the plan check, `buildWorld`, every frame), so the tables always match the state in hand. The test yard is exactly as before: same track, buildings, bays and tool spots, and the same sim hash for the same commands (checked against the old build with the world field and schema number set aside).
- **Map (336u x 128u, 168 x 64 tiles; x -64 to 272, z -80 to 48).** Long and narrow because the line is: the hub (120u x 80u) at the west end, the interchange running off the west edge, and the 160u spur east to Willow Ford. The spur bends north in a gentle S (radius 60u, 30 degrees each way, a 39.85u straight between) so the map stays 128u deep and Willow Ford stands 24u clear of the east edge. Terrain: 6 x 2 chunks of 32 x 32 tiles (the last column 8 tiles wide), each culled on its own; the flat skirt beyond the edge is now one separate mesh (in both worlds), so the chunks keep small bounds. Usually 2 to 4 chunks are drawn. Held flat: 7u round every track sample, the hub boundary plus 2u (and the interchange beside it), and Willow Ford's station area.
- **Hub.** Boundary x -60 to 60, z -78 to 2 (60 x 40 tiles), drawn as a pale dashed line 1 cm up with survey stakes every 16u (in the merged buildings mesh). Track, west to east: the main track (z 0) from a dead end at x -40 (headshunt) through switch 0 (x 5, the interchange switch) and switch 1 (x 40, the platform-track switch) to the spur; the platform track (z -3.41) from switch 1 west past the platform to a dead end at x -36; the interchange (z +3.41, B4's fixed edge, 138u, node A at x -5.73 joined to switch 0's diverging leg, s = 58 about the map edge), outside the boundary. Buildings stand north of the platform track, laid out like the test yard's station moved 8u east (same distances from the track): the 20u platform (x -2 to 18), the depot (ticket window, telegraph and desk spots on the platform), a telegraph pole, the water tower (x 22.6), a wood stage (x 27.5), the freight shed with its dock and 4 bays (x -7.6), 4 lamps, and the hand truck and handcart spots east of the shed. No. 1 starts at the platform, engine east; the player on the platform by the depot. The build tool refuses plans outside the hub boundary ("Outside the hub boundary").
- **Willow Ford** (switch 2 at x 192, z -36): satellite kit with the main track and a run-around loop 3.41u south between switches 2 and 3 (40u apart), a 16u headshunt east of switch 3, a 16u x 2.4u platform north of the main track, the small depot behind it (its office bay on the platform's back edge), a water tower, 2 lamps, 4 cabins (scenery). Unstaffed.
- **STATIONS** (plain data, for C1, C3, C4): per station `id`, `name`, `town` (CARGO_TOWNS id), `staffed`, `depot` (layout id), `platforms`, `tracks` (named straight centre-line spans `{x0, z0, x1, z1}`; turn an end into `{e, s}` with `trackNearest`, since building a switch splits edges), `stops`, and at Willow Ford `runAround`. `buildingSpots()` gives the ticket, telegraph and desk spots of each depot as world points. Switch ids: 0 interchange, 1 platform track, 2 and 3 Willow Ford.
- **Walk grid**: one 0.5u grid over the whole map, 672 x 256 = 172,032 cells (the test yard keeps 240 x 240). Measured on the test machine: built in about 15 to 25 ms, 6.9 MB with the handcart grid; walking from the hub to Willow Ford (about 200u) costs 1.8 to 2 ms a tick on average. A search now stops after 60,000 cells (`WALK.MAXEXP`) and walks towards the nearest cell reached, so a goal cut off by a train cannot cost a whole-map search every tick (the test yard's largest area is 57,600 cells, so it never reaches the cap). Per-area grids were not needed.
- **Camera limits:** the point the camera looks at is kept inside the world's bounds; a pan past the edge is held there.
- **Saving:** schema 7 (6 is C1's economy; 8 is C3's timetable; 9 is C4's passengers; 10 is the towns, C5). The 6 -> 7 step marks an old save's track `world: 'yard'`, so it keeps the test yard's map, ground, buildings, bays and tool spots beside its own track, exactly as before, and its buildings cannot suddenly overlap its track. Only a new game gets the real start.
- **Debug:** `rh.scenario('start')` (a new real game) and `rh.scenario('willow')` (the same, with the player on Willow Ford's platform); the other scenarios are all in the test yard.

### Provisional choices for the designer (not settled by the design doc)
- The map size and shape (336u x 128u), the spur's S-bend north, and Willow Ford at the east end.
- The hub's track plan: the platform track is a stub off switch 1, so the hub has two switches (the interchange switch and the platform switch), not the one switch stand section 15 lists; the interchange joins the main track by its own switch, as asked. From the platform track to the interchange a train reverses on the main line.
- Parallel tracks are 3.41u apart (a switch leg, 2u straight and a radius 8u arc back), not 1.6u: with section 6's switch (radius 10u, 30 degrees) two tracks 1.6u apart cannot be joined. The same holds for Willow Ford's loop.
- Willow Ford has one platform, 16u long (the kit lists two of 24u), a 16u headshunt, and its depot has no 4-bay freight room yet (no bays there).
- No telegraph shanty in the hub (the telegraph is in the depot); a telegraph pole stands beside the depot.
- Speed limits: hub and Willow Ford main tracks 4.0, platform track, loop, headshunts and connections 1.0 (yard).
- Sending No. 1 onto the interchange itself is still refused (B4's rule); it can go to the lead beside it. (Changed by C3, timetable: in the real start Send to onto the interchange is allowed, and Send to a set-out car couples it.)

## M1 acceptance check and the bot (round D, builder D3)

- **Files:** `tests/m1-bot.js` (the bot, `botFactory(R, mem)`; `BOT_SRC` is its source, evaluated in the page) and `tests/check-m1.js` (the check). The bot never edits the state and uses no debug tools: each tick `bot.decide(S)` returns at most one command (the same commands the taps and buttons send), `R.simTick(S, cmds)` runs it, and `bot.observe(S, ev)` reads the messages (a refused command is not tried again for a while). Its memory `bot.mem` is plain data; a save carries a copy of it so a reloaded game goes on exactly as before.
- **Since D13 (builder D6):** a new game has 8 cords at the wood stage, so the bot no longer takes No. 1 off the timetable on day 1 and day 1's afternoon train runs (the check now wants all 14 services). It fetches the handcart for cords when no departure is near and two or more cords can go somewhere (tender or piles), loads up to 6 and puts them on the tender or the piles one by one; while it pushes the handcart it uses it for any cord it can reach; near a departure it carries cords by hand from the piles as before. It orders cordwood when fewer than 12 are at hand or coming, enough for 16. It no longer sells tickets at Willow Ford (unstaffed: travellers pay on arrival). The pressure check now takes No. 1's evening arrival only (with day 1's afternoon train running, the morning's return had stood in for it).
- **How it plays (most important first):** day 1: (until D4: retag the Outside bay for Hub goods; there is a Hub bay by default now) stake a 20u extension of the platform track's dead end (the only place a hub siding can be staked with No. 1 at home, see backlog), tap No. 1 and put it on the timetable, accept the 5 starting offers and order their goods, 12 cords and the siding's materials at the telegraph. Every tick it first does what No. 1's run asks of it at the station it is at (uncouple for the run-around, take water); it waits by the hub's water tower from 40 minutes before a departure, fills the tender from two cord piles (2 cords at the platform's west end, where the tender stands when a run ends, 3 just west of the water tower, near the spout), loads goods for Willow Ford into No. 1's boxcar, and walks to Willow Ford as soon as the hub owes the train nothing; there it unloads and carries the goods to the store's door, and walks back once the train has left. At the hub: unload the set-out cars (hand truck for crates, sacks and kegs, 3 at a time; long bundles by hand), sort into the bays, deliver from the bays to the doors (lumber straight to the door), cords to the tender or the piles (the rest stay in their flatcar), materials to the job. Every morning at the telegraph it accepts offers and orders what open contracts still need, plus cords to keep 8 or more. Otherwise it staffs the ticket window (06:00 to 21:00, while travellers queue), else it works the siding. It walks (never runs): on a phone tap to walk cannot run. Day 1: after the morning run it takes No. 1 off the timetable (no wood for the afternoon) and puts it back once that service is too late to start (20:00).
- **Since D4:** it waters No. 1 at the hub's tower as the train comes in (it walks back from Willow Ford to the tower, and answers the train's call from anywhere at the hub); the departures then leave from the platform, so it keeps selling tickets while a ready train waits for its time, and walks to Willow Ford no earlier than 20 minutes before the departure and not before the morning's telegraph; at Willow Ford it puts the store's goods down beside the car while more are aboard (the train waits for them) and takes them to the door afterwards (Willow Ford's store now offers contracts in the week). The two cord piles stay (the one by the tower serves a run that still moves to the spout).
- **Results before D4 (seed 1866):** 73 items ordered, 73 arrived; 12 through the bays; 22 delivered at doors (2 at Willow Ford's store); contracts 0 to 3 done on day 2, the standing coffee's first week done; 13 runs, worst 10 game minutes late (afternoon hub departures), WF departures on time; the siding finished on day 2 or 3; 82 tickets sold, 153 travellers gave up (94 at Willow Ford); cash $2,400.72 after the week. Pressure (day 3): Overland in 18:31, No. 1 in 18:22, queue 2, 8 cords in the set-out cars, No. 1 needing wood. 79 to 87 draw calls in the screenshot.
- **Results after D4 (seed 1866):** 84 items ordered, 84 arrived; 16 through the bays; 33 delivered at doors (9 at Willow Ford's store); contracts 0 to 3 done, the coffee's first week done, and 3 town offers in the week (5 flour, 6 meat, 7 beans), all done; 13 runs, every departure and arrival within 8 game minutes late (worst +8, a run back that waited at the spout); the siding finished on day 3 at 00:24; 109 tickets sold (82 at the hub, 27 at Willow Ford), 189 travellers gave up (102 at the hub, 87 at Willow Ford); cash $2,457.52. Pressure (day 3): Overland in 18:31, No. 1 in 18:31.
- **Since D13 (riding, builder D5):** the bot rides No. 1 between the stations instead of walking 93 minutes each way: it boards the combine 12 game minutes before a hub departure and 20 before a Willow Ford one (the train leaves Willow Ford from the water tower's spout, about 21u from the ticket window), and steps off once the train stands at the other station (at the hub that is under the spout on the way in, where it then takes water). It walks only after missing a train. Results (seed 1866, after merging D1, D2 and D6): 82 items ordered, 82 arrived; 16 through the bays; 33 delivered at doors (9 at Willow Ford's store); contracts 0 to 3 and 5 to 7 done, the coffee's first week done; all 14 runs, worst +2 game minutes, none cancelled; 22 legs ridden of 28 (2 trains left as the bot reached the door; the rest walked); 87 tickets sold at the hub plus 3 fares paid on arrival from Willow Ford ($1.20); 252 travellers gave up (109 hub, 143 Willow Ford: No. 1 stands at Willow Ford's platform only a moment before moving to the spout, D6's gap); cash $2,452.92; the siding finished day 2 at 23:25. (Before the merge, with D4's rules: 20 of 26 legs ridden, worst +6, 108 tickets, 180 gave up, cash $2,457.12.) The ride takes about as long as the walk (about 85 against 93 minutes), so the week's figures change little: the gain is that the bot no longer leaves early and arrives back with the train.

- **Results after D13 (seed 1866, builder D6):** 82 items ordered, 82 arrived; 16 through the bays; 33 delivered at doors (9 at Willow Ford's store); all contracts done, the coffee's first week done, 3 town offers done; all 14 runs, day 1's afternoon included, every departure and arrival within 3 game minutes late (worst +3; early arrivals at Willow Ford up to 28 minutes); no cancellations; the siding finished on day 2 at 23:21; 66 cords moved on the handcart; 71 tickets sold at the hub, 3 fares paid on arrival from Willow Ford ($1.20); 271 travellers gave up (130 at the hub, 141 at Willow Ford); cash $2,446.52. Pressure (day 1): Overland in 18:31, No. 1 in 18:31.

- **Results after D7 (platform work, seed 1866):** 82 items ordered, 82 arrived; 16 through the bays; 33 delivered at doors (9 at Willow Ford's store); all contracts done, the coffee's first week done, 3 town offers done; all 14 runs, worst lateness 0 (no departure or arrival late; arrivals at Willow Ford 4 minutes early, at the hub 10 to 12 minutes early); no cancellations; the siding finished on day 3 at 00:07; 78 cords moved on the handcart; 27 legs ridden; 173 tickets sold at the hub (was 71: the combine is no longer filled by Willow Ford travellers riding round and round), 36 fares paid on arrival from Willow Ford ($14.40, was 3); 283 travellers gave up (174 at the hub, 109 at Willow Ford, was 130 and 141); cash $2,500.52 (was $2,446.52). Pressure: days 3 to 7, Overland in 18:29 to 18:31, No. 1 in 18:19 (10 minutes apart, the check's limit; day 1 is 11).

- **Since D20 (slower walk 2.0, manual switches, patient line; integration 2026-11-03):** the bot throws the switches (`switchTask`) and plans around the walk: no handcart round of wood with a hub departure under 2 hours off and no more cords loaded onto it under 90 minutes off, no wood to the pile in the last hour before boarding; boarding lead from `CFG.WALK`; at the hub it waits by the combine on the platform (not at the water tower, 20u off) once the train stands there waiting for its time or for the bot's last cord; it finishes the morning telegraph once at the key; at Willow Ford it meets the engine at the water tower, waits by the combine's door during the run-around, and in the last 25 minutes before the departure only takes goods off the train onto the platform (the door the next visit), then boards. With AN's bot alone at walk 2.0 the worst run was 218 game minutes late (it kept missing trains and walking 2 hours between the stations). **Results (seed 1866, all of D20 merged):** 82 items ordered, 82 arrived; 16 through the bays; 33 delivered at doors (9 at Willow Ford's store); all contracts done, the coffee's first week done, 3 town offers done; all 14 runs, worst lateness 0 (Willow Ford arrivals 4 minutes early, hub arrivals 8 early), none cancelled; 28 legs ridden; the siding finished day 3 at 20:37; 215 tickets at the hub, 140 fares paid on arrival ($56.00), nobody gave up (D20 (3)); cash $2,555.94. Pressure evenings: all 7 days, Overland in 18:28 to 18:31, No. 1 in 18:21 (7 to 9 minutes apart), queue 7 to 23, unsorted 1 to 38. Run time about 25 s alone.

### Gaps found by the bot (for the lead and the designer)
1. (Fixed in D13: 8 cords at the hub's wood stage in a new game.) No cordwood before day 1's 18:00 Overland, so day 1's afternoon Willow Ford train could not run.
2. (Riding fixed in D13: the bot now rides No. 1.) On a phone the player cannot run (only Shift with the arrow keys runs) and could not ride the train, so the hub to Willow Ford walk is 93 game minutes each way: about 6 hours of each game day is walking between the stations to water the engine at Willow Ford.
3. (Fixed in D4: water on the way in, departures from the platform, runs 60 minutes early, afternoon at 14:00.) The hub's departures are tight for any player: a run starts 30 minutes early, then crawls from the platform to the water tower at yard speed (about 25 game minutes), leaving about 5 minutes for water and wood. The morning return (12:52) gives 8 minutes before the afternoon run starts. The bot needs its two cord piles to stay within 15 minutes. Consider taking water at the inbound stop, a longer early start, or a faster platform track.
4. Wood: every cord is a long bundle carried by hand from the interchange (about 40u to the water tower), about 5 cords a day.
5. (Fixed in D4: opening stock 5 days, first offers on day 3.) The towns offer no contracts in the first week (opening stock is about 10 days), so after day 2 the only imports are cordwood and the weekly coffee.
6. One player cannot keep two ticket windows: 153 of about 235 travellers gave up in the week.
7. (All four fixed in D4.) Smaller rule issues: switches cannot be staked on the platform track while No. 1 stands on it; the Willow Ford bay is outside the hub's goods area; the wood need can come back after the move to the spout; no default bay for Hub goods.

## Office, telegrams, alerts and Look (M1 package 7, builder D1, D13)

- **Where the code is:** sim: section "telegrams and alerts" (after "passengers", before "tutorial"): `S.tg = { list, next, seen }` (the comment block explains each field), `telegramSend`, `alertsOf`, the event table `TG_FROM_EVENT`, commands `tgRead { id }` (-1 all) and `tgAnswer { id, b }`, `tgTick` (step 9 of `simTick`). The office desk in "economy": `econDeskSpot()` (the hub depot's 'desk' use spot), `econAtDesk`, command `useDesk` (event `reachedDesk`); every economy command now works within 3u of the key or the desk (`econAtKey`). `ttAssign` takes an optional `svc` to take one service off. Page side, at the end of "HUD and panels" (block "Office, telegrams, alerts and Look"): `openOffice`/`officeUpdate`/`officeHtml`, the inbox (`openInbox`, `inboxUpdate`), the telegraph button (`tgBtnHud`), the slide-in (`tgSlideHud`), alerts (`alertsHud`, `alertGo`), Look (`lookPickAt`, `lookCardOf`, `lookUpdate`). The telegraph panel's parts are shared, not copied: `econOrderHtml`, `econContractsHtml`, `econCreditHtml`, `econLatestHtml`, `econActClick` serve both the telegraph panel and the office. Hit tests factored out of the taps so Look reuses them: `itemHitAt` (carryTap), `townHitAt` (townTap), `jobHitAt` (jobTap). Test hooks `__railhead.office`.
- **The office:** tap the depot wall behind the desk spot (`econTelTap` picks the nearer of the telegraph key and the desk); `useDesk` walks there; while the player stands within 1.5u the Office button shows in the bottom row (beside Look). Tabs: Contracts (offers with Accept and Decline, open contracts with progress, due and an order button, the last 20 done or cancelled), Market (buy and sell price and an 8-week line of the buy price per good, - and + and Send order: the telegraph's order list with the same draft), Ledger (money in and out by category for today and this week, totals, loans and next Monday's interest, Borrow and Repay, the latest 6 entries), Trains (each train: where, wood and water, next stop, delay, cars with item counts; the Overland and standing cars), Timetable (each service's stop table with arrive, leave and the work at each stop; Start with No. 1 / Stop this service). It does not pause; it closes with X or beyond 3u of the desk. While paused, its buttons run one tick (as the telegraph's do).
- **Telegrams:** the event table: town offers (`contractOffered`), the Overland coming in with cars for us, a contract done, late or cancelled. Alerts that start: a train waiting past its time (`wait`, one telegram per train, run, day and stop) and an engine under one cord of wood (`wood`); `seen` remembers them until 6 game hours after they end. Each new telegram (found by the inbox's `next`, so ones sent outside a tick show too) slides in under the clock for 4.5 s (below the right-hand buttons where it would meet them; it waits while the office or inbox is open); a tap on it opens the inbox at it. The telegraph button (envelope drawn in SVG, red unread count) opens the inbox: newest first, unread ones with a red edge; a tap opens one (marks it read), its buttons answer it (`tgAnswer`, then the inbox closes); All read. D2's tutorial sends its telegrams through `telegramSend`.
- **Alerts:** `alertsOf(S)` lists active problems: `wait` (a timetable train waiting past its stop's time, or a train on a route stuck), `late` (a contract late), `stock` (a business with none of a good it uses this season), `wood`. The page shows one icon per kind (a word, Wait, Late, Stock or Wood, a coloured edge, and the count), at the right edge below the telegraph button (and below the tutorial chip when it shows), looked at about twice a second. A tap moves the camera to the next one of that kind (`cam.ox/oz`, the existing pan; Centre brings it back) and shows its words. Icons hide where they would meet the debug bar, Centre or a panel, and in portrait while the office or inbox is open.
- **Look:** the Look button (bottom row) arms one inspect; the next tap shows a card for what is there (an item: its whole waybill and where it is; a car: what it holds; a train: status, next stop, delay, wood and water, consist; a business: stock and wants and waiting offers; a job: needs and pieces done; a traveller: from, to, ticket, how long here; a switch: its setting and lock). A tap anywhere closes the card (and does nothing else); Look turns off after one tap.
- **Layout** (portrait / landscape): telegraph button under Build / left of Build; alerts below it / below Build; the tutorial chip at 122 px / 66 px from the top; the office and inbox panels at the bottom middle above the bottom row (at most the screen height less 240 px tall, so they stay below the right-hand buttons) / at the right, 110 px in from the edge, so Centre and the debug button stay clear; the Look card where the train panel goes. Passing messages (the toast) are left out of the overlap check; the toast moves below a slide-in.
- **Saving:** schema 11. The 10 -> 11 step adds an empty inbox. `tgCheckSaveShape` checks it on load.
- **Provisional choices (for the designer):** which events become telegrams (above; Overland visits only when they bring cars for us); "low on wood" means under one cord; one alert icon per kind, not per problem; alerts named with words, not drawn icons; the Office button appears within 1.5u of the desk and does not open by itself; answering a telegram closes the inbox; the slide-in waits while a big panel is open.
- **Draw calls:** none added (all page elements). `check-office.js` measured 42 to 46 at the depot, the same with the office open and closed.

## Tutorial (M1 package 8, builder D2)

- **Where the code is:** section "tutorial" (after "telegrams and alerts", before "sim tick"; pure sim). `S.tut` = `{ done, skipped, step, tg, got, tickets, item }` (the comment block at the top of the section explains each). `tutTick` is step 9b of `simTick` (after `tgTick`). Command `tutorialSkip` (the welcome's Skip tutorial button); events `tutStep { step, key }`, `tutDone { skipped }`, `tutRefused`. `tutCurrent(S)` (the step in hand) and `tutTarget(S)` (where the hint marker stands) are read by the page. Page side, in "HUD and panels": `tutHud` (the Current task chip; since D1's merge a tap opens the inbox at the step's telegram) and `tutDraw` (the marker, called from `render`). Test hooks: `__railhead.tut`.
- **The steps** (`TUT_STEPS`), each a telegram from the Eastern investor (`E. Hartwell, Boston`, signed HARTWELL), kind `tutorial`: 0 welcome (buttons OK and Skip tutorial; the goal is OK), 1 accept a contract at a hub business, 2 order goods for it (an order with a line for a hub contract), 3 throw a switch, 4 sell 3 tickets at the hub's window, 5 unload an item from one of the Overland's set-out cars, 6 put an item in a freight shed bay whose tag matches, 7 deliver an item at a business door, 8 well done (the tutorial ends). Goals are recorded from the events of every tick in any order (`got`, a bit mask); when the step in hand is met, the next step whose goal is not met yet sends its telegram, so a goal met early skips its telegram. A delivery at a door also counts as sorting. The texts are worked out when sent (the order's Overland time, the bay's letter, the business's name).
- **Order of the steps (judgement call):** design-doc 16 lists unload, sort, deliver, tickets, switch, contract, order. In a new game there is no car to unload until something is ordered (the Overland brings only orders), so the tutorial asks for the contract and the order first (before noon they come on day 1's 18:00 Overland), then the switch and the tickets while the goods are on their way, then unload, sort and deliver in the evening. Since D20 (6) the tickets come before the switch (the flow of work: desk, ticket window, work board, switches, the train), see "Helper arrows". The first car and goods are real: the general store's flour contract and the player's own order.
- **New games only:** a new game in the real start begins with the tutorial (its first telegram on the first tick); a test-yard game (`?world=yard`, debug scenarios) and every save from before schema 12 have it done, so no tutorial telegram appears there. The tutorial carries on past day 2 if the player has not finished (the telegrams are never modal).
- **Hint (until D22):** the chip (under the telegraph button in portrait, under Build in landscape; hidden while the office, inbox or a Look card is open) names the current task and pulses 3 times when a new step's telegram comes; a tap opens D1's inbox at that telegram (its buttons while unanswered) and marks it read. The tutorial's own card was dropped at D1's merge, so there is one way to read and answer telegrams. The brass diamond was replaced by the helper arrows (D20 (6), below). **Since D22 (5) the chip is gone**: the Next job line names the step (and pulses 3 times on a new step's telegram); during the welcome a tap on the line opens the inbox at it; later telegrams are read from the telegraph button.
- **Saving:** schema 12. The 11 -> 12 step adds `tut` with `done: true`. `tutCheckSaveShape` checks it on load.
- **Draw calls:** the marker added 1 while a step has a place (73 to 76 in the check's later-step screenshots).

## Helper arrows (D20 (6), builder TA)

- **Where the code is:** page side, "HUD and panels", block "Helper arrows" (after the tutorial chip): `guideTarget` (the place, looked at 4 times a second), `guideTutTarget` (per tutorial step), `hintTarget` (after the tutorial), `guidePath` (the dotted way), `guideDraw` (called from `render`, where `tutDraw` was), `guideHud` (the toggle). Test hooks `__railhead.guide` (`now`, `on`, `setPref`, `hintTarget`, `path`, `ui`). Sim side only `tutTarget` changed (the desk for the contract and order; the switch step's switch) and the step order (below).
- **What shows:** a big orange arrow (low-poly head and shaft, unlit, drawn over everything so a roof or a train never hides it) bobs over the place, about 74 px tall at any zoom, with a paper label above it ("Office desk: take a contract"). Orange dots on the ground (one InstancedMesh, at most 140) mark the way from the player, worked out with the walk grid's A* only when the place changes or the player strays 2.5u off it; dots behind the player are dropped. When the place is off screen, a round orange arrow at the screen edge (inside a frame clear of the HUD) points towards it, with the place's name. Labels and the edge arrow hide while the office, inbox, a Look card or the build tool is open; nothing shows while riding.
- **The steps, in the flow of work:** (since D21 the contract and the order at the work board behind the ticket counter, `tutTarget` k 'workboard'; the telegraph key or desk only in a world without one) welcome (no arrow), contract and order at the telegraph key in the depot window (the office desk instead when the player stands within 3u of it; both open Contracts), sell tickets at the counter inside the booth (the label counts them), the work board (the depot's `workboard` use spot from `buildingSpots()`, added at integration: in the booth strip in front of WB's board, `BLD_SPOTS.depot.workboard`; else the ticket counter; seen once the player stands within 1.6u of it, page memory only) and then the switch (the first not-done switch from `ttSwitchNeeds(S)` when that function exists, else the platform switch, id 1, when free, else the nearest free switch), meet the Overland at the interchange (the Overland itself when on the map) and then its car, the bay with the item's tag, the business's door; then, after the tutorial's last telegram (not after Skip), while No. 1 is not on the timetable (`guideStage` 'tt'): No. 1 herself ("tap her"), and with her panel open the round arrow beside the panel's Timetable button ("Tap Timetable"). Once she is on a run the guided start is over. (A "Your train: No. 1" tag stood over the player's engine while the arrows were on; since D21 the always-on name plates replace it, see "Clarity".) Since D21 No. 1 starts on both runs, so the 'tt' stage only comes when the player took her off. **The step order changed:** tickets now come before the switch (`TUT_STEPS` indices 3 and 4 swapped); no schema change: `tutRealign` (run on every load after the shape check) finds a mid-tutorial save's step again by its telegram's title.
- **After the tutorial:** the Arrows toggle (under the Current task chip in portrait, beside it in landscape, in the same box (hidden in landscape while the train panel is open), so the alerts and the slide-in keep clear of it; at least 48 px) is on during the tutorial and the timetable step and off after them (or after Skip) by default; the player's choice is kept in `localStorage` (`railhead-arrows`), not in the save. On, it points at the most pressing job: a switch the next train needs (`ttSwitchNeeds`), travellers in line at the hub's window with nobody selling, goods in the Overland's set-out cars, goods for a business on the ground in the hub.
- **Draw calls:** at most 2 (the arrow and the dots), only while shown; no shadows. `check-arrows.js` measured +1 on its first-step frame (the dots and the arrow against the diamond's 1 before).
- **Switch needs:** the arrows read `ttSwitchNeeds`' real rows (`sw`, `want` 'normal' | 'reverse', `trainName`, `by`); the label reads "Switch 1: set it for the turn-off for No. 1 by 08:07" (fixed at integration: the branch read `switch` and 'N'/'R', so its switch arrow never found a need).
- **Check:** `tests/check-arrows.js` (the arrow over each step's place; the work board's use spot must exist and stand in the hub's booth; the hint arrow, No. 1 on the timetable, stands over the first switch still to set from the real `ttSwitchNeeds` with its words; the edge arrow's direction off screen, Skip, the toggle, draw calls; screenshots `arrows-first-*`, `arrows-edge-*`, `arrows-later-*`, `arrows-hint-*`).

## Scale (D15), sim side (builder S1)

The world is 1.6 times its M1 size around the people (design-doc D15). `SC = 1.6` (config; `CFG.SCALE` is the same number) and `MILE_U = 16 * SC` (25.6u). Every scaled length in the sim is written as its M1 value times `SC`, so the intent stays visible; `scaleDeep(table)` grows a plain table whose numbers are all places (used for `TT_TEST_STATIONS` and `TOWN_KINDS`). The drawing side (models, `BLD_EXTENTS`, `BLD_LAYOUT`, recipes, camera, zoom, walk speed) is builder S2's.

- **Scaled (x SC):** gauge (`CFG.GAUGE_HALF`), `CFG.GEOM` radii and straights, switch legs and the turn-out (`TURN_OFF` is 5.46u), switch stand offsets, `STOP_GAP`, the build tool's geometry and spacing (`BUILD`, the building clearances 1.2/1.0/0.9 x SC, map-edge margin, snaps), both layouts (`buildTestYard`, `ICH`, `buildStartWorld`, `HUB`, `WFORD`, `START_ICH`, `STATIONS` spans, `TT_TEST_STATIONS`, `ttFromWorld`'s offsets, the tool parking spots, the town lots and town building plans, `TOWN_WILLOW_SPOT`), vehicle lengths (`CONSIST_NO1`, `OVERLAND_LOCO`, `OVERLAND_CARS`), `COUPLER_GAP`, car-relative offsets (`TT.TENDER_AT`, `TT.TOUCH`, `TT.SPOUT_REACH`, passenger `DOORS` and `DOOR_OUT`, `CARRY_DOOR` along the car), train speeds and rates (`LOCO_MAX`, `LIM`, `ACCEL_K`, `BRAKE`: the timetable's times are unchanged), `DIVERGE_PENALTY`, `CORD_U`, `WATER_U`, `FUEL_RESERVE_U` and the fare mile (`PAX.U_PER_MILE = MILE_U`), terrain (tile 3.2u, flat radii, hill height 8.8u, edge fade), hand-building lengths (`JOB.STAKE`, `TIE_GAP`, `RAIL_LEN`, `SITE_R`) and the debug scenarios' places.
- **Not scaled (human-sized):** `REACH` 1.5, the player's radius, carried items and their spacing, the hand truck and handcart, `CARRY_APPROACH`, travellers' walking speed and queue spacing, `TOWN.DOOR_OUT`, `ECON.TEL_REACH`. Walking speed is S2's.
- **Choice: standing beside a car.** A spot beside a vehicle is half its (grown) width plus 0.6u (`BESIDE_TRAIN`, `TT.BESIDE`), and a car's door spot half its width plus 0.25u (`CARRY_DOOR.OUT`), not 1.6 times the old offset, so the car stays within the player's 1.5u reach. `TRAIN_HALF_W` (0.55 x SC + 0.2) is the vehicle footprint the walk grid and `insideTrain` block.
- **Choice: hand-building.** A job of the same shape needs the same pieces (a bundle of 10 ties now lays 8u, a rail is 8u). Each step is quicker by SC (`JOB.TIE_S` 18.75, `RAIL_S` 37.5, `SPIKE_S` 48.75 game seconds), so a job of the same shape takes about the same real time although the player walks further. `JOB.STAND` is 1.05u (just off the wider ties, within reach).
- **The map.** The start map is the M1 map times SC plus open land: 640u x 320u, x -102.4 to 537.6, z -192 to 128 (200 x 100 terrain tiles of 3.2u; walk grid 1280 x 640 cells of 0.5u). Willow Ford's east end is at x 396.8. The test yard is exactly the old one times SC (walk grid 384 x 384). The hills of the start map are new (the noise lattice depends on the map size); the yard's are the old ones grown.
- **Walk grid timing.** Measured in the page (SwiftShader test machine), hub to Willow Ford. Before: grid 172,032 cells built in about 15 ms; the long path search expanded 38,189 cells in 17 ms. After scaling with nothing else changed: grid 819,200 cells built in about 90 ms (the hill sampling, 3.3 million height samples, was most of it), and the long search hit the 60,000-cell cap (53 ms) without reaching Willow Ford, so the walk would have been re-searched every tick. Two changes, cells kept at 0.5u: (1) the grid build samples the ground only in cells touching a terrain tile with mixed corners (heights are never below 0, so a tile with 4 zero corners is flat and one with 4 raised corners is not; checked to give exactly the same grid); the connected-area pass no longer makes an array per cell. Build now about 20 to 45 ms. The plain and the handcart grid share their search arrays (`G.sc`: g, from, seen, dyn and their counters; every search and train stamp starts by bumping a counter), so both grids take about 20 MB instead of 33 MB. A long walk (hub to Willow Ford, `check-world-start.js`) costs about 0.45 ms a tick on average, 15 ms at most. (2) A*'s estimate prices the rest of the way as grass (exact on open grass) instead of as platform: the long search now expands about 6,800 cells in about 10 ms. A path that crosses a platform may be a little longer than the cheapest one; it is still the same every time.
- **Saves (schema 14).** `migrate` 13 -> 14 calls `saveScale14(st, SC)`: track nodes, edge lengths, arc centres and radii, speed limits, switch stands, train positions along edges, speeds, routes and lock points, car lengths, wood and water (u of range), the player's position and target and any ground spot in their action, items on the ground, tool spots, construction jobs (length, geometry, command, stakes, where items were put down) and travellers (position, target, path). Hundredths stay whole numbers (rounded). Older migrate steps that make things from today's constants (the 2 -> 3 tool spots, the 4 -> 5 interchange track and Overland trains, the 7 -> 8 full tender) shrink what they made back to M1 size with `saveUnscale14`, so 13 -> 14 grows them once. Checked: every fixture migrated by the old build to v13 and then by this one equals the same fixture migrated by this one directly; and an old-build run of the same commands, migrated, matches this build's run exactly (train position, speed, wood, track, stands, tool spots). Fixture `tests/fixtures/save-v14.json` (from `tests/make-fixture.js`, whose scripted places now grow with `CFG.SCALE`). The recorded hashes depend on the buildings too (the walk grid), so after the drawing side's scale (S2: `BLD_LAYOUT`, `BLD_EXTENTS`, spots) is merged, run `node tests/make-fixture.js` again (it rewrites `save-v14.json` and the `v14` hashes of the older samples) before `check-save.js`.
- **Checks.** Updated by the integrator after both scale builders merged: positions and sizes are read from the game (`__railhead`) or written as M1 values times `CFG.SCALE`; all of `npm test` passes (2026-11-02).
- **Walking beside a train (integration fixes).** The walk grid's train stamp shuts every 0.5u cell a vehicle touches, so a spot just beside a train (the tender, `TT.BESIDE` 1.48u from the centre line, reach 1.5u) can sit in a shut cell. `walkPlan` then walks the last bit straight from the nearest open cell to the target, when that bit is at most 5 cells (2.5u), clear of the trains (`pathClear`) and on cells the plain grid has open (`walkLineOpen`); a player already on that last bit keeps going straight. Needed at the hub's water tower, where the tower and the tender under its spout leave no open cell between them: without it the tender could not be fuelled there and the afternoon trains were cancelled. A moving train's footprint also reaches ahead of it (`walkTrainAhead`).

## Clarity (D21, builder CL)

The designer: "it isn't clear where to take a contract, which is your train, why the hub says no engine on the timetable". Five parts, all built 2026-11-03:

- **(1) No. 1 on both runs from the start.** `newState` (real start only) puts train 0 on both default services after `ttNewState`. No save change and no migration: a save keeps its services (every fixture loads with its recorded hash). The M1 bot still sends its `ttAssign` on day 1 (now a no-op): the week's figures and hash are unchanged. The tutorial's last telegram (`TUT_STEPS` 'done', worked out when sent) says No. 1 runs both trains, or asks to put her back when she was taken off; the helper arrows' 'tt' stage (No. 1 and her Timetable button) only comes then. `check-determinism.js` takes her off the timetable at tick 0 so its driving commands still count.
- **(2) Contracts at the work board.** Sim: `econWorkBoardSpot()` (the hub depot's 'workboard' use spot, in the booth); `econAtKey` also accepts 3u of it, so `contractAccept`, `contractDecline` and `econOrder` work there (no new command); away from all three, the refusal names the work board. `tutTarget` for the contract and order steps is the work board (k 'workboard'); their telegrams say so. Page: the hub's work board in the world shows a cream painted header "WORK BOARD" and pins up to 2 offers as paper notes at its right (`workBoardOffers`, `boardPaintWork`; `b.offers` beside `b.rows`); its card starts with a Contracts part (the telegraph's `econContractsHtml` and `econActClick`: Accept, Decline, Order n), or, opened from afar by Look, a "Walk to the work board" button. A painted **WORK BOARD** sign stands on the depot roof over the booth door (`BLD_BOOTH.sign`, a third board kind 'sign' in the boards' one mesh and canvas, painted once; its frame and legs are in the fading shell); it is put away (its quad folded) while the player is in that booth. A tap on the sign (or Look) walks the player to the board and opens the card on arrival (`workBoardGo`, `wbUi.pending`). The station card keeps clear of the sign (above it, or below the depot front where there is no room, landscape). Measured: the sign is 28 px tall at zoom 1.5 and is the first thing the camera sees there from the platform.
- **(3) Your train.** Name plates (`plateDraw` in "world rendering"): "No. 1, yours" on a yellow plate over each engine of ours and a plain grey "Overland" over the Overland's, always on, 30 px tall at any zoom, facing the camera, drawn over the scene (no depth test). One mesh (1 draw call) on one 512 x 256 canvas atlas (a 256 x 64 slot per text, painted once). The page's buttons are above the canvas, so a plate never covers one. The stripe: `OUR_STRIPE` (#ffd21f, the plate's yellow) along both sides of our engine's cab and tender, the combine's side walls (part of their cutaway runs) and the boxcar and flatcar sides; `trainStockOurs` (stock ids below the Overland's, any train but the Overland's), so the Overland's cars stay plain even when No. 1 hauls them. TA's "Your train" tag is gone.
- **(4) "Not running: needs an engine"** (`TT_NO_ENGINE`) replaces "No engine" / "No engine on the timetable" / "No engine on it" in `schedStops`, `ttBoard`, `stationDepartures`, the work board's lines and card, and the office's Timetable tab. The painted schedule board puts the long state on its own line (big layout) or across the platform and status columns (full layout). The station card's row for such a run is a 48 px button, and the work board card's row has "Put an engine on it"; both call `ttNeedEngineGo`: with our engine on no run, her panel opens and the helper arrow points at its Timetable button (`guideUi.engineFor`); with her on the other run (that button would take her off), the player walks to the office desk and the office opens at its Timetable tab (`officeUi.pendingTab`, on 'reachedDesk').
- **(5) The Next job line** (`#nextJob`, `nextJobHud`, "HUD and panels" after the Arrows toggle): `guideJob()` is now the one source of truth for the arrows and the line: the tutorial step's place (`guideTutTarget`), the 'tt' stage, or `hintTarget` (extended after its first four: a timetable train of ours waiting on the player for water, wood or the run-around's uncoupling; the Overland due within 3 game hours with goods ordered; contract offers on the work board; an open contract with goods still to order), else the next departure. Each place carries its line words (`next`). A tap shows the arrow to the place even with the arrows off (`guideUi.peek`, until the job changes or 60 s); a second tap hides it; during the welcome it opens the inbox. Settings (the gear beside the Arrows toggle; a bigPanel) turns the line and the arrows on or off; the line's choice is kept in `localStorage` (`railhead-nextjob`), like the arrows'. Portrait: under the clock and speeds (top 104, 290 px wide, clear of the right-hand column), the tutorial box, the alerts and the toast move below it (`body.nextOn`); landscape: from the clock and cash to the telegraph button (placed each frame). Hidden while the Send to banner or the build tool use the top.
- **The first-step arrow in landscape** (integrator's note): when the arrow would reach above the top band (160 px portrait, 66 px landscape: the clock, the Next job line and the buttons there) it stands under the place pointing up, with its label below it (`guideUi.flip`); a label with no clear spot is tried further down beside the way. The edge arrow's frame starts below the Next job line.
- **Draw calls:** the plates +1 (measured in `check-clarity.js`: 40 portrait, 38 landscape at the start; plates on and off differ by 1); the sign and the pinned offers none (in the boards' mesh); the stripe none (in each vehicle's mesh). In `npm test` (2026-11-04): the busiest scene, the busy hub zoomed out with 200 walkers, 84 portrait and 82 landscape; the busy hub at 1.5x 73 and 68; the hub zoomed out to 0.4 by the boards 77 and 75; the M1 pressure evening 81 (was 80). All under 100.
- **The M1 bot (check-m1):** unchanged: the same hash as before D21 (527b1453), all 14 runs, worst lateness 0, cash $2,555.94, 215 tickets, 140 fares on arrival, all contracts done, the siding day 3 20:37, the pressure evenings Overland 18:28 to 18:31 and No. 1 18:21 (7 to 9 minutes apart).
- **Checks changed for D21 (none loosened):** `check-determinism`, `check-world-start`, `check-carry`, `check-timetable` (the fetch) and `check-passengers` (the real line) take No. 1 off the timetable first where they drive her themselves (as a pre-D21 new game); `check-office` expects Stop on both services (then Stop and Start by touch) and Look's "On the timetable"; `check-board` and `check-workboard` count three quads per depot (with the roof sign); `check-workboard`'s state pattern reads the new words; `check-office`'s overlap list also has the Next job line, Settings and the work card; `check-arrows` as above.
- **Provisional choices (for the designer):** the stripe and plate colour (yellow, #ffd21f); the sign on the roof over the door (the wall over the door is hidden by the eave from the default camera) reading "WORK BOARD / contracts and trains, inside"; offers pinned only on the hub's board (Willow Ford's has trains only); "Put an engine on it" opens No. 1's panel when she is on no run, else the office's Timetable tab; the Next job line's order of jobs (the four hint jobs first, as TA had them, then a waiting train, the Overland, offers, orders); the Settings gear sits beside the Arrows toggle.

## Explaining as it happens (D22, builder EX)

The designer, after playing D20 and D21: "Why doesn't the game explain this? Stuff just starts happening. Also the work board is way too close to the schedule board. I can barely see and click it." Built 2026-11-04:

- **(1) First-time cards** (`CARDS`, `cardsTick`, `cardShowNext`, `cardClose`, "HUD and panels" after Settings; `#fcard`). The first time each of these happens in a game, the game pauses (speed 0, as II; the frame loop stops ticking at once) and a card says what happened, why and what to do; **Show me** puts the helper arrow on the place (`guideUi.card`, read by `guideTarget`, even with the arrows off, until the player is within 2.4u or 60 s) and closes the card; **OK** closes it; either way the game runs on at the speed it had (a game already paused stays paused). The cards: `overlandIn` (the Overland set out cars of your goods), `travellersOff` (travellers off the Overland), `ticketLine` (a traveller at the hub's window, nobody selling), `trainIn` (our train in at the hub after a run), `switchWait` (a train of ours stopped at a switch set against it), `switchNeed` (a switch on the work board due within 90 game minutes and not set), `lowTender` (under a cord of wood, or a run waiting for water), `offer` (a contract offer pinned on the work board), `shedGoods` (goods for a business in a freight shed bay), `delivered` (a delivery paid at a door), `saturdayPay` (standing contracts paid), `overlandAway` (the Overland took cars away; goods sold Outside). Event cards read one tick's events; state cards are looked at every 4 ticks. The words are in the card table (2 to 4 sentences each; the check prints them all).
  - **Once per game, in the save:** the ids shown are kept in the save's `ui` part (`save.ui.cards`, beside the camera's turn and zoom), not in `S`: the sim, `stateHash`, the fixtures and their recorded hashes are untouched and there is no schema change. A save without the list (every old one) has shown none. `importSave` and the start read it (`cardsLoad`); a new game starts with none.
  - **Settings:** "First-time cards" On/Off beside the Next job line and the arrows, kept in `localStorage` (`railhead-cards`).
  - **The tutorial:** a card whose moment a tutorial step teaches (`tut`: the step's goal bit: offer = accept, ticketLine = tickets, switchNeed = switch, overlandIn = unload, shedGoods and delivered = deliver) waits while the tutorial runs and is dropped (marked shown) once that goal is met; after Skip it shows the next time. `switchWait` and `travellersOff` wait only while the switch or ticket step is the one in hand (`tutNow`). No card during the welcome, over a big panel, a Look card, the build tool, Send to or a sliding telegram; a waiting state card is looked at again before it shows, a waiting event card shows as it was (it is never stale: it is shown within a second or two, or dropped from the queue only when the game is reloaded).
  - **The checks:** a card pauses and covers the middle of the screen, so `tests/browser.js` opens the older checks with the cards off (the Settings choice) unless `openGame(..., { cards: true })`.
- **(2) Warnings ahead** (`warnRows`, `warnTick`, after the cards). About 30 game minutes before the Overland comes in, and before each of our timetabled trains arrives at a station (hub and Willow Ford), a message (the toast, held 6.5 s) and the Next job line (`hintTarget`, after a switch the train needs) say what it brings, from the work board's rows (`wbRows`): the Overland's cars packed from the orders due, "about 2 travellers" (the mean of 1 to 3), and the cars it takes away; ours: travellers getting off, goods to unload and load, travellers waiting, wood and water. Only for a train running to time (its row On time, not held at an earlier stop past its departure, not waiting on the line): a late train's time is not known. Once per arrival and day (page memory). The line uses shorter words than the message ("No. 1 is due at Willow Ford at 09:30 with 8 off and 4 to board. Meet her at the platform."; the Overland's leaves out the cars it takes away), and a long line goes to 3 lines in 12.5 px in the same 48 px (`#nextJob.long`).
- **(3) Room at the depot.** The public schedule board left the depot's front wall: it stands on two posts with a little roof and a lamp (layout kind `schedboard`, `BLD_SCHED_POST`: 2.4 x 2.2u, its bottom 0.9u above the deck) at the back edge of each platform east of the depot, behind the ticket line (travellers waiting face it): the hub's at x 15.05 (planter1 moved from 16.5 to 17.0 to make room), Willow Ford's 2.6u east of the platform's middle. Its posts are drawn only, not footprints (`buildingFootprints` skips the kind), so the walk grid, the sim and every hash are unchanged; it keeps 0.5u or more from the queue's places and 1.5u from the use spots (checked). The test yard has none (`boardPlaces` finds no posts there). At zoom 1.5 it is about 75 px tall on a phone in its big layout (the old one on the wall was 1.79u, about 61 px). The depot's wall keeps only the **WORK BOARD** roof sign, bigger: 2.42 x 0.8 M1 (was 1.56 x 0.52), over the window and the door, on three legs; about 44 px tall at zoom 1.5 (was 28). The helper arrow for the work board is drawn at the depot's door (in front of it) while the player is outside the booth (`guideDrawAt`; the place and the dots' end stay the board), so it no longer covers the roof sign. The floating train orders card stays; it now keeps clear of the roof sign and the schedule board (`boardScreenRect`; tried above the sign, below the depot front, beside the boards), and the helper arrow's label keeps clear of both boards too.
  - **The Work board button** is in the bottom row while the player is anywhere on the hub's station area (`ttStation('hub').area`: its tracks, platform, depot and freight shed) or in a booth (`wbAtHub`): in a booth it opens the card at once, elsewhere it walks to the hub's work board and opens it there (`workBoardGo`). Two short lines, 66 px wide; where the bottom row (with Office) reaches the Centre button, Centre stands one row up (`centreHud`).
- **(4) One line at the top.** The Current task chip is gone; the Next job line is the only line (the tutorial's step, pulsing 3 times on a new step); the box under the telegraph button keeps the Arrows toggle and the gear. `check-explain` checks no HUD element overlaps (with Office, Work board and Centre showing, and with a 3-line warning).
- **(5) Switch work from day 1:** see "Manual switches" (switch 1 starts straight in a new game; the work board lists it; the `switchNeed` and `switchWait` cards explain it).
- **Checks changed (none loosened):** `check-switches` (a new game's switch 1 straight and its needs not done; the parts written for D20's start set switch 1 for the platform first), `check-clarity` (the line's first job after Skip is now switch 1, then, thrown, the contract as before), `check-arrows` (switch 1 thrown before the ticket-line hint), `check-office` (its played days start with switch 1 set), `check-board` (the board on its posts, clear of the depot front, letters off its frame), `check-workboard` (the Work board button now shows on the platform), `check-tutorial` (the Next job line instead of the chip; a later telegram opened from the telegraph button), `check-arrows` (the work board's arrow is drawn at the door from outside), `check-ride`, `check-cutaway` and `check-passengers`' real line (their timetabled games start with switch 1 set for the platform, as a player who threw it), `check-clarity`'s roof sign tap (a quarter of the way up from its foot: in landscape its middle is now within a finger of the Next job line, which a phone's touch adjustment gives the tap to).
- **Draw calls:** none added by the cards, warnings or button (page elements); the schedule board's posts are in the buildings' mesh and its letters in the boards' mesh. In `npm test` (2026-11-04): the busiest scene, the busy hub zoomed out with 200 walkers, 84 portrait and 82 landscape (as before); the busy hub 73 and 68; the M1 pressure evening 81; `check-explain` 63 at the hub.
- **The M1 bot (check-m1) with switch 1 straight at the start:** passes unchanged in what it checks: all 14 runs, worst lateness 4 game minutes (was 0; day 5's afternoon Willow Ford departure +5 by the clock, counted 4), none cancelled; 211 tickets at the hub (was 215), 139 fares on arrival (was 140); cash $2,553.94 (was $2,555.94); the bot threw 1 switch; the siding finished day 3 20:20 (was 20:37); pressure evenings all 7 days, the Overland in 18:28 to 18:31 and No. 1 18:21 (day 5 18:26), 3 to 9 minutes apart; hash 1298a074 (was 527b1453).

and the prototype: `PROTO`, `PROTO_SW`, `buildProtoWorld`, `WORLDS.proto`; D27: `PROTO_LEVERS`, `PROTO_BRIDGES`, `PROTO_LEVELS`, `PROTO_DECKS`, `TOWN27`, `SHOPS27`, `d27On`), game state, routes, commands, trains, timetable, railroad trains (D24, TR; D25: `RR_WORLDS`, `PROTO_SCHEDULE`, `PROTO_STOCK`, `protoStopPos`, `rrThroughTick`), cargo (items and waybills; D27: `GOODS27`, `SHOP_GOODS27`, the handling places, `itemAudit`), construction jobs, town, the town (D27, W), hired workers (D32, Town: stand-ins), the bigger town (D32, Town stage 3: `TN`, `TN_PEOPLE`, `TN_ROUTINES`, the economy `tnRoll` / `tnTick`, `tnShop`, `tnHome`, `tnLook`; its houses are TOWN27's block "D32 stage 3 (Town)" in "starting world layout", `TN_OFF`), the line (D32, Foundation: `LINE`, the country `lnTile` / `lnScatter` / `lnBorder`, the fronts `lnWork`, the work train `wt*`, away `aw*`, dev mode's commands `dvCmd`, the clock `clockRate`; D33: `D33`, the line's place in the world, `lnBegin`, `lnCrewNow`), the crew (D32, Crew: stand-ins), on the line (D32, Play: stand-ins), economy, freight trains' goods (D24, CG), walk grid and path-finding (D27: levels, `walkDeckY`), player, passengers, tickets on board (D24, PX), prototype passengers (D25, P), tickets on board (D27, P), prototype goods (D25, F), freight handling (D27, F), prototype day (D25, U; D27: the block "Lessons (D27)" at its end, U), telegrams and alerts, tutorial, sim tick, saving, models (procedural, low-poly), renderer and camera, world rendering, terrain, day and night, buildings (D25: `BLD_LAYOUTS.proto`, `PROTO_SHOPS`), people (crowd), build tool, debug, game loop state, HUD and panels (D25: block "Prototype (D25)", U, with the D27 lesson card, jobs and buttons; D32: block "Dev mode (D32)" at its end, Foundation), input (touch first, mouse and keyboard as extras), pixel renderer (D25, R; D32: block "The line's view (D32)" at its end, Foundation; D33: that block also draws the line inside the station's world, `pixLnIn` / `pixWorldIn`, `pixBranch`, `pixStnChunk`, `pixLnAdd`), drawing each frame, main loop, test hooks, start.

## Draw calls

`__railhead.drawCalls` (and `rh.drawCalls`) counts a whole frame: the shadow pass plus the main pass. three.js clears its own counter after the shadow pass, so the page sets `renderer.info.autoReset = false` and calls `renderer.info.reset()` once per frame just before drawing. Earlier figures in these notes (14, 16 to 18) counted the main pass only and were too low. The phone budget is under 100 in a busy scene.

- **After the scale (D15, both sides merged, measured 2026-11-02 in `npm test`; the figures further down are from before it):** the hub at the start zoom 1.5x with No. 1 at the platform and 200 walkers 70 (portrait and landscape), zoomed out to 0.4x 78 and 79, the player on the platform beside No. 1 (2.5x) 70, Willow Ford with No. 1 in 69 and 68 (screenshots `test-output/d15-*.png`); the busy hub (`check-world-start.js`, No. 1 leaving, 2 set-out cars, 200 walkers) 75 portrait and 71 landscape, zoomed out 86 and 84, Willow Ford 71 and 70; M0 station scene with 200 walkers 75 and 76; boot 40 to 42; cargo scenario 51 and 50 (36 and 35 without items); interchange unloading 73; hand-building busy scene 55 and 57; carrying 45 by hand, 47 with the hand truck, 70 with the handcart of cords at the hub; passengers 76 with 40 waiting, 72 boarding; office 40 to 45; town 39 and 40; riding 44 and 57; tutorial 66 and 72; the M1 pressure evening 68. The busiest is the hub zoomed out to 0.4x with the Overland's cars and 200 walkers: 86, under the 100 budget.
- **D20 (builder AN):** the player is one skinned mesh: 2 draw calls (was 10), so every scene with the player is 8 lower. Measured in `npm test` (2026-11-03): the busy hub zoomed out 83 portrait and 81 landscape, the busy hub at 1.5x 72 and 67, the cutaway 72 and 67, carrying by hand 40, pushing a loaded hand truck 42, the handcart of cords 67. The crowd's knees and elbows cost no draw calls (still 15 instanced meshes).
- **All of D20 merged (SW, WB, AN, TA; integration, measured in `npm test` 2026-11-03):** busiest scene the busy hub zoomed out with 200 walkers, 83 portrait and 81 landscape; the busy hub at 1.5x 72 and 67; the M1 pressure evening 80; riding with the cutaway 72; Willow Ford 67 and 65; the hub zoomed out to 0.4 by the boards 76 and 74; in the booth by the work board 37; the schedule board at zoom 1.5 47; a train waiting at a wrong switch with the player by the stand 61 portrait, 58 landscape; the helper arrows +2 at most (39 against 37 on the first step). All under the phone budget of 100.
- Test yard with terrain, buildings and the train, no crowd: 30 to 33 (portrait and landscape).
- Carrying adds: the hand truck and handcart, 1 mesh each (4 draw calls with shadows), the parking spot boards (1, no shadow), and the progress ring (1, only during an action). Measured in `check-carry.js`: 44 carrying by hand and 45 pushing a loaded hand truck (station scene with 10 loose items drawn by the cargo section, no crowd). Carried items cost nothing extra: they share the cargo instanced meshes.
- With 200 walkers (`?crowd=200`): 61 to 63 (full station scene, noon or night, portrait or landscape). The crowd is 15 instanced meshes (3 kits x 5 body parts), 30 draw calls with the shadow pass, whatever the number of walkers.
- Cargo: one InstancedMesh per item shape in use (up to 7, each with a shadow, so 2 calls) plus 1 for all the tags. The cargo scenario (35 items drawn: 19 in bays, 15 on the ground, 1 in hand, 7 shapes) has 44 draw calls, against 29 without items (portrait and landscape). That does not grow with the number of items.
