- Interchange (B4): each vehicle is its own mesh (2 draw calls with its shadow). `check-interchange.js` measured 44 in portrait while the Overland was pulling out (engine, 2 set-out cars, flatcar load drawn, no crowd) and 39 in landscape with the 2 cars standing. With 200 walkers that is about 75 at most during a visit. A long row of set-out cars adds 2 per car; if it grows past about 10 cars the cars should become instanced (follow-up).
- Construction jobs: the fixed parts of all jobs (bed, stakes, string, signs) are 1 merged mesh (2 calls with its shadow); placed and loose ties, rail pieces and spikes 1 InstancedMesh each (no shadows cast, like the laid track: 3 calls); the maul 1 while spiking. So a job in progress adds about 6. Stockpile items use the cargo meshes. Busy hand-building scene (job in progress, materials on site, the train beside it, 10 loose items, noon): 41 portrait, 43 landscape (`check-handbuild-look.js`).
- Town (C5): each town is one KIT_MAT mesh (2 calls with its shadow) and one GLOW_MAT mesh, so up to 3 draw calls per town in view and none when it is out of view (and out of the shadow camera's), as it is culled. `check-town.js` measured 36 (portrait) and 39 (landscape) at the hub with the town in view, the train running and the player walking.
- Office, telegrams, alerts and Look (D1): nothing in the scene; 42 to 46 at the depot with the office open or closed (`check-office.js`).
- Economy: nothing in the scene (the panel and cash readout are page elements). `check-economy.js` measured 36 in portrait and 37 in landscape at the station.
- Timetable (C3): the order boards are 1 merged mesh plus 1 lamp disc per station (about 5 with shadows). `check-timetable.js` measured 36 (portrait, test yard by the hub's board, No. 1 and a cut), 39 (portrait, run-around) and 33 (landscape, real start). A split train adds nothing: each vehicle was already its own mesh.
- The real start (`check-world-start.js`, noon): the busy hub (No. 1 running, the Overland's 2 set-out cars, 200 walkers) 69 portrait and 76 landscape; zoomed out to 0.6x 76; Willow Ford with No. 1 at its platform 66 (both with 200 walkers on the page; they stay at the hub). The new buildings, cabins and the hub boundary are in the existing merged meshes (no extra calls); terrain is 2 to 4 culled chunks plus the skirt.
- Riding (D13): nothing extra in the scene (the readout and Board button are page elements; the player is hidden). `check-ride.js`: 51 riding mid-line (portrait, zoom 2) and 52 in landscape. Since the cutaway (D15) the player is drawn while riding (+10, the same as walking); see "The cutaway into the combine".
- Passengers: none extra with a crowd showing (they share its meshes); without a crowd, up to the crowd's 30. `check-passengers.js`: 67 with 40 travellers waiting (portrait, full station, the train beside it), 62 close up at the queue, 63 boarding; with a crowd of 30 the same count with or without the travellers.
- Plank platforms and station detail (D19): nothing extra (merged into the buildings and depot-shell meshes); about +13,000 triangles a frame (both passes) wherever the stations' meshes are drawn. See "Plank platforms and station detail".
- Debug overlays add 1 each (main pass only). Build mode adds 2 (the ghost and the track-end rings).
- Crowd triangles: about 570 per person per pass (hidden variant shapes such as hats and skirts are folded away in the shader but still sent), about 115,000 per pass for 200 walkers.

## Scale (D15), drawing side

The world grows 1.6 times (`CFG.SCALE`, called `WS` from the models section on; sections before it use `CFG.SCALE`). The sim side (track, layouts, stations, vehicle lengths, speeds, terrain, walk grid, jobs, saves) is a separate change; this part covers what is drawn and the building data.

- **Vehicles:** each model keeps its M1 recipe (rail top `RAIL_TOP_M1` 0.21) and the finished geometry is grown by `WS` about the vehicle centre on the rail line (`growMesh`), so length, width and height all match the x1.6 lengths and gauge. `RAIL_TOP` is 0.336 and `FLAT_DECK` 1.264. Items on a flatcar keep their size; their spots on the deck spread by `WS`.
- **Track drawing:** ballast, rails, ties (size and spacing), buffer stops, the construction-job pieces (ties, rails, spikes, bed, stake offsets) and the build tool's end rings are x1.6. The build ghost's width is the sim side's change; its height (0.235 and 0.245) should become `RAIL_TOP + 0.025` and `+ 0.035` with rails `0.05 * WS` wide after the merge, so it shows above the taller rails. The gauge itself is `CFG.GAUGE_HALF` (sim side). Job stakes, string and sign stay human-sized.
- **Switch stands stay human-sized** (the lever post, its foot bar and the target disc), because a person throws them; they stand where the track data puts `W.stand`, beside the wider track.
- **Buildings:** recipes keep their M1 numbers; `bldPlacer` grows each part by `WS` about the building origin. `b.raw()` places parts in true units (not grown) and `b.on(dy)` lifts grown parts by a true height (onto a platform or dock). `BLD_EXT_M1` holds the M1 plan sizes and `BLD_EXTENTS` (what the walk grid and everything else reads) is them x1.6. Layouts: the yard's is M1's x1.6 (`bldGrow`); the start's offsets from `HUB_PZ` and `WFORD` are x1.6, so with the sim's x1.6 track it is exactly M1 grown about the origin. Bays, the dock rectangle, lamps (`buildingLights`), order boards (a signal for engine crews, so grown), water tower and spout, wood stage, cabins, the hub boundary dashes and stake spacing and Main Street are x1.6. The town's plan data (`TOWN_KINDS`, `TOWN_LOTS`), the tool parking spots and the office window height are the sim side's; the town recipes are drawn through `bldPlacer`, so they grow once, to match.
- **Platform and dock height stay 0.3u** (`BLD_PLATFORM_TOP`, still exported as `__railhead.buildings.platformTop`): it is a step a person takes, and 0.48u would be above a person's knee (legs are 0.34u). The slab grows in plan only; the canopy and its posts grow, the posts reach from the platform top to the canopy. Benches stay human-sized.
- **Use spots:** the depot's ticket, telegraph and desk spots move along the wall with the building (x1.6), and the person stands 0.85u in front of the depot's front (on the platform, more than one 0.5u walk grid cell past the depot's block, within the 1.5u reach). Town door spots stay `TOWN.DOOR_OUT` (0.6u) out from the grown front.
- **Camera:** zoom 0.4x to 4x (`CFG.ZOOM_MIN` 0.4); a new game starts at 1.5x (was 1.25). The shadow box is no longer fixed at 24u: `fitShadowBox` grows it with the ground in view (in 4u steps, never below 24u) and moves the sun back to match, so shadows still cover the whole view at 0.4x (softer there, as the map stays 1024 pixels). The camera's far plane (300) already covers 0.4x; there is no fog.
- **Walking:** since D20 the player walks 2.0 u per game minute with no running, 1.8 pushing a tool; crowd walkers and travellers 1.8 (see "The walk"). The crowd's path clearance from track (`CROWD.BALLAST`) and the test map's crowd area are x1.6.
- **Look things on grown objects:** the Board button over a car door, the town door labels (held inside the top edge of the screen when the grown name board is near it), the ticket window button, the order board card, the joint and water spout tap targets, the tutorial marker (3.6 x 1.6 up) follow the bigger models.
- **Draw calls** (on this branch alone, old track; measured with 200 walkers): the hub with No. 1 at the platform at 1.5x 72 (portrait) and 73 (landscape), at 0.4x 78 and 78; the busy hub (the Overland's cars set out, No. 1 leaving) at 1.5x 75 and 78, at 0.4x 84 and 86. Nothing new is drawn; zoomed out more of the town and the terrain chunks are in view.

## The walk (D20, builder AN; first built 2026-11-02 by builder W)

**Speeds (D20, game units per game minute, which is u a real second at 1x):** the player walks 2.0 (`CFG.WALK`, was 2.8); there is no running at all (`CFG.RUN`, the Shift key and the player's `run` flag are gone; a `walkDir` or `walkTo` sent with `run` set walks; an old save keeps its unused `run` flag, so its load hash is unchanged); pushing the hand truck or handcart 1.8 (`CFG.TOOL_WALK`); crowd walkers 1.8 (`CROWD.WALK`, was 2.5) and travellers 1.8 (`PAX.STEP` 24, was 27). The bot (`tests/m1-bot.js`) never ran, so nothing there changed.

**The player's model** (`modelChunky`, models section) is the same shapes and colours as before, cut at the knees and elbows, and is now ONE skinned mesh (`THREE.SkinnedMesh`, `PLAYER_MAT` with skinning on): each shape is bound whole to one bone (no blending, so it stays flat-shaded low-poly). Bones in `userData`: `hips` (pelvis) > `legL/R` (thighs) > `shinL/R`; `footL/R` (boots, placed where the walk puts the feet); `upper` (torso) > `head`, `armL/R` > `foreL/R`; markers `handL/R` (fists) and `soleL/R`. Rig sizes in `PLAYER_RIG` (thigh 0.16, shin 0.125, ankle 0.055). The maul hangs from `handR` (its handle was tilted the wrong way off its head, fixed). **Draw calls: the player costs 2 (was 10).**

**The walk** (`GAIT`, drawing only; the sim never reads it): a step cycle `g.ph` moves on by the distance drawn over the stride, so planted feet stay put. Each foot is down for half its cycle (`GAIT.F`); in the swing it lifts early (heel up behind, so the knee bends), swings through and lands toes up. `gaitLegs` is two-bone IK from each hip joint (after the pelvis's drop, twist and roll) to its foot target: thigh pitch and tilt, knee bend, and a stretch over 1 only at the ends of a long stride (up to 1.15 at the walk; the legs are 0.34 long and a step is 0.49u). The hips drop at each heel strike (0.05u, twice a stride), twist with the forward leg and roll down on the swing side; the shoulders twist the other way, the torso sways over the standing foot, the arms swing against the legs with the elbows bending more forward, the head stays level. Standing: a slow weight shift (`GAIT.IDLE_HZ`, one knee eases) and breathing. Starts and stops blend in and out (`g.sw`).
- **Cadence:** stride `GAIT.STRIDE_K` x hip height x the square root of speed over 2.0 (shorter for slower people, x 0.78 pushing): walking 0.99u, 2.03 cycles = **4.06 steps a real second** at 1x; pushing 0.73u, 4.9 steps; crowd chunky walkers at 1.8 about 3.9 steps.
- **Poses** (all on `u.pose`, put on the model once a frame by `gaitApplyPlayer` after the walk, carry and work layers in `render`): carrying by hand (`carryDraw`), both hands at the item's sides, shoulders back, and the item is drawn between the hands (`cargoCarriedPose`); pushing, a lean into the handles with the arms nearly straight to the grips (`CARRY_PUSH` now holds the tool farther out and the arm angles per tool); picking up and putting down bend the hips and knees; hand-building (`jobPose`, `WORK_POSE.blow`): the tie on the right shoulder then set down bending at hips and knees, the rail pair held low then dropped, maul blows with wind-up overhead, a fast strike folding at the hips and knees (impact at 0.72 of a blow, when the spike drops) and follow-through, feet set apart; throwing a switch (`throwPose`): reach, a two-handed pull back sitting into the legs, let go.
- **Crowd and travellers** (people section): the same `GAIT` and `gaitLegs`, still 15 instanced meshes (30 draw calls with shadows, unchanged). The knee (legs) and elbow (arms) angles ride in each instance matrix's spare slot (column 0, w); `CROWD_BEGIN` (vertex shader, colour and shadow passes) turns the shapes added after `CrowdPart.bend(y)` about that joint and clears the slot before the matrix is used. Seated travellers in the cutaway sit with the knees bent and feet on the floor by the same IK.
- **Hooks:** `__railhead.gait` has `GAIT`, `PLAYER_RIG`, `gaitPose`, `gaitLegs`, `gaitApplyPlayer`, `throwPose`, `WORK_POSE`, `maul`, and `posePreview` (a function run on the pose each frame, for checks and screenshots only).
- **check-m1 after D20** (the bot, its timetable and the sim unchanged apart from the speeds): it fails. Worst lateness 236 game minutes (was 9), none cancelled; the Overland and No. 1 come in 46 to 145 minutes apart on the pressure evenings (never within 10); 21 tickets (was 129); cash $2,430.44. A one-person station cannot keep this timetable at walk 2.0 (as builder W found at D16): a timetable or bot question for the lead.
- `tests/check-walk.js`: speeds and no running; planted-foot drift (worst 0.0007u), sole on the ground, shin end on the foot, knee bend, stride against distance, cadence 3.5 to 4.2 steps; pushing shorter steps and leaning; ease-out back to the standing pose; crowd planted feet as the shader bends them (worst 0.007u); the poses measured (hands at the handles, maul overhead then on the ground in front with knees bent, hands together and pulled back on a lever, the weight shift, hands at a crate's sides). Screenshots `walk-*` (zoom 1.5) and `anim-{walk,push,carry,maul,throw,idle}-*` (portrait, zoom 2.5, side on, open grass).

## The cutaway into the combine (D15, builder V)

While the player rides, the car they are in opens: its roof, the side wall and the end wall facing the camera are not drawn, showing the inside. Only that car opens (when riding as staff that is No. 1's combine); every other car stays whole, and it closes again when the player steps off. Drawing only: the sim is unchanged.
- **Model:** `modelCombine` (models section) is still one mesh, but built in named runs of vertices (`parts`: `sideP`, `sideM`, `endP`, `endM`, `roof`, `inside`). The walls are thin skins (outside colour, a darker lining inside, windows and the baggage door through both faces), so closed it looks as before. The inside (`COMBINE_IN`, M1 units grown by WS): a plank floor, 12 seats on 6 rows of benches (one seat each side of the aisle, facing the baggage end), a low partition with a gap at x 0.40, and the baggage end beyond it.
- **Opening:** `combineCut(m, side, end)` swaps the mesh's geometry for a cut one that shares the whole one's vertex buffers with an index leaving out the roof, one side and one end (made once per car and opening, at most 4). `cutawayDraw` (drawing each frame, called right after `placeCamera`) picks the side and end facing the camera from the car's heading and the eased camera yaw, so the open side follows the camera's turns and the car round curves.
- **Inside:** the player stands in the baggage end (`COMBINE_IN.STAND`), facing the open side, in the standing pose (breathing) with a little sway that grows with the car's speed as drawn. Travellers aboard that car are drawn seated (`paxDraw` with `cutawaySeat`; `crowdPose` has a seated pose when `w.sit` is set: thighs forward to the floor, hips on the seat), filling the rows from the partition back, in the crowd's instanced meshes. The car's freight (`CAR` items) stands in the baggage end against the end wall, 3 across in layers (`cargoFlatPoses`); items in the player's hands are drawn with them.
- **Cost:** no extra draw calls for the car (one mesh, the cut geometry is the same draw). The combine is 808 triangles (was 208). The player drawn aboard costs what walking costs (5 meshes, 10 calls with shadows; before D15 they were hidden while riding), and freight in the car uses the cargo meshes (2 calls per item shape in use plus the tags). `check-cutaway.js`: riding mid-line with 7 travellers seated and 5 items (5 shapes) 77 in portrait and landscape at zoom 1.5; `check-ride.js` riding (no items) 54 and 67.
- **Hooks:** `__railhead.cutaway()` lists each combine's open side and end and the parts drawn, and for the open car the player, seated travellers and items in the car's own frame.
- `tests/check-cutaway.js`: whole before boarding; riding mid-line the roof, near side and near end are not drawn and the rest is, the player stands in the baggage end on the floor, each traveller aboard sits on their own seat in the passenger end, each item is in the baggage end, other cars whole, under 100 draw calls; the 4 turns of the view move the open side (always the one facing the camera); after stepping off at Willow Ford all whole and the player drawn. Screenshots `test-output/cutaway-riding-{portrait,landscape}.png` (zoom 1.5), `cutaway-close-portrait.png` (3.5), `cutaway-turned-portrait.png`.

## Wide platforms and the see-through canopy (after the D15 playtest, builder P)

The designer asked for much wider platforms and a roof that does not hide the player.
- **Depth:** the hub's and Willow Ford's platforms are `BLD_PLAT_DEEP_M1` = 6 M1 units deep (9.6u, was 3.84u), same lengths. The track-side edge stays 0.9 x WS from the platform track; the platform grows away from the track. Everything on or behind it moved back by `BLD_WIDE_BACK` (5.76u): both depots, the telegraph pole, the 4 lamps on the platforms and the benches (0.65 x WS in from the back edge). The towns hang off their depots (`townAnchor`), so Main Street, the hub's lots and Willow Ford's trading post moved back with them (still inside the hub boundary; `check-world-start.js` checks overlaps, clearances, the boundary and the use spots). The canopy stays along the track-side edge, over the middle 10 x WS.
- **The test yard** keeps its old 2.4 x SC platform (kind `yardplatform`, so old saves and the yard checks are unchanged). `bldIsPlatform(kind)` names the three platform kinds; use it rather than comparing with `'platform'`.
- **Tool parking (real start):** the hand truck and handcart spots moved west (x -4.5 and -3.0 x SC) to stay clear of the deeper platform. An old save's handcart at the old spot sits on the platform's no-cart margin; it can still be taken and pushed away (tried with `save-v14.json`). A saved player inside a moved building walks out (the walk's escape step).
- **Departure card:** the order board did not move (it is by the track), so the ticket window is now about 20u from it; the station card also shows within 6 x WS of the station's ticket spot, standing over that spot (`ttCardHud`).
- **Canopy fade (D15):** the canopy roof is its own mesh (`buildingRoofs`, `bldRoof.mesh`, material a transparent clone of `KIT_MAT`), placed by the recipes with `b.roof(...)`; posts stay in the buildings mesh. `bldRoofFade` (every frame, after the camera) eases its opacity to 0.15 (`ROOF_FADE.LOW`) and turns depth writes off while the player is under it, within 3u of it in plan, or behind it from the camera (the line from feet, middle or head towards the camera passes under it), and back to solid when they walk away. Its shadow stays. The depot roof was left solid: with the default camera the ticket window side faces the camera, so the player there is in front of it.
- **Draw calls:** +2 (the canopy and its shadow) where the hub is in view: hub busy 77 portrait and 72 landscape, zoomed out to 0.4x 88 and 86 (the busiest), Willow Ford 72 and 70; on the hub platform at 1.5x 41 to 42.

## Departure boards in the world and the ticket booth (D15 and 2026-11-03, builder B)

- **Painted boards (D15, "make it both"):** each depot has a dark board with cream letters on its front wall, between the agent's bay and the ticket window (west of the window, so it never hides the player at the counter from the default camera) (`BLD_BOOTH.board`, 2.0u x 1.6u), facing the platform, its letters 1.2 cm in front of its frame. It shows the station's name and its next 4 departures: time, destination, train and a short state (On time, Late, Boarding, No engine, Cancelled), from the same `ttBoard` lines as the station card (`boardRows`, `boardState`). Both stations have one (Willow Ford is unstaffed; its board works the same). Code: "departure boards" at the end of the buildings section (`boardBuild`, `boardPaint`, `boardUpdate`, `boardHitAt`).
- **Drawing:** all boards are ONE mesh (a quad each, no shadow: 1 draw call) on one 512 x 1024 canvas texture (a 512 x 512 region per board). `boardUpdate` (every frame) compares each board's rows with its last ones and repaints only on a change (`__railhead.board.redraws` counts the repaints). Lit like the walls by day; at night a small lamp over the board (glow mesh) and an emissive that follows `GLOW_MAT` keep it readable. Readable at phone size from about zoom 2.5 (screenshots at 3 are clear by day and night); at the start zoom 1.5 only the shape and header show, so a tap on it opens the card.
- **The card stays:** a tap on a board, or Look and then a tap on it, opens that station's card from anywhere (`ttCardPin`, over the board, for 15 s or until the next tap). New state in `ttBoard`: a run cancelled today (`svc.cxl = { day, i, at }`, set by `ttEnd` and "too late to start") shows its stops still to come as Cancelled for 2 game hours (`TT.CXL_SHOW_S`), on the card and the board. Old saves have no `cxl`; nothing else changed in the save.
- **Ticket booth (designer, 2026-11-03: "when selling tickets you should be inside the booth"):** the depot recipe now has an open door (`BLD_BOOTH.door`) and a ticket window that is an opening with a counter. Inside the front wall is a walkable strip (the booth); the rest of the depot is a closed block. `buildingBooths()` gives each depot's strip and doorway as rectangles; `walkBoothCarve` opens their cells in the walk grid (after the buildings are stamped) and `walkInBuilding` lets the walk by direction into them; `bldDecks` includes them so the feet stand at platform height inside. The depot's `ticket` spot is behind the counter; a new `line` spot outside (where the player used to stand) starts the queue (`K.line` in `paxStations`; the first in line is served within `PAX.REACH` of it). Staffed, the player faces the window. All depots share the recipe, so Willow Ford and the test yard have the booth too. A player saved inside the depot walks out (the walk's escape step).
- **See-through shell:** the depot's roof, front wall, the strip's end walls, the sign and the board's frame are their own mesh (`bldShell.mesh`, b.shell in the recipe, all depots together), faded like the canopy (`bldShellFade`) while the player is in a booth or its doorway. The board's letters stay solid.
- **Moved with the relayout:** the front is now, west to east, the agent's bay (x -1.45 M1), the board, the ticket window (x 0.62 to 1.17) and the door (x 1.32 to 1.97), so the walk in and out of the booth is short (with the door at the far west end the M1 bot missed rides and No. 1 ran up to 47 minutes late). The telegraph spot follows the bay; the desk spot is in front of the door. `tests/m1-bot.js` waits at `K.line` (the old staff spot); `check-world-start.js` checks the ticket spot is in the booth and the other spots on the platform; `check-passengers.js` gives the walk in 20 more ticks (100) before counting sales; `check-paths.js` counts the booth as open ground. The window's and the desk's tap points are close on screen now, so `onTap` gives the tap to the nearer of the two (`paxTapBest`, `econTapDist`).
- **Draw calls:** +1 for the boards and +2 for the shells (with their shadow) where a depot is in view. `check-board.js`: hub board at zoom 1.5 54 to 55 by day, 44 to 45 at night; zoom 3 48 to 51; Willow Ford 38; in the booth with 40 travellers 72 to 74; the hub zoomed out to 0.4 with 200 walkers 83 to 84. `check-world-start.js` (busiest): the hub busy 80 portrait and 75 landscape, zoomed out to 0.4x 91 and 89 (was 88 and 86), Willow Ford 75 and 73.
- **Checks:** `tests/check-board.js` (boards at both depots, rows match the card, repainted only on change, a departure and a cancellation, tap and Look open the card, the walk into the booth by the door, selling there, the shell fade, escape from inside; screenshots `test-output/board-*.png` and `booth-*.png`).

## The work board and the public schedule board (D20 (2) and (3), builder WB)

- **Where the code is:** the data (plain, no three.js) just before "departure boards" at the end of the buildings section: `schedStops`/`schedRows` (public board), `wbRows` (work board) with `wbGoodsAt`, `wbEngineNeeds`, `wbSwitches`, `ttShortName`; drawing in "departure boards" (`boardPlaces`, `boardBuild`, `boardPaintSched`, `boardPaintWork`, `boardUpdate`, `boardHitOf`, `workBoardHitAt`, `wbBoothStation`); the card in "HUD and panels" after the Office button (`wbUi`, `openWorkCard`, `workCardUpdate`, `workBtnHud`). Nothing in the save changed; nothing here writes the state.
- **Public schedule board** (the old departure board on the depot's front wall, now 1.22 x 1.12 M1, grown downwards: the eave hides the wall above 1.42 from the default camera): every stop of a service at the station still to come today, in time order, 4 rows: train (short name, "Mixed AM"), from, to, arrival, departure, platform (always 1), and On time / Late (more than a minute past) / Cancelled (for 2 hours after, as before) / No engine. Arrivals at the end of a run are listed too. Two layouts by zoom (repainted once when it crosses 2.5, `BOARD.FULL_ZOOM`): below 2.5 (the start zoom 1.5) the BIG one, 3 trains in big letters, "08:00 / to Willow Ford / On time" with no title; from 2.5 the columns (train, from, to on one line, arr, dep, pl, status under them). At 1.5 on a phone the big layout's time and destination read clearly (screenshots `schedule-hub-*-z1.5-*.png`); the columns need 2.5 or more. The train orders card now stands above the roof when it is shown for the ticket window or a tapped board, so it no longer covers the board (it hides while the work board's card is open, as for the other big panels).
- **Work board** on the booth's inside wall (the closed block's face) behind the counter, facing the window (`BLD_BOOTH.work`, 1.45 x 0.87 M1, frame in the kit mesh, not the fading shell). Rows (`wbRows`): each of our timetable trains coming in or leaving within 6 game hours (any standing there now), in time order, plus the Overland at the hub when it is due within 6 hours (what was ordered for that visit, and the cars it takes away from the interchange). A row: kind (Freight, Passengers, Mixed from the cars), train and engine, cars by kind, arrival and departure, goods to unload here per tag (aboard now unless an earlier stop of the run takes them off, plus goods waiting at the run's earlier stops for here), goods to load here per tag (in the station area for a later stop), travellers getting off (aboard for here, or waiting at an earlier stop) and waiting to board (with tickets), wood (cords to put on: at the stop the dispatcher's own `ttWoodNeedU`, before it an estimate from the legs still to run) and water, and the switches. In the world it shows the next 3 trains in short ("08:00 Mixed AM, 2 cars / Off 2 On 3 Pax 0/10 Wood 3 Water Sw 1/2"). The card (a bigPanel, scrolls, X and buttons 48 px) opens by a tap on the board from inside the booth (from the platform the tap does not reach it: it is behind the front wall), by the **Work board** button in the bottom row (shown only in a booth), or by Look and a tap on it from anywhere; opened in the booth, it closes when the player walks out. Willow Ford's depot has one too.
- **Switches:** `wbSwitches` reads SW's `ttSwitchNeeds(S)` rows (`{ svc, sw, want ('normal' | 'reverse'), byTime (game time, as S.time), by ('HH:MM'), station, stationName, spring, done, passed, ... }`, see "Manual switches"); each need goes with the row of its service nearest its deadline; the card lists "Switch 3: to the turn-off (red), by 08:42", ticked when done and marked late past the deadline. If the function were missing or threw, the card would say "Switches: (coming)". `check-workboard.js` checks the real function first (every need it lists for the hub's services is on the board with the same switch, setting, deadline and tick, `want` is 'normal' or 'reverse', and the card's lines read "Switch n: straight (green) / to the turn-off (red), by hh:mm"), then a stub of the same row shape for the placing per row and the ticks (checked at integration, 2026-11-03).
- **Ticket line (D20 (3)):** see "Passengers": nobody gives up; the cap per station is 48 (was 24), so 16 game hours with nobody selling fill the hub's line to 48 and then no more come (they are not lost from the line, they never arrive).
- **Draw calls:** none added: the work boards are 2 more quads in the boards' one mesh (its canvas is now 1024 x 1024: schedule boards on the left, work boards on the right) and their frames are in the kit mesh. `check-workboard.js`: in the booth by the work board 44 to 45, the schedule board at zoom 1.5 50 to 54 by day and 40 to 44 at night, zoom 3 46 to 48; `check-board.js` as before (the hub zoomed out to 0.4 with 200 walkers 83 to 84).
- **Checks:** `tests/check-workboard.js` (both work boards behind the counters, the rows against the sim state: trains in the next 6 hours, cars, goods off and on, travellers, wood; the Overland; the card by real touch only from the booth, Look, the button, buttons 48 px, scrolling; switches from the real `ttSwitchNeeds`, then a stub of its row shape; the world board's 3 lines and no repaint without a change; the schedule columns at both stations; 10 in line keep waiting 12 game hours; screenshots `workboard-*.png` and `schedule-*.png`). `check-passengers.js` now checks that nobody leaves the line in 16 hours and the cap holds (instead of the 60-minute give-up).

## Plank crossings (D15, builder X)

Track is walked across only at plank crossings (design-doc 11 "Pathfinding"; D15 replaces D12's "cross anywhere").
- **Walk grid:** `xingTrackMask(T)` shuts every cell whose centre is within `XING.BAND` (1.15u) of a track centre line (ties end at 0.96u; the spots beside a train, 1.48u out, stay open); each pad opens its cells (shrunk by half a cell, so walkers keep to the planks). Kept as `G.trk` (also on the handcart grid). `walkGridSig` includes the number of crossings. Trains are stamped as before, so a train standing on a crossing shuts it: the path goes round by another crossing, or the walk ends beside the train and goes on when it has gone. Press and hold slides along the track like a wall; a hold aimed across the track becomes a tap-style walk there (`holdRouteTo`, page side).
- **Data:** `T.crossings` = pads `{ x, z, hx, hz }` (middle on the centre line and the track's heading), 3.7u long (10 planks). Fixed ones from `XING_FIXED` (segments across the tracks, a pad where each meets a track): the hub platform's west end (x -5.8, between it and the freight shed) and east end (x 31), both over the platform track and the main; west of the freight shed (x -19.2) over all three hub tracks (to the Overland's cars and the land south); Willow Ford's platform ends (x 322.4 and 355.8) over the main and the loop; the test yard x -6.4 (main, siding) and x 6.4 (main, siding, interchange). Schema 15: migrate 14 -> 15 lays the fixed pads on an old save's own track.
- **Either side:** `walkPrefer`/`walkPreferG` pick the spot on the side the player can walk to (same connected area), nearer first. Used for a switch (`switchSpots`: the stand's spot or `XING.FAR` 1.6u across the trunk; a throw counts within reach of either), couplings and the tender (`ttWalkBeside`), water (`ttWaterSpots`: the tower's spot or its mirror across the track; `P.use.far`), a car's door (`carryCarDoor`) and `trainApproach` (the camera's side wins only when not across track).
- **Building one:** in build mode a tap (no drag) on a track shows a crossing ghost, "Plank crossing: needs 1 tie bundle" and Stake it (`stakeCrossing { e, s }`, `xingPlan` checks: on one edge, 2.6u clear of other track and switch legs, not on a crossing or job). The job (`job.pad`) has 10 tie steps (planks, set from 1.4u beside the track on the reachable side, reach 1.85u), needs 1 tie bundle; done, its pad joins `T.crossings` (`crossingBuilt` event rebuilds the world mesh). Track plans keep 2.6u clear of crossings.
- **Crowd:** the crowd grid marks track as never-walk (code 2) except the pads. Travellers use the walk grid, so they obey it already.
- **Drawn:** planks are boxes in the merged posts mesh of `buildWorld` (0 extra draw calls), `XING.LIFT` above the ties, rail heads 3.6 cm proud. A job's planks are its tie pieces at the same height.
- **Speed (SwiftShader):** grid build 20 to 26 ms in `npm test` (was about 20; up to 40 on a loaded machine); hub to Willow Ford search 4,209 cells before and after, 1.2 to 3.4 ms (the way runs north of the line); round by a crossing 0.5 to 0.7 ms (2,276 cells); a walk hub to Willow Ford 0.37 ms a tick on average.
- **Draw calls:** unchanged (planks are in an existing mesh): hub busy 77 portrait, 72 landscape; zoomed out 88 and 86; Willow Ford 72 and 70; the M1 pressure evening 85.
- **M1 bot week (check-m1):** every run on time (worst 2 game minutes late, none cancelled), siding finished day 3 00:23; pressure evenings: Overland and No. 1 0 to 11 minutes apart (check passes). A goal behind a long detour (south of the spur out in the country, no crossing near) can hit the 60,000-cell cap: stake a crossing there.
- **Check:** `tests/check-crossings.js`.

## Plank platforms and station detail (D19, builder D)

The designer asked for "more visual detail at the station" and "the platform should be made of planks".
- **Planks:** `bldDeck` (buildings section) draws a deck as boards running lengthwise (`BLD_PLANK`: 0.22u wide, 2.5 cm gaps, 2.0u to 4.4u long with staggered joints, six weathered tones picked by a seeded random number from the building's position, `bldSeedOf`). The boards are flat quads (2 triangles each) at exactly `BLD_PLATFORM_TOP`; the dark under-structure's top is 3.5 cm lower and shows in the gaps (no surfaces on each other). A heavier edge timber (sill, 0.34u) runs along the track side and the ends, a thinner one along the back, all with their tops at the deck top; short posts stand under the track-side and end sills in front of the set-back under-structure. Used by `bldPlatform` (all three platform kinds) and the freight dock in front of the doors (`freightshed`; under the shed it stays a plain base). The hub platform has 374 boards, Willow Ford's 306, the dock 15 (`__railhead.buildings.planks`).
- **End steps:** `bldSteps` puts two plank treads (at 2/3 and 1/3 of the deck height, 0.3u each) at both ends of every platform, on the track-side half only (`bldStepWidth`: 2.4u from the track-side edge back), where the crossings will meet them; the middle of each end, where travellers leave (`paxStations` exits), keeps its short step. The feet follow a straight slope over them (`bldStepList`, `bldStepY`, used by `bldStandY` with the decks), from the deck top to the ground over 0.7u. They are drawing only: the walk grid is unchanged (the steps are open ground).
- **Depot detail** (`bldDepotDetail`, both depots and the test yard's): battens on the back and end walls, corner boards, sills and heads on the back and end windows, brackets under the eaves, a stovepipe with a collar and rain cap (was a box chimney), the station's name on both gables in a 3 x 5 block font (`BLD_FONT`, `bldSignText`: HUB, WILLOW FORD), a notice board with four paper bills on the east end wall by the door, and a wood box with logs under the east end window (the stove's wall). Everything on the shell (roof, front wall, the booth strip's end walls: brackets, chimney, name boards, the notice board, the strip's battens and front corner boards) goes to `b.shell`, so it fades with the booth's see-through shell.
- **Props** (`BLD_RECIPES`, human-sized, true units): at the hub a freight pile (crates, barrels, sacks, milk cans) and a four-wheeled baggage wagon (iron-tyred wheels, a trunk and a valise) on the platform's back west of the depot, a platform scale by the depot's west corner, a drinking-water barrel with a dipper by the east corner, two planters either side of the bench, and a hitching rail with a horse trough on the ground behind the platform's east part (town side); at Willow Ford a small crate pile and a water barrel. They are layout entries at the end of `BLD_LAYOUTS.start` (so the older buildings keep their colour variation), each with a footprint marked `prop: true` that blocks walking like a lamp post (overlaps with decks are allowed in `check-world-start.js` as for lamps). The test yard has none.
- **M1 bot:** the props' small detours made the bot miss day 5's 08:00 train (it set off for the combine a fixed 12 minutes ahead while No. 1 stood at the water tower, about 30u from the booth). `boardLead` in `tests/m1-bot.js` now sets off earlier when the combine is far (walk time plus a quarter, plus 3 minutes; at least 12). `check-m1.js` is unchanged: worst lateness 9 minutes, 23 rides in the week.
- **Cost:** no new meshes or materials, so no extra draw calls (busy hub 80 portrait and 75 landscape, zoomed out to 0.4x 91 and 89, as before). Triangles: the buildings' kit mesh is 8,952 (both stations; it is one mesh, so all of it is drawn when any part is in view), the shell 1,546. A frame (shadow and main pass together) at the hub at zoom 1.5 went from about 44,000 to 57,000 triangles; the busiest, the hub zoomed out to 0.4x with 200 walkers, from about 262,000 to 277,000 (about 138,000 a pass, under D5's 150,000; the crowd is most of it).
- **Merged with the plank crossings (2026-11-03):** the end steps sit on the track-side half of each platform end, so they come down beside the crossings' approach: at the hub's east end (x 31) the steps' foot is on the pad's line; at the west end (x -5.8) beside its corner; at Willow Ford about 1.3u to 1.5u short of each pad. No prop is near a crossing (they are all on the platforms' back edges or behind them). The telegraph pole moved from 1.1u east of the depot's east gable to 1.6u east and 0.5u back (`tpole` in `BLD_LAYOUTS.start`), as its crossarm covered the B of the HUB name board from the default camera. Full `npm test`: 36 checks pass; check-m1 worst lateness 2 minutes, none cancelled, 21 rides, siding done day 3 00:17; busiest draw calls 91 (hub zoomed out to 0.4x, portrait).

## Technical choices to know about

- **Crowd speed is checked by update time, not frame rate:** the test machine draws in software (SwiftShader, no graphics chip), so its frame times say nothing about a phone. `check-people.js` limits the crowd's own update to 4 ms a frame and applies the 50 ms frame limit only on a real graphics chip.
- **Hills and walking:** the player, trains and the tap-to-walk ground plane are at height 0. The sim keeps the player on flat ground with `terrainFlatAt(track, x, z)` (pure math, no three.js; it works out the heights for the given track if needed). Hills are blocked cells in the walk grid, so tap to walk goes round them; press and hold slides along their foot. Crowd walkers never target or cross hills or buildings.
- **Crowd:** `?crowd=N` or `__railhead.setCrowd(n)` (default 0). Walkers are render-side only, with their own seeded random numbers, not in the save. One shader colours each person (skin, hair, top, lower, hat, vest packed per instance) and hides the shapes they lack (bit mask). Women: chunky or lanky with a skirt and a bonnet or bun; children: chunky at 0.7.

- **Shadow map:** 1024 pixels, not the design doc's 2048, to save phone memory and time.
- **Test yard geometry:** the switch's 30-degree diverging leg plus the 8u yard minimum radius and the 2u straight between reverse curves give a siding about 3.4u from the main line, wider than the 1.6u minimum.
- **Speeds:** the main loop is 4.0 u/min, diverging legs 1.5 u/min, and the siding and spur 1.0 u/min (yard speed).
- **Tender load:** the tender carries cordwood, not coal, because engines start on wood (section 2).
- **Switch points:** the point rails do not animate yet. The target disc shows the setting.
- **Send to:** a moving train only takes a route in the direction it is already going; otherwise "Stop the train first." Routes are compared by estimated time (tender-first runs at 60%).
- **Changing direction:** Forward or Back the other way while moving sets mode `turn`: the train brakes to a stop, then runs the new way. A braking train (modes `stop`, `turn`) still looks ahead and stops 0.5u short of a switch set against it or a dead end.
- **Route locks:** freed on arrival, Stop or a new command. A switch under a train stays locked because it is occupied (`occupiedSwitches`), not by the route.
- **Saving:** IndexedDB (gzip) a couple of seconds after a world-changing action and every 10 game minutes; on page hide or close also an uncompressed quick save in localStorage (`railhead-quicksave`). On start the save with the higher tick wins. A damaged save is kept as `broken-<time>` (IndexedDB) or `railhead-broken-<time>` (localStorage) and the game starts fresh with a console warning.
- **Terrain (piece 3a):** per world (see "The real start"); the test yard 64 x 64 tiles of 2u (3.2u since D15), 2 x 2 chunks of 32 x 32 tiles, one shared vertex-colour material (up to 4 draw calls), plus a flat skirt beyond the map edge (its own mesh since M1 package 9, 1 more draw call). Hills come from seeded value noise (up to about 5.5u). The ground is held at height 0 within 7u of every track corner sample and over x -25..25, z -20..24, and fades to flat at the map edge. The heights are worked out again from the current track each time `buildTerrain` runs. `terrainHeight(x, z)` uses the same triangles as the mesh. The terrain receives shadows but does not cast them.
- **Day and night (piece 3a):** `daylightAt(time)` gives the light for a game time (no three.js); `applyDaylight` applies it. Colours and sun elevations follow design-doc 17. The intensities are tuned rather than copied from the doc (provisional, for the designer's review): noon stays at the approved sun 0.8 and sky 0.5, while dawn, dusk and night are lifted so the scene stays readable on a phone. Sunrise and sunset: spring and autumn 06:00 to 19:00, summer 05:00 to 21:00, winter 07:00 to 17:00. Because the game starts at 05:00, the first minutes are in dawn twilight. `GLOW_MAT` glows from just before sunset until just after sunrise.

## Team mode (from 2026-11-01)

The designer asked for a team: the lead session hands out work to up to 6 workers (5 for M1), and an integrator merges it. Workers are helpers inside the lead session, never separate sessions (designer, 2026-11-01). Questions for the designer always go on an interactive survey page whose answers are saved in the page database (see CLAUDE.md).
The designer chose to review once, when all of M0 is done, instead of after every piece. The plans are recorded in `plans/m0-team-plan.md`.
Each builder owns one section of `index.html` (terrain, day and night, buildings, people (crowd), build tool, debug) and works on its own branch.
Each builder adds its own check file in `tests/` and one line to `tests/run-all.js`.
The integrator merges the branches into `ccr-59cb4c1f-rnscv9`, runs `npm test`, and fixes anything broken.

## Manual switches (D20, builder SW)

No train ever throws a switch: the player does, at the stand (`useSwitch`, 0.5 game minutes, from either side). Code: "track graph" (`switchSpring`, `swName`, `swWant`, the comment above `trackIndex`), "game state" (`switchApproach`, `routeWrongAt`, `switchLockReason`), "routes" (`findRoute` needs carry `face` and `stopAt`; `routeNew`, `routeThrows`), "trains" (`trainTick`, `springPush`, `springTick`), "timetable" (`ttSwitchNeeds`, `ttSwitchPlan`, `ttRaPlan`, `swStation`).
- **Routes** (Send to and every timetable move, `ttGrant`) no longer set or lock switches. `tr.route.needs` = `[{ sw, state, face, stopAt }]` (stopAt: route distance to the switch's block: its node when facing, the far end of the leg when trailing). `trainTick` stops the train `CFG.STOP_GAP` short of the first one set the other way (`trackScan` already stopped a trailing move at a switch set against it, also for Forward and Back); it stands with `tr.swWait` = the switch (status "Waiting: the switch ahead is set against us", no number, as check-trains wants), event `trainSwitchWait`, alert `k: 'switch'` ("No. 1 is waiting at switch 2 (Willow Ford)") and one telegram. Thrown right, it goes on. Nothing is damaged: a trailing move never enters the switch.
- **Locks:** a switch under a train, or one a moving train would run over before it could stop (`switchApproach`: braking distance plus the stop gap, along the switches as set), cannot be thrown. A switch the train is stopping short of anyway can always be thrown right. `lockedBy` stays in the data, always null.
- **Send to** uses the switches as set: the `routeSet` event lists `throws` (`[{ sw, want }]`), the toast says which to set, and the train runs as far as it can and waits short of the first one. A route needing a switch another train stands on is refused as before.
- **Needs data for the work board:** `ttSwitchNeeds(S, day?)` (hooks: `__railhead.tt.ttSwitchNeeds`) -> `[{ svc, service, train, trainName, stop, move ('leg' | 'runaround'), station, stationName, sw, want ('normal' | 'reverse'), byTime (as S.time), by ('HH:MM'), spring, set, passed, done }]` in time order, for every timetabled service running that day. Routes are planned once per track and service from the stations' stopping tracks (`ttSwitchPlan`); byTime on a leg = departure + the leg's time in proportion to the distance to the switch; in a run-around the three moves at 1/4, 1/2, 3/4 of the dwell (estimates). `done` = passed, or set with no earlier move still wanting it the other way. A new timetabled day: 08:07 switch 1 reverse; 09:18 switch 2 normal (spring); 10:10 switch 3 reverse (spring); 10:30 switch 2 normal (spring); 12:34 switch 1 reverse; then the same for the afternoon (14:07, 15:18, 15:55, 16:07, 18:04).
- **Saving:** schema 16. 15 -> 16 frees every lock (`lockedBy` null, `route.locks` []), and in the real start makes switches 2 and 3 spring switches (levers N and R; a free one springs to its lever on the first tick). New fields: `W.spring`, `W.lever`, `tr.route.needs`, `tr.swWait` (only while waiting). An old route under way has no needs and runs on over the switches as they lie.
- **The M1 bot** (`switchTask`): throws the switch No. 1 waits at, or the earliest switch in `ttSwitchNeeds` not set for a move due within 3 hours, at the station it is at.
- **Check:** `tests/check-switches.js`.

### Provisional choices (switches, for the designer)
- **Willow Ford's run-around switches are spring switches** (it is unstaffed): switch 2 (west) lies for the main track, switch 3 (east) for the loop. Arrivals run to the platform, the engine runs out to the headshunt pushing 3 over (it springs back), back into the loop, out through 2 pushing it over, and onto its cars on the main. The player still uncouples the engine there, as before, and can throw either lever by hand (for a meet on the loop, for example). Without them the player would walk 64u between the two switches twice per run-around (about 25 to 30 game minutes each way).
- **A new game's platform switch (1) lies straight, for the main track (D22 (7), since 2026-11-04)**, so the player throws it to the platform track (reverse) before No. 1's first departure at 08:00 (needed by 08:07); every timetabled move goes through switch 1 reverse, so once thrown it stays right until the player sets it straight again (to the main track or the interchange). Set in `newState` (real start only), not in `buildStartWorld`/`startSwitchStates`, so saves keep their switches. (Until D22 it lay for the platform track and nothing needed a throw.) The interchange switch (0) lies for the main track.
- **The Overland needs no switch**: it comes in from the map's west edge on the interchange's own track and stops short of switch 0's connection, so nobody throws one for it today. **D22 (8) (designer): a switch in the Overland's way would be thrown by the player**, not by its crew (the D20 builder's provisional choice was its own crew); there is none in the layout now, so nothing is built for it. A later layout that puts one there must list it on the work board for the Overland's row and warn of it.
- A spring switch pushed over by a train counts as the train's wheels, not a throw; its lever never moves by itself.
- Send to goes as far as it can and the toast names the switches to set; the panel's status does not name switch numbers (the piece 1 review), but the alert, the telegram and the toast do (`switch N`, the id shown by Look).

## Station rework (D24)

The designer's D24 (design-doc section 0): the hub is the whole game; the railroad's trains are seen coming in from either end of a through line, stop short of a wrong switch until the player throws it, run into the platform (passengers) or a cargo track beside the cargo dock and the freight shed (freight); tickets are checked on board car by car; the player sets the switches and waves each train off. The Overland, the interchange, the ticket counter as the way to sell tickets, No. 1's Willow Ford runs and Willow Ford are parked. The technical plan, the layout's figures, the schedule, the four builders' split and their interfaces: **`plans/rework-tech.md`**.

**The foundation (built 2026-11-06, this branch):**
- **The hub world** (`'hub'`, every new game: `PAGE_WORLD` defaults to it; `?world=start` and `?world=yard` open the old worlds). `HUBW` (section "starting world layout") holds its numbers: map x -176 to 144, z -112 to 48 (320u x 160u, walk grid 640 x 320); the through line z = 0 from x -240 to 208 (64u past each edge; edge 0 is its west end); switches 0 (x -52, a spring switch lying for the cargo track), 1 (-12), 2 (-5.6), 3 (31.2), all diverging south to a bypass loop per section, all stands on the north side; the cargo section (switch 0 to 1, the dock span x -49.6 to -14.4) and the platform section (switch 2 to 3, the platform x -3.2 to 28.8). `buildHubWorld` builds it; `WORLDS.hub` gives its terrain, walk grid, bounds, crowd area and `parked` flags; `STATIONS.hub` is one station (spans `platform`, `cargo`, `bypassP`, `bypassC`); `XING_FIXED.hub` is empty (nothing the player uses is south of the line). `HUB_SW` names the switches; `hubLineAt(T, x, dirX)` gives a track position on the line; `rrEntry(T, end)` where a train appears off the map.
- **Buildings** (`BLD_LAYOUTS.hub`): the real start's platform, depot, pole, lamps, props and schedule board moved 5.46u south with the track (every distance from the track as before); the freight shed by the cargo track; a new open **cargo dock** (kind `cargodock`, a flush plank deck, no handcart); the **cargo yard** (trodden earth, `HUBW.YARD`); water tower and wood stage east of switch 3 (scenery: the railroad's crews fuel their trains, provisional); tool spots behind the shed; Main Street and the lots behind the depot as before (Willow Ford's store left out); **M2's town area** staked out north of it (`HUBW.TOWN`), and the **town exits** (`HUBW.TOWN_EXITS`) where travellers who step off will walk.
- **New game** (`newState(seed, 'hub')`): No. 1 kept but parked away off the map (`away`, `parked`); the Overland and its cut away and empty; two empty railroad train slots (ids 3 and 4, owner `rr`); no timetable services; the tutorial done (until UI's rewrite); switch 2 set for the bypass, against the first passenger train (D24: "you need to throw the switch").
- **Parked flags** (`worldParked(T, what)`, hub world only): `overland` (`overlandTick` returns at once), `interchange` (no track; the save check skips it), `willow` (no Willow Ford; its store's lot is left out and offers nothing: `townOffers` skips a business with no lot), `no1` (no services), `counter` (`staffWindow` refused; `paxTick` hands over to `pxTick`).
- **Stubs with the agreed names** (each in its own section, owned by one builder): "railroad trains (D24)" (`RR`, `RR_SCHEDULE`, `rrNewStore`, `rrNewTrains`, `rrTick` (wired into `simTick` after `overlandTick`), `rrPlanDay`, `rrRunOf`, `rrSwitchNeeds`, `rrBoardRows`, `rrWaveOffCmd`, `rrCheckSaveShape`, `rrDebugScenario`), "tickets on board (D24)" (`PX`, `pxTick`, `pxCheckCmd`, `pxOnArrive`, `pxOnDepart`, `pxReady`), "freight trains' goods (D24)" (`cgConsist`, `cgLoad`, `cgReady`, `cgOnDepart`, `cgTick` (wired after `econTick`)), and in "HUD and panels" the block "Station rework" (`rrHud` every frame, `rrOnEvent` for the events starting rr, px, cg). Commands `rrWaveOff` and `checkTickets` are in the command switch. Hooks `__railhead.rr`.
- **Debug:** `rh.scenario('hub')` (a new hub game) and `rh.scenario('hubtrains')` (06:50, a passenger train standing at the platform and a freight train at the cargo track, each with its run entry), so PX, CG and UI can work before TR's trains run.
- **Saving: schema 17.** 16 -> 17 gives an old save `S.rr` switched off and nothing else; it keeps its world. `S.rr` = `{ on, day, runs, next, nextStock, tk, cg }` (the plan has the run's fields). Builders add optional fields inside their own part without a schema bump. Sample `save-v17.json`; the older samples record their v17 load hashes.
- **Plates:** the railroad's trains get a plain plate with their name (not "yours"); No. 1 and the Overland as before.
- **Check:** `tests/check-hubworld.js` (in `run-all.js`): a new game's shape; the track validates; the line runs straight past both map edges and a train walks it end to end with every switch straight; the four switches' places, sides and the spring; **walking distances on the walk grid against the plan's limits** (measured: the inner throat 4.9u, each switch to its platform or dock end 3.4 to 3.6u, along the platform 37.8u, along the cargo track 41.8u); every place the player uses reachable from the start without crossing the track (stands, use spots, bays from the dock, tools, doors, town exits, yard, docks, the M2 area); section 6 clearances, flush decks, no overlaps; the parked systems stay off over a game day; the old worlds still open; screenshots and draw calls.
- **Draw calls** (`check-hubworld.js`): at the start 27 portrait, 26 landscape; zoomed out to 0.4 37 and 39; with 200 walkers 67 and 69; **the busiest, two trains standing (an engine and 3 cars, an engine and 4 cars) with 200 walkers zoomed out to 0.4 (the hubtrains scenario): 81 portrait, 88 landscape**. Full `npm test` (2026-11-06): 41 of 42 passed on the first run; check-switches failed only on its hard-coded schema number (16), fixed to "16 or later" and passing alone. The M1 bot week in the real start is unchanged (cash $2,553.94, worst lateness 4 minutes, 211 tickets) (each vehicle is 2 calls; longer trains add 2 per car).
**How the hub plays (TR, PX, CG and UI merged and wired by the integrator, 2026-11-06).** Sections "railroad trains (D24)" (TR), "tickets on board (D24)" (PX), "freight trains' goods (D24)" (CG), and in "HUD and panels" the block "Station rework" plus the shared reads in "tutorial" (UI).
- **The railroad's trains (TR).** `rrTick` plans the day's 9 runs at its first tick (`rrPlanDay`) and takes each through `due` > `in` > `stand` > `out` > `gone` (or `cxl`). A run comes in at its time less its running time (`rrRunTime`, 2 minutes' margin) once a slot, its track and the line are free; it appears off the map (`rrEntry`), runs an ordinary route round any standing train by the bypass, and stops short of a switch set against it (D20). It stands with its engine at the far end of its section, `RR.CLEAR` inside the switch legs, so a standing train never locks a switch. Single-line rule: a train never starts onto track that one moving the other way still needs (`rrLineClear`). Wave-off (`rrWaveOff`): within 3u of the engine's side, refused while goods for the Hub are aboard; early is fine. An unattended train's crew leaves 60 minutes after its time; a run that cannot come in for 6 hours is cancelled (its freight orders move to the next freight train with room, `cgOnCancel`). `rrSwitchNeeds` gives `ttSwitchNeeds`-shaped rows (plus `run`, `move`); `rrBoardRows` a row per run with `off` / `on` (PX's `pxRunStatus`), `unload` / `load` (CG's `cgCarStatus`, or `cgPlan` before the train is made up). Look: a small green and cream engine, green coaches, ochre boxcars, flatcars, all drawn as one InstancedMesh per kind (`rrInstDraw`). Debug `rh.rrJump('f2')`.
- **Tickets on board (PX).** About an hour before a passenger run leaves, 4 to 12 travellers set off from the town exits and wait along the platform for a train going their way. When a run stands (`pxOnArrive`), 4 to 12 riders for the Hub step off and walk into town, then the waiting ones board unchecked (16 seats a coach). `checkTickets { tr, car }` walks the player to the car's spot (`pxCarStatus(...).spot`) and checks one traveller per 6 game seconds, 40 cents each (`econPost` 'fares'); any walk stops it; a late boarder is unchecked again. `pxOnDepart`: everyone unchecked rides free (`fareMissed`, `S.rr.tk`).
- **Freight trains' goods (CG).** In the hub world an order is booked on the first freight train due at least 3 game hours later (spilling to the next with room; `cgBook`, `econNextVisit(time, world)`); `cgConsist` makes up the cars, `cgLoad` puts the orders in as items when the train comes in. Unloading works on the railroad's cars while their run stands at the cargo track; goods go onto the cargo dock (drawn on its deck), the shed's bays or the yard and are sorted and delivered as before. `cgReady`: nothing for the Hub aboard. `cgOnDepart`: goods for Outside sold ("Sold down the line" in the ledger), anything for the Hub left aboard set down in the yard. The Willow Ford coffee offer is not offered in a hub game.
- **The screen (UI).** Both boards, the work card and the train orders card ("Hub: next trains") read `rrBoardRows`; the Next job line and the arrows follow a train waiting at a switch, a switch due within the hour, tickets to check (PX's spot of the nearest car), goods to unload, a train ready to send off, a train due within 30 minutes. Buttons over the cars near the player: "Check tickets (n)" and over the engine "Send off" (green when ready; from further than 3u it walks there first). A train is Ready on the boards only with TR's ready and every car checked or unloaded. A tap on an engine opens the train panel's railroad part; on a coach it checks it; on a freight car it opens it; a tap on the joint between two railroad cars does nothing (they are not ours to uncouple; the uncouple command refuses them too). Toasts for the rr, px and cg events (Settings: Train messages), first-time cards, and **the hub tutorial** (`TUT_HUB_STEPS`: welcome, throw switch 2, check tickets, send off, contract, order, unload, sort, deliver, send the freight train off, done). Order texts in the hub name the freight train ("Freight 2, 12:30"); the old worlds keep the Overland.
- **Integrator's changes to the builders' designs:** `rrDebugScenario` (`rh.scenario('hubtrains')`) now plans the day first and replaces p1 and f1's planned runs keeping their ids, so a state ticked before it never has them twice and the later trains still come; the UI reads PX's and CG's real fields (it guessed shapes) and points a coach's arrow at PX's checking spot; `rrUiReady` needs TR's ready and every car checked (a late boarder makes a train unready again); cancelled freight runs rebook their orders (`cgOnCancel`, new). Check timing fixes: check-tickets' walk-away part also works with 2 travellers in the car (TR's shorter coaches), check-rrtrains' scripted player waits for rrReady up to 5 minutes (PX's late travellers), check-hubui's taps wait for a button that stands still.
- **The bot and the acceptance check:** `tests/hub-bot.js` (`hubBotFactory(R, mem)`; `HUB_BOT_SRC`) plays the hub through touch-level commands only: switches from the work board's list (a waiting train or one due within 4 minutes first, then a train to send off, then the switches due within 12 minutes and a standing passenger train's way out, then tickets car by car), unloading a freight train onto the cargo dock beside the car, sorting dock to bays and bays to doors (the hand truck for 2 or more, for one business, not with a passenger train due within 75 minutes), contracts and orders at the work board, never an order it could not deliver before the end. `tests/check-hub-day.js` plays 3 game days: all 27 runs in and out, worst arrival 9 and departure 11 game minutes late, 152 tickets checked and 7 ridden free (96 percent), 20 items ordered, arrived and delivered (all 4 starting contracts done), cash $2,500.00 to $2,589.90, money and items balanced every midnight, the same hash twice and after a save-reload at day 2 17:00. Screenshots `hubday-busy[-wide]-*.png` (day 2 13:28, two trains standing) and `hubday-unload[-wide]-*.png` (day 1 12:40); busiest 74 draw calls (landscape, zoomed out).
- **Tests (2026-11-07):** full `npm test`, 47 checks, all passed (the first full run failed only check-hubworld's count of the scenario's runs, fixed and passing alone, then the whole suite again). The M1 bot week in the real start is unchanged (cash $2,553.94, worst lateness 4 minutes, 211 tickets).
- **Draw calls:** busiest measured 78 landscape (check-rrtrains: a 4-coach and a 5-car train standing, 200 walkers, zoom 0.4), 74 in the bot's busiest moment zoomed out; budget 100.
- **Provisional, for the designer:** TR's shorter engine and cars (4.2 and 1.9 x SC) and the schedule changes (f1 in from the west, f3 18:00 to 19:30); PX's 25 to 60 minute window, 16 seats, the checking spot at the car's middle; CG's 3-hour order lead and "a freight train brings only what was ordered"; UI's Send off shown before the train is ready (the crew's refusal says why), buttons within 9u, the train messages wording; the ledger's "Sold / Ordered down the line".
- **Known gaps:** no new contract offers come in the hub's first 3 days (the town's offers are the real start's timing), so the bot's money comes from the 4 starting contracts and fares; the timetable's "train orders" post at the platform's west end stays as the "Hub: next trains" card.


## Prototype (D25)

The designer's D25 (design-doc section 0): back up and streamline. A small prototype station is the game for now (every new game; `?world=hub`, `start`, `yard` open the older worlds unchanged, in 3D), drawn as pixel art from one fixed isometric view; everything else is parked. Technical plan: **`plans/proto-tech.md`** (layout, schedule, the split, the provisional choices in section 10); sprite contract: **`plans/proto-sprites.md`**. Built 2026-11-10 as a foundation, five builders (T, P, F, R, U), the buildings artist's redraw for T's layout, and the integration; each builder's longer notes are in the comments at the head of its section and in git history (`git show af9d465:DEV_NOTES.md`, section "Prototype (D25)").

**How it fits together (one day):**
- **The world** (`'proto'`, section "starting world layout": `PROTO`, `PROTO_SW`, `PROTO_SHOPS`, `PROTO_BRIDGES`, `buildProtoWorld`): platform 1 behind track 1 (z -4), the through track 2 (z 0), the island platform 2, track 3 (z 10.48); switches W3, W1, E1, E3 with their levers by the platform ends; two wooden footbridges (x -10.75 and 9.25) and the plank walkway at the east end (the handcart's only crossing); the station building, the storage shed (4 bays: STORE, SMITHY, SALOON, OUTSIDE) on platform 2; the main street at right angles behind platform 1 with the general store and the saloon facing each other and the blacksmith further up. Walkers' heights on the bridges come from the walk grid (`walkBridgeY`). Parked: money, the work board, contracts, ordering, building, telegrams, fuel, the tutorial, the town, cards and the D24 hub (`WORLDS.proto.parked`).
- **The day** (section "prototype day", U): opens 06:00 held at the morning board (the clock waits until Start the day, `pdStart`, or 06:30); `PROTO_SCHEDULE` (T): 10 runs, 5 passenger at track 1, 3 freight at track 3, 2 through on track 2; the evening once every run has gone; the summary (`pdEndDay`, or 22:00 by itself); Next day (`pdNextDay`) jumps to the next 06:00.
- **Trains** (T, D24's railroad trains through `RR_WORLDS`): a train stops short of a switch set against it and waits (late, never cancelled); the player sets every switch in and out (`rrSwitchNeeds`); `rrWaveOff` from beside the engine is refused while travellers are in line (`ppReady`) or a freight train is not unloaded and loaded (`pgReady`), and from a footbridge.
- **Tickets** (P, section "prototype passengers"): travellers walk in from the street and line up behind the coach doors (`ppLineSpots`); riders step off first; `{ t: 'punch', tr, car }` walks to the door and punches one ticket (one command, one ticket); `ppCarStatus` (line, ready, off, spot, doors).
- **Goods** (F, section "prototype goods"): f1 and f3 bring goods for the three shops, f2 and f3 take goods for the railroad from the OUTSIDE bay; ordinary carry commands (by hand, hand truck, handcart); the handcart crosses the tracks only at the walkway; `{ t: 'pgDeliver', shop }` hands that shop's goods in at its door. `pgCarStatus` gives `unload` and `load` for the screens.
- **Screens** (U, block "Prototype (D25)" in "HUD and panels", CSS `body.pdui`): the clock, speeds and gear only; the board card; the Next job line (`pdJob`; a switch is asked for in train order: a later train's way is not asked while an earlier train still needs that switch the other way); buttons over the world (Throw, Punch, Send off, Unload, Load, Deliver); the summary card; the first-day guide. In the prototype every tap on a train is answered here (D24's train panel does not open).
- **Drawing** (R, section "pixel renderer"; data in section "pixel art data (generated)", built by `python3 tools/pixelart/build.py` then `embed.py`): the baked ground, everything standing in painter's order from the atlas; travellers from `ppDrawList` at the walk grid's height, the agent's punch from `ppPlayerAct`, goods from F's `pgDrawList`, the pushed carts from `pgCartPose`, the shed open by `pgShedOpen`, the helper arrow at U's `pdHintTarget`; the station's TRAINS board shows the next two trains not gone (`pdBoardRows`; each letter steps down whole on the sloping face); see-through where a train, building or bridge hides the agent or a traveller in line. Default view: scale 3 in portrait (about 260 native px across), 4 in landscape; zoom in whole steps.
- **Saving:** schema 18 (`save-v18.json`); P's and F's stores are optional fields (no schema change).

**Integration (2026-11-10):** wired U to P's and F's real status (`spot`, `unload`, `load`), R to P's travellers, F's goods and carts and U's arrow; the morning card closes when its morning has gone (a game loaded mid-day); no label over a button that says the same; the Centre button steps up over the carry readout; the TRAINS board fix (U's rows were read as times and showed `NaN`); check-protoui keeps its fakes of P and F (it tests the screens; the real pieces are played by check-proto-day); check-protoworld punches p1's tickets before its early wave-off; check-prototrains replaces `ppReady` and `pgReady` for its switch-and-wave player.

**The bot and the acceptance check:** `tests/proto-bot.js` plays through touch-level commands only (reads the board, sets every switch in and out, punches every ticket, sends trains off, unloads and loads with the hand truck, sorts the handcart, delivers to the three shops, ends the day, Next day). It plans switches, send-offs and each standing train's work together (the order of the four most pressing jobs that is least late), sets a switch early for the next move when nothing comes over it first, and breaks off nothing on the way to a throw. `tests/check-proto-day.js` plays two days and checks what its header lists. Measured (2026-11-10): both days all 10 runs in and out, 20 to 21 switch throws a day, 24 travellers punched each day and none left behind, 10 goods unloaded, 6 loaded, 10 delivered each day, the summary matching, items conserved, the same hash twice and after a reload at day 1 about 12:30; worst lateness 30 min (day 1) and 40 min (day 2) on arrival or departure: the schedule is tight for one player who also punches and unloads (the far switches W3 and E3 are 20 to 25 game minutes' walk from platform 1), see the designer's choices. Busiest frame (two trains standing, punching): about 2.3 ms average at 1x, about 120 sprites drawn.

**The station building (2026-11-11):** the designer chose (answers/station-place, "before") to put it back where it was, behind the platform roof: `PROTO.STATION` x 0, right against platform 1's back edge (x -6 to 6, z -18.44 to -12.84), no forecourt, and `PROTO.STREET` starting behind it again (z1 -18.44); builder T's set-back west spot (x -14, 2.5u back; in git history, c3ac9a6) is replaced, the rest of T's layout is kept. It clears the footbridge stairs (on platform 1's track side, z -7.17 to -6.02) and the schedule board (x 9.5); the ground re-baked from the layout. Screenshots `station-back-{portrait,landscape}.png`.

**The view follows the player, and a faster walk (designer, 2026-11-11):** (1) `pixFrame` locks the pixel view on the player's drawn point (his feet, at his height on a bridge: `pixPlayerPoint`), rounded to whole native pixels the same way his sprite is, so while he walks he stays on the middle pixel and the world moves under him in whole pixels (no shimmer; as smooth as his walk, no lag). The old start view on the station's middle (`pixHome`) and the follow-only-near-the-edge are gone; a new game or a load starts centred on him. Pinch and wheel zoom as before. A drag (or `pixLookAt`, the arrow keys) is a look away kept in `cam.ox, oz`: it stays while he stands, eases back once he walks (`PIX.BACK_S` 0.25 s), and the Centre button snaps back. At the map's edges the view stops at the ground layer's edge (in portrait the bottom edge is near platform 2's far half), so there he is off the middle. (2) The prototype's walk is 1.5 times faster (the lead's provisional figure): `WORLDS.proto.pace` gives 3.0u a game minute walking and 2.7u pushing the hand truck or handcart (was 2.0 and 1.8; other worlds unchanged), read through `playerWalkSpeed(T, tool)`; the pixel walk cycle runs at 3.5 frames a u (`PIX.STEP_FR_U`), 10.5 a minute walking and 9.45 pushing, so the feet keep to the ground; the 3D gait (`?view=3d`) already matches stride to speed. `walkTrainAhead` keeps CFG.WALK for how far ahead of a train is kept clear (a slower walk keeps more room). The bots (proto-bot, check-prototrains) read the pace from the game. Measured with the bot after the change: day 1 worst 9 min late (f1 in, p2 out), day 2 worst 22 min (p1 out; the morning starts by taking the handcart back), against 30 and 40 before; busiest frame about 2.2 ms average at 1x. check-pixrender checks the start view is centred on him and that his feet stay on the view's middle pixel every frame of a walk, the look away staying, easing back and the Centre button; screenshots `pixrender-follow-{portrait,landscape}.png`.

**The stall fix (2026-11-11):** the designer's "partway into the day the trains stopped arriving even though I'd thrown the switches it pointed out". Found by playing the page like a person (following only the arrow and the Next job line; late, slow, a switch left wrong, a reload): three causes, each fixed and each reproduced by `tests/check-proto-stall.js` (fails on the old page, passes now). (1) The page's game loop died the first time goods for the town were picked up in the day: the Next job line's "put them in the storage shed" called `pdBay(null)`, which threw inside the frame, so no next frame was asked for (everything froze, and a reload froze again); `pdBay` now takes no filter, and the line points at the good's own bay. (2) Two trains from the same end held off the map together (the line not clear behind a late train leaving that way, e.g. F1 and P2 behind a late X1) came in on the same tick at the same spot, on top of each other, and each waited for the other for ever, while the line kept asking for their switches; a train now comes in only when its length at the entry is clear of other trains (`rrEntryClear`, in `rrTryEnter` and the through trains' entry; prototype only, the hub is unchanged). (3) The line asked for the way in of a train that cannot come in because another still stands at its track (P4 behind a late P3) ahead of that train's tickets, so the player walked to far switches while the train holding everything up stood; such a train's way in is now not asked for (`pdTrackTakenBy`), and when nothing is left to do at the train in the way the line says "P4 cannot come in yet: P3 is still standing there on track 1" with the arrow at its engine; a train waiting at a switch with a train on it says "once it is clear (a train is on it)". Rule chosen (provisional): a train never comes in on top of another; it waits off the map, late, as for any blocked track.

**Checks:** `check-protoworld.js` (layout, walking distances, parked systems, the day's phases, the older worlds in 3D), `check-prototrains.js` (T), `check-punch.js` (P), `check-protogoods.js` (F), `check-pixrender.js` (R), `check-protoui.js` (U), `check-proto-day.js` (the two days), `check-proto-stall.js` (the stall fix). Screenshots `protoday-{morning,busy,busy-close,bridge,deliver,summary,trainsboard}-{portrait,landscape}.png`.

**Provisional, for the designer:** the list in `plans/proto-tech.md` section 10, and from the integration: the bot's lateness (the schedule may want more time between trains, or the far switches nearer); a pushed handcart stays with the player overnight (the next morning starts by taking it back); the 22:00 end of day can cut the evening's second handcart trip short when the day ran late.

## D27 (the working day made visual)

Technical plan: **`plans/d27-tech.md`** (the scale, the layout, punching on board, freight handling, the townsfolk, the lessons, the art list, the split; its section 8.2 is the foundation's list). Sprite contract: `plans/proto-sprites.md` sections 6 (A2) and 7 (A3). The D25 notes above describe the flows that still play until each D27 builder turns theirs on.

### The foundation (2026-11-11)

One builder, alone, before the eight (T, P, F, W, U, R, A1, A2; A3 after T). Built on the lead's tip (the D25 prototype, the 20 px people, the camera follow, the stall fix) with the three D27 art commits merged (A1 people, A2 vehicles/interiors/goods/carts, A3 town/station/nature/tiles; A2's goods and tags live in `sprites_goods.py`).

**What is in place:**
- **The D27 layout** ("starting world layout"): `PROTO` rewritten with the plan's numbers (a person 2.5u = 1.75 m, `PROTO.M`; tracks 1 and 3 at z -6 and 18.6; switches W3 -72.9, W1 -62.5, E1 62.5, E3 72.9, legs of radius 20u; platform 1 60 x 9u, deck `DECK_Y` 0.375; the raised **cargo platform** `CARGO` (alias `P2`) 52 x 14u at `DOCK_Y` 1.6 with two ramps (`RAMPS`), the freight house (`FREIGHTHOUSE`), the storage cells (`STORE`: blocks W and E of 10 x 4 cells of 1.4u), `CART_LANE`, `LOAD_EDGE`; the footbridges `FB` (x -14 and 14, top 7.6, flights out to x +-24.5); `XING_CART` (the plank crossing at x 37 over tracks 2 and 1, `XING_FIXED.proto`); `CART_PATH`; the lever frames `LEVERS` with each switch's stand at its lever (`PROTO_LEVERS`); `STOP`; `STATION`, `CANOPY`, `SCHEDBOARD`, `BENCHES`; `SQUARE`, `STREET` (with boardwalks), `LOTS`, `LANES`, `HOUSES`, `CREEK` (`protoCreekAt`), `WASH`; `BOUNDS` 190 x 185u, `LINE` +-165). `TOWN27`: the twelve street buildings on their lots (A3's boxes as numbers), the church, the school, eight houses, props and nature (a fixed random stream, clear of everything); `SHOPS27` (id, building, name, sign, tag, door and pickup spots on the boardwalk, storage row, keeper, takes, sends); `STORE27_OUT`. `BLD_LAYOUTS.proto` is made from them (an entry may give its own `ext` and `h`; plain boxes in `?view=3d`); `PROTO_SHOPS` (the D25 flow's three shops) is a subset of `SHOPS27`.
- **Stock and the day** ("railroad trains (D24)"): `PROTO_STOCK` at the D27 sizes (engine and tender 18u, coach 13, boxcar and flatcar 10; widths and heights; `vmax`: engines twice as fast, as the line speeds `PROTO.LIM_*`) with the coach's and the boxcar's interiors (`PROTO_STOCK.coach`, `.boxcar`: A2's numbers); `PROTO_SCHEDULE` re-timed as a first cut (plan 8.5: nine runs; f2 leaves 16:10, not 15:50, see the deviations); `RR_WORLDS.proto.ROOM` (a 58u train comes in from off the map); `PD.LATE_S` 23:00.
- **Walking heights and walls** ("walk grid and path-finding"): the walk grid's levels (`PROTO_LEVELS`: the two footbridges, then the cargo platform with its ramps as flights); a walker steps between a level and the ground (or the cargo platform, for a footbridge's cargo-side flight) only at a foot, so the cargo platform's faces are walls; `walkDeckY(T, x, z)` gives a walker's height everywhere (the levels, platform 1, the boardwalks: `PROTO_DECKS`), and the renderer's `pixSimY` and P's `ppSurfY` use it; `walkLevelAt`; `walkBridgeAt` is footbridges only (the wave-off's "come down" rule); the creek is not walked in; the cart may not use the footbridges but goes up and down the ramps.
- **The tables** ("cargo"): `GOODS27` (the 16 kinds: size, cart cells, layers, verb, stack, commodities), `SHOP_GOODS27` (the day's goods by run, the weekly specials, the pickups, `OUT_DAWN`), the D27 commodities (not enumerable on `COMMODITIES`, so the economy and older saves do not see them), `CARGO_TAGS` for all twelve shops, `CARGO_CAR_RULES` costs for the new kinds; `TOOL_KINDS.cart27` (the platform cart, carrier `CART27`, 16 slots; tool 1 in the prototype, used by the D25 flow as its handcart); `toolIsCart`.
- **The switches** (`WORLDS.proto.d27 = { board, handling, town, lessons }`, all off; `d27On(T, what)`; `?d27=0`, `?d27=all` or `?d27=board,handling` on the page; `parked.doorline` follows board (`?doorline=1` keeps the D25 line for check-punch), `parked.guide` follows lessons).
- **The item store's new places** (`CAR` with `cell`/`layer`, `CELL`, `CART`, `TRUCK`, `HAND`, `SHOP`: `cargo27Where`, one good per place and layer, `cargo27Key`, `storeCellAt`, `storeCellOf`) and `itemAudit(S)` (conservation, one place each, layers without gaps, then F's `frAuditDay` once handling is on).
- **The stubs, wired** (each in its owner's new section, with the plan's names and safe defaults): "tickets on board (D27)" (P: `pbTick` after `paxTick`, `pbCmd` for `pbEnter`/`pbWalk`/`pbPunch`/`pbLeave`, `pbPlayerTick`, `pbReady`/`pbOnArrive`/`pbOnDepart` behind `pxReady`/`pxOnArrive`/`pxOnDepart` when board is on, the lists for R and U); "freight handling (D27)" (F: `frTick` after `cgTick`, `frCmd` for `frDoor`/`frTake`/`frPut`/`frCart`/`frDeliver`/`frPickup`, `frPlayerTick`, `frConsist`/`frLoad`/`frReady`/`frOnDepart`/`frCarStatus` behind the `cg*` redirects and `frNewDay` behind `pgNewDay` when handling is on, `frStowPlace`, the lists); "the town (D27)" (W: `tfTick` after `townTick`, `tfAt` true until built); the block "Lessons (D27)" at the end of "prototype day" (U: `PD_LESSON_IDS`, `PD_LESSONS`, `pdLessonTick` in `pdTick`); `playerModeTick` (first thing in the player's tick: `pbPlayerTick || frPlayerTick`); `rngFor(seed, ...keys)`; the debug scenarios `d27coach`, `d27freight`, `d27evening` (`protoDebugEvening`), `d27town`; the test hooks `__railhead.pb`, `.fr`, `.tf`, `.les` (each builder's `*_TEST` object, filled in its own section) and `__railhead.d27` (the foundation's tables and helpers).
- **Saving: schema 19.** 18 > 19 moves a prototype save to the next morning on the D27 layout (`proto18to19`: trains and travellers cleared, the player at the board, every good re-stowed by `protoStow`, the platform cart, lessons done if the first day was); other worlds only the number. The new stores (`S.pb`, `S.fr`, `S.tf`, `S.pd.les`, `S.player.in`) are optional; `checkSaveShape` calls `pbCheckSaveShape`, `frCheckSaveShape`, `tfCheckSaveShape`. `save-v19.json`; every older fixture's v19 load hash recorded.
