- **Drawing** (the renderer's minimal mapping, R draws D27 properly): the new atlas embedded (575 KB; the budget raised to 700, target 600; `build.py` no longer bakes the D25 ground: `--ground` keeps it); the ground worked out in the browser (platform 1, the street, the square, the lanes, the cart path, the creek, the wash, the crossing's planks: about 0.3 to 0.7 s once per track, 3,048 x 1,588 px); the raised cargo platform as one sorted item (`pixDockItem`); A3's buildings by parts at their anchors (shops, church, school, houses, the station, the freight house with its roof see-through over the player, the lever frames with `bld/lever/<N|R>` at each lever as the switch's pickable stand), the canopy and the schedule board at D27 places, TOWN27's props and nature and the shops' dressing (`pixScenery`), A2's stock at its sizes (the sort boxes and picking use each vehicle's `wid` and `h`), the platform cart (`cart27/<dir>/<f>`, the D25 slots on its cells) and the hand truck (`truck/<dir>/<upright|tilt>`), `goods/barrel` drawn as `goods/barrel/whiskey`; in the pixel view a tap on something's own pixels wins over a lever's thumb margin (`pickAt`).
- **The layout export** (`node tools/pixelart/export-layout.js`): `proto-layout.json` v 2 with a `d27` block (every number above, the levels, the decks, `TOWN27` with each building's atlas key, `SHOPS27`, the creek sampled every 2u) for the artists' scene scripts.

**The D25 flows on the D27 layout** (until each switch is on): the line at the door boards at the coaches' ends (`PP.END` in from each; a door beside a footbridge is left out), the freight house's four bays (the storage shed's id `storage`), the platform cart as the handcart (16 slots: one evening trip), the three D25 shops at their SHOPS27 doors; f2 brings 4 goods too (`PG.UNLOAD.f2`). `check-proto-day` plays two days with them (measured: all 9 runs in and out each day, 14 goods unloaded and delivered, 6 loaded, 24 travellers punched; worst lateness 91 minutes (f2 on day 2) with the D25 bot: the walks are about twice D25's; T re-times the day).

**How to build on it** (plan 8.3; one owner per section): fill your own section's functions (their names and shapes are the contract, listed at each section's head), add your fields inside your own store (`S.pb`, `S.fr`, `S.tf`, `S.pd.les`; no schema bump), add your hooks to your `*_TEST` object, replace your placeholder check (`tests/check-<yours>.js`, already in `run-all.js`), and turn your switch on (`WORLDS.proto.d27.<yours> = true`) when it works; until then test with `?d27=<yours>`. Shared sections (track graph, routes, trains, walk grid, player, cargo except F's place rules, saving, debug, test hooks, sim tick, the command switch) are not edited by builders; layout changes are T's (then `node tools/pixelart/export-layout.js`). The atlas is generated: artists commit only in `tools/pixelart/`; the integrator runs `python3 tools/pixelart/build.py && python3 tools/pixelart/embed.py`.

**What each builder turns on:** P `board` (and parks the D25 line: check-punch keeps `?doorline=1`); F `handling` (the platform cart's own rules replace the D25 handcart use; goods a migrated save put in the freight house's bays need moving into cells, or `frNewDay` takes them); W `town`; U `lessons` (parks the D25 first-day guide); T, R, A1, A2, A3 have no switch. The integrator turns every switch on and writes the bot and `check-d27-day.js`.

**Left for R** (placeholders or the foundation's quick drawing): the chunked ground from A3's `tile/` sprites (the worked-out ground is slow on the big map and plain), the cargo platform's faces from `dock/face_*` and its top from `dock/top`, the storage cells from `dock/cell`, the ramps' sorting (drawn flat now), the point rodding (`bld/rodding_x`), the footbridges' sorting against people on the cargo platform at the flights' feet, the east row's see-through over door spots, culling and the faster sort for about 400 items at scale 1, the D27 camera (look-ahead, framing a car), and everything of D27 itself (the cut-away coach and boxcar, seated people, goods in every handling state on the cart's cells, townsfolk).

**Checks:** `check-d27world.js` (new: the numbers, clearances, walking distances (platform 1's middle to each lever frame 33u, the board to the east frame 20u, platform 1 to the cargo platform by a footbridge 36u, the storage to the street pushing the cart 100u), heights and walls, reachability, the v18 save to the next morning, the switches all off and the stubs keeping a day going with `?d27=all`; screenshots `d27-foundation{,-cargo,-street,-overview}-{portrait,landscape}.png`); check-protoworld, check-prototrains, check-punch, check-protogoods, check-pixrender, check-protoui and check-proto-day updated to the D27 numbers (read from the game's tables where they can be: the schedule, PG.UNLOAD, the stock, the switch settings the trains needed).

### T: trains, levers and the day (2026-11-11)

Builder T, in "railroad trains (D24)" (plan 8.3). No switch of its own: it works whatever the D27 switches say.
- **Speeds:** the foundation already doubled the prototype's line speeds (`PROTO.LIM_*`) and the engines' top speed (`PROTO_STOCK.loco.vmax`); kept as they are (walking 3.0u a game minute and the clock unchanged, plan 9 (18)). A train takes about 25 game minutes from appearing off the map to standing.
- **The levers:** each switch is thrown only at its lever in a lever frame by platform 1's end (`PROTO_LEVERS`; the stands were the foundation's). New: in the prototype `switchSpots` gives the lever's use spot as both its spots (before, a player coming from the far side could walk to a spot by the points, 40u off, and throw there). `rrSwitchNeeds` rows carry `lever` ('W1', 'W3', 'E1', 'E3') and `frame` ('W', 'E') in the prototype, for U's lines and R's arrow; `protoLeverOf(T, sw)` gives a switch's `PROTO_LEVERS` entry.
- **For P and F** (`__railhead.rr`): `protoRunCars(S, run)` (each car of a stopping run where it stands or will stand: stock id or null before it is made up, kind, centre, length, which end faces the engine; from the train while it stands, else from `PROTO.STOP` and the stock lengths: the same places); `protoCarEnds(S, run)` (each coach's two ends: the end door, the corner steps on the platform's side, the spot on the deck to step up from, `bridge` when that spot is under a footbridge deck); `protoCarDoors(S, run)` (each boxcar's north door, a flatcar's deck side, with its door spot on the cargo platform as `carryCarDoor`'s).
- **Coach ends and the footbridges:** with the 13u coaches a 3-coach train's ends cannot all clear both footbridges and still stand inside platform 1 (the decks at x +-14 leave 26.6u, two coach pitches are 26.3u); with `PROTO.STOP` as the foundation set it, no end stands beside a flight, and one end of each 3-coach train (the third coach's end towards the engine: x -13.8 east-bound, 13.8 west-bound) is under a deck: that coach boards at its other end (`bridge: true`; the D25 line already leaves such a door out).
- **The day re-timed** (`PROTO_SCHEDULE`, provisional): p1 06:55-07:50, f1 08:40-10:20, p2 10:50-11:35, x1 12:20, p3 13:05-13:50, f2 14:10-16:50, p4 15:20-16:10, p5 17:20-18:00, f3 18:45-20:15. Passenger trains stand 40 to 55 minutes (the plan's 30 were too short with the walks); f1 leaves before p2 comes; x1 an hour after p2 has gone west, so both frames can be set straight and back for it before p3; f2 leaves east after p4 has gone west. The careful player's way through it: the frames are 22 minutes' walk apart, W1 and E1 lie reverse all day but for x1, W3 and E3 are thrown for each freight train in and out. With the D25 flows (tests/proto-bot.js, unchanged) the worst lateness over two days is 13 minutes on seeds 1866, 3, 5, 7, 11 and 42 (the foundation's first cut: 91). Where the remaining minutes come from: the bot underestimates how long a line of tickets takes (it walks off to a far lever first) and returns the platform cart on the second morning. The integrator's D27 bot (punching on board, every handling step) will need its own look at the standing times; the knobs are in `PROTO_SCHEDULE` only.
- **Checks:** `check-levers.js` (new, replaces the placeholder: the frames, every switch thrown over and back at its lever from platform 1, the needs' lever names, p1 waiting at W1 and at E1 set against it until the agent throws them at the frames, the coach ends and freight doors before and while standing (none beside a flight, every coach with an end to board at, every door along the loading edge with its spot on the deck), two days of the D25 flows with `?d27=0`, every throw at its lever, worst lateness at most 15 minutes; screenshots `levers-west-frame-{portrait,landscape}.png`). check-prototrains unchanged (passes on the new day: worst 2 minutes with its trains-only player); check-proto-stall part 3 and check-d27world's stubs run read their moments from the schedule now (P3 stands 20 minutes before P4 is due; the stubs run until the third stopping train stands), same intent.

### Builder R: the renderer (2026-11-11)

All in "pixel renderer (D25)" (plus one branch in "input"), drawn from the other builders' agreed shapes. Until P, F and W fill their lists, `tests/pix-standin.js` puts stand-ins in those shapes on the page (`window.pbDrawList = ...`), on the foundation's scenarios; the renderer reads every list once a frame through `pixCall` (a failing one is reported once on the console and drawn as empty).
- **The ground in chunks** (`pixGroundDraw`, `pixChunkBuild`): 256 x 256 px chunks made when the view needs them from A3's `tile/` sprites (a tile per 1u cell by `pixCellTile`: grass with soft patches of dry grass, the street, dirt for the square, lanes and alleys, the cart path, ballast, the crossing's planks, water, banks and sand along the creek, gravel in the wash), then ties and rails per pixel at the D27 gauge (`PIX.GAUGE` 1.025, ties 3.7 long every 0.85, ballast 4.6), platform 1's deck from the D25 `plat/` tiles, and the flat things baked in once (`pixFlatList`: the shops' boardwalks and shadows, the church's and school's shadows, tall grass, flowers, cattails, the wash bridge, the point rodding from each lever along x to its points). Cache of 40 chunks (LRU; kept across a new game or a load, the layout being the same), made within 8 ms a frame (at least one; a missing one is plain grass for that frame), one more round the view in idle time while the cache has room. A chunk takes about 1.5 to 3 ms (unthrottled). The old worked-out ground (`pixBuildGround`) is no longer called.
- **The cargo platform** from `dock/top`, `dock/cell` (the storage cells), `dock/face_s` (a post every 4u, a bumper between) and `dock/face_e`, one canvas, one sorted item (`dock`). **The ramps** are sorted items (`ramp_e`, `ramp_w`); a walker on a ramp is drawn after it and after the platform (`pixWalkerY`: `base` and the new `after`).
- **Sorting** (`pixSort`): culled to the view first (as before), pairs compared only within a screen grid of 48 px buckets, Kahn's order from a binary heap; `pixBehind` also honours `after` (a list of ids an item is drawn after). Scale 1 at the busiest: about 300 items, 1,000 pairs.
- **The cut-away** (`pixCutItem`, `pixCutInner`): a coach P lists (`pbCutaway`) or the player is in (`pbPlayerPose.stock` or `S.player.in`), a boxcar the player is in or at the door of (within 1.6u of its north door spot), and any boxcar with its door open (`frDoorOpen` > 0), while standing straight (heading e or w), is one item in the global order whose drawing sorts its own insides: `veh/coach_in/<h>/floor`, then the benches, stove, cooler, closet, lamps (lit at night), rack bags, P's people in that coach and the agent, in painter's order, then the sill and the open end (boxcar: floor, `veh/boxcar_door/<h>/<round(3 open)>`, goods on its cells, the agent, the sill). A seated person sorts after his own bench when he faces se or sw, before it when ne or nw (its back is then nearer the view). People in a whole coach are hidden; a traveller climbing the steps of a whole coach is drawn outside it. With the car whole the north door is hidden, so no open-door sprite is used.
- **People:** P's `pbDrawList` (acts wait, benchSit, walk, climb, sitDown, sit, sitTicket, standUp: A1's frames; seated people at their seat anchor at floor height, A1's seated anchor; the punched mark `ppl/mark/punched/<dir>` at the frame's `extra.mark` when `punched`), W's `tfDrawList` (walk, idle, talk, benchSit), the D25 `ppDrawList` as before. A tap on a seated traveller picks `{ k: 'pp', id, who, stock, seat, tr }`; in the input a tap on one sends `{ t: 'pbPunch', tr, stock, seat }` once D27 board is on.
- **The agent** (`pixAgentItem`, `pixAgentFr`, `pixHeldDraw`): F's `frPose` (`verb`: carry/lift/put with phase lift/put/walk, shoulder, roll, drag, heave, truck, stack/stackHigh, door, handOver, push, pull; `prog` 0..1 picks the frame; `anim`/`frame` override), else P's `pbPlayerPose` (walk, idle, punch, climb), else the D25 rules. Goods in his hands (F's place `hand`): `goods/<kind>/held/<dir>` at the frame's `extra.hands` (in front or behind by `extra.behind`), dragged, rolled (`goods/<kind>/roll/<dir>/<f>`) and heaved goods on the ground `extra.ahead` u ahead of his feet.
- **Goods** (`pixGoodsList`): F's `frDrawList` once it lists anything (`{ id, kind, variant, tag, x, y, z, rot, place, stock, cell, layer }`; place `cell`, `shop`, `ground` drawn in the open; `car` only inside a cut-away boxcar or on a flatcar; `cart`, `truck`, `hand` with them), until then the renderer's own reading of the D27 places in the item store (CELL, CAR with cell, CART, TRUCK, HAND, SHOP). Keys `goods/<kind>[/<variant>][/z]` (variants by commodity, `PIX_VARIANT`), lying barrels and kegs `goods/<kind>/side/<x|z>`, tags `tag/<shop>` at `extra.tag_at` (left out at scale 1).
- **The cart and the hand truck** at F's `frCartPose` / `frTruckPose` (`{ x, y, z, f, held, tilt }`): `cart27/<dir>/<f>` (wheels turn when it moves) with goods on A2's `extra.cells` (`cell` = c * 2 + r, a second layer a good's height up); `truck/<dir>/<tilt|upright>` with `goods/<kind>/truck/<dir>` when tilted or the good at its `nose`. Without F's poses, the D25 rules.
- **See-through:** as before for the player and anyone standing (travellers, townsfolk), now also goods at the east row's pickup spots and the cart and hand truck (behind a car or building); never a cut-away car's insides, sill or end, a lever or the barber's pole (`PIX_NO_SEE`). Partly see-through frames are kept (`PIXA.part`) and the frame is drawn once with last frame's see-through parts, again only when they change.
- **Night** (`pixNight`): from 19:00 to 21:00 the scene darkens to a deep blue (a multiply, `PIX.NIGHT_RGB`), back from 05:00 to 06:30; then the light of every `_lit` sprite (the pixels where it differs from its unlit frame: the station's and coaches' windows, the lamps, a coach's hanging lamps) is drawn again on top, so they glow. Coaches use `veh/coach_lit`, lamps `prop/lamp_lit` after 19:30.
- **The camera** (`PIX.CAM`; plan 1.6): a dead zone (the middle third of the view), a look-ahead (6u walking, 10u with a tool, eased in over 0.8 s), re-centring on him while he stands (1.5 s), framing a car he is in (its centre plus a third of his offset, 0.35 s), snapping on a jump (a load, a check placing him) and on the Centre button; whole pixels (his rounded point plus the rounded offset of the resting centre, so nothing shimmers while it follows); the look away (drag, `pixLookAt`) on top, and the view clamped to the map as before. The designer's "stay centred" (2026-11-11) becomes "stay in the middle third": `PIX.CAM.DEAD = 0` and `AHEAD = 0` give the old locked view back.
- **Upscale:** the page's canvas is now the native buffer itself, shown `PIX.scale` times bigger by its CSS size (image-rendering: pixelated); no full-screen copy each frame (it was most of a frame without a GPU). check-pixrender and check-protoworld check the canvas size that way now.
- **Frame times** (check-pixperf, 4x CPU throttling, best of three 1.5 s windows; the test machine draws without a GPU): station (p2 with a coach cut away mid-punch, f1 with a door open, storage stacked, the cart loaded) about 5 ms average at the default zoom and 6 to 8 ms at scale 1 (300 items), 98th percentile 7 to 12 ms; the street at dusk with 16 townsfolk 3 ms and 5 ms. Unthrottled 1.4 to 2.3 ms. Within the plan's 8 and 14 ms, the scale-1 station closest.
- **Checks:** `tests/check-interiors.js` (the coach and boxcar cut away and their painter's order, picks on sitters, the camera framing, every handling step, ramps and flights against people on the cargo platform, townsfolk and the east row's see-through, lamps at night, the chunks), `tests/check-pixperf.js`; `check-pixrender.js` updated (the camera's dead zone, the canvas scaling). Screenshots `test-output/d27-r-{coach,coach-close,coach-overview,boxcar,boxcar-close,boxcar-overview,cargo,cargo-overview,street,street-overview,street-night}-{portrait,landscape}.png`.
- **For the integrator / P, F, W:** the shapes read are listed above; differences are best settled in the renderer's adapters (`pixGoodsList`, `pixAgentFr`, `pixActAnim`). Missing art for a follow-up: lit windows for the town at night (`bld/shop_<kind>/<f>/body_lit`, `bld/house/<n>/<f>_lit`, `bld/church_lit`, `bld/school_lit`, `bld/freighthouse/body_lit`), the rodding's turn and crank to reach the points across track 1 (`bld/rodding_z`, `bld/rodding_crank`; the runs now end beside the points), a keg of nails (`goods/keg/nails`), and the creek's second water frame is not used (the ground is baked).

### Freight handling (builder F, 2026-11-11)

Section "freight handling (D27)" (its head comment is the full contract); **`WORLDS.proto.d27.handling` is on**. Every step is a timed, visible action of the player, kept as one job in `S.fr.job` (a list of steps worked out when the command is given: `walk` (the ordinary walk), `go` (a short straight walk inside a car, which the walk grid does not cover; `S.fr.inCar`, `S.player.y` the floor), `act` (`door`, `pick`, `put`, `grab`, `park`, `hand`, each so many game seconds)). A good moves only at the end of its pick or put, so it is in one place every tick; R draws the in-between from the job's progress. Another command stops a job (out of a car first, then that command).
- **Commands:** `frDoor { stock }`, `frTake { item }`, `frPut { to }` (a full place, or `{ kind: 'CART' | 'CELL' | 'CAR', stockId }` for the first free place there, or `{ kind: 'GROUND', x, z }`), `frCart { act: 'grab' | 'park', tool?, x?, z?, f? }` (the cart's centre at x, z facing f), `frDeliver { shop }` (pushes the cart to the shop if he has it, then every good of that shop off the cart to the door), `frPickup { shop }` (the shop's waiting goods onto the cart). Truck goods fetch the hand truck on the way when it is within 40u (else heaved); taking by hand leaves a pushed tool where it stands.
- **Grids and stacking:** car floor 11 x 4 (cell = c + 11 r; columns 4 to 6 in front of the door kept clear), cart 4 x 2 (cell = 2 c + r, A2's order), storage 10 x 4 per block (long goods take 2 or 3 cells); a stack sits square on the good under it; a storage stack is one kind. The cart never buries a shop's good under one delivered later (`frCartFree`, `FR_SHOP_ORDER`).
- **Trains:** `frConsist` (a flatcar at the back of Monday's f1 for the plough), `frLoad` (SHOP_GOODS27 in and the weekly specials, from each car's ends), `frReady` (no goods for the town aboard and the run's load aboard, or all there is at the station), `frOnDepart` (goods for the railroad sold, doors shut, the crew stows anything left). 17:00: `frPutOut` (pickups plus tomorrow's weekly OUT specials from the shop that makes them; nothing new while a shop's spot still holds goods). Day 0 starts with OUT_DAWN in the OUT rows. Goods a pre-handling save left in the freight house's bays are moved into the cells (each tick and at `frNewDay`); the v18 fixture's v19 hash was re-recorded for this (its goods now go to the cells).
- **For R:** `frDrawList` (every good with its world position, `place`, `rot`, `variant`, `tag`, `layer`; `place: 'moving'` with `prog` while lifted or set down), `frPose` (A1's animation key, frame and phase), `frCartPose`, `frTruckPose` (with tilt), `frDoorOpen`, `frInCar`. These only read the state (`frRead`). The foundation's stand-in renderer still draws the D25 lists (`pgDrawList`), so with handling on the goods in cells, on the cart and in cars are not drawn until R uses `frDrawList`.
- **For U:** `frCarStatus` (pgCarStatus's fields plus `doorOpen`, `door`, `spot`, `cartSpot`), `frStorage`, `frToDeliver`, `frPickups`, and `frJobHint(S)`: the next handling step as a sendable command with a sentence and a place (`{ cmd, text, x, z }`, `{ busy, k, text }` while a job runs, null when freight has nothing): it plans unloading (the cart to the nearest car door, as full as it gets, then to the storage rows), loading, the evening (load the cart for town in street order, deliver, pick up, back to the OUT rows) and stepping out of a car. Both checks play by it, so it is also a working scripted player for the integrator's bot.
- **Measured (the checks' scripted player, freight only):** Monday's three freight trains are unloaded, loaded and sent off on time (f1 10:47, f2 16:11, f3 21:03); Monday's evening work (15 goods to 9 shops, 7 pickups back) takes about 10.5 game hours: one cart trip from the storage to the street is about 50 to 60 minutes each way (about 140u from the west block), and a day's goods need 3 trips. So **the evening cannot carry a day's goods** (provisional; for the designer, below).
- **Provisional choices (for the designer):** the handling times (door 40 s; pick-up and put-down each: carry 32, shoulder 40, roll 24, drag 16, truck 24, heave 64; stackHigh +8; cart handle 16; handing in 24); the hand truck used for truck goods within 40u, else heaved; the cart parks east of a car's doorway on the loading edge and in the carriageway at a shop; the express box does not return yet (the tables have no pickup for it); deliveries are proposed in the evening only, so the goods a day brings take longer than an evening to deliver: options are deliveries between trains as well, a faster or bigger cart, the shops' rows moved to the east block (nearer the ramp), or fewer goods a day.
- **Checks:** `check-handling.js` (each of the 16 kinds by its verb and timing, storage > cart > storage; stacking and its refusals; the door; walking in and out of a car; Monday's f1 with the flatcar; a freight day by the hints with the audit every hour, twice for the hash, and a save mid-step), `check-delivery.js` (the evening by the hints: pickups at 17:00, every good to its own shop, the cart over the crossing only, refusals, the pickups into the OUT rows; Tuesday's f2 loads 5 with the furniture, f3 2; uncollected pickups stay and nothing new is put out; hash twice and a save mid-delivery). check-protogoods, check-proto-day, check-protoui, check-pixrender and check-proto-stall now open with `?d27=0` (they test the D25 flows); check-d27world expects handling on.

### P: tickets punched on board (2026-11-11)

Section "tickets on board (D27)" (its head comment has the whole flow, the store and the shapes). **`WORLDS.proto.d27.board` is on**, so the D25 line at the door is parked (`parked.doorline`; its code stays; `ppDrawList` and `ppPlayerAct` return nothing while parked). Everything runs only while `pbOn(T)` (board on and no `?doorline=1`); otherwise the `pb*` redirects hand back to the `pp*` code, so `check-punch.js` (`?doorline=1`) still plays the D25 line.
- **The flow:** travellers (`PB.PER_TRAIN` 4 to 10) walk in from the street an hour before a passenger run and wait on platform 1, half on the benches (`benchSit`), the rest standing under the canopy (`pbCome { run, n }`). At the arrival every coach gets 29 places (28 walk-over seats and the side seat, `S.pb.cars[stock]`), 2 to 6 through riders (punched elsewhere) and 1 to 3 riders for the Hub; those stand up, walk the aisle, climb down at the nearer usable end and walk off (`pbOff`). Each waiting traveller is given the nearest usable coach end and a seat (`pbPickSeat`: nearest that end's door, window first, alternating sides, the side seat last), walks to the end's steps, waits there while the coach still has a rider for the Hub, climbs, walks the aisle and sits (`pbSeated`). An end is usable when its boarding spot on platform 1 (2.6u north of the track, at the steps) is open and not under a footbridge (p1's last coach has its west end at the west footbridge).
- **The player:** `pbPunch { tr, stock, seat? }` from the platform walks him to the nearer usable end and up the steps (`pbEnter`), then along the aisle to the row; he faces across the aisle, the traveller holds the ticket out, 30 game seconds, `pbPunched` and `pdCount('punched')`. Without a seat: the nearest unpunched traveller in that coach. A repeated pbPunch for the punch in hand carries on (does not restart it). `pbWalk { s, stock? }` walks the aisle and on into the next coach over the end platforms; `pbLeave`, or any walk or use command (a tap on the ground, a switch, a send-off), takes him to the nearest usable end and down, then that walk carries on. A held joystick walks the aisle; towards the platform it leaves. Inside, `S.player.in` holds his place, `S.player.x, z` his world point and `S.player.y` the floor (1.6; the end platforms 1.55), so the renderer's walker height and everything that reads the player work; the walk grid is not used.
- **Departure (provisional, plan 3.1):** `pbReady` only when every rider for the Hub is off, everyone who came is seated, every ticket aboard is punched and the player is off the train; `pbWhyNotReady(S, run, tr)` gives the reason for the screens ("3 tickets still to punch", "Step off the train first"). The alternative is in the code: `PB.RIDE_FREE = true` lets unpunched travellers ride free (counted missed, `pbMissed`).
- **For R:** `pbDrawList(S)` (everyone of P's: platform, steps, aisle, seats; `act` per plan 3.2, `key` the atlas key, `y` the surface, seated `x, z` the seat's hip point at the floor's height (A1's sit anchor), `punched` for the mark, `stock` null on the ground), `pbPlayerPose(S)` (null unless on a train; `act` walk, idle, punch, climb; `key`), `pbCutaway(S)` (the coach he is in, also on its steps), `pbCoachView(S, stock)` (seats with who sits there, the 15 benches as their own sprites with keys `veh/seat/<f>` and `veh/sidebench/<f>`, stove, cooler, closet, lamps, ends; world u; `h` 'e' or 'w' for `veh/coach_in/<h>/...`). Coaches stand on the straight of track 1, so a local point is (cx + h lx, cz + lz) with h +1 for a coach heading east (stove west), -1 heading west (stove east; facings se and nw swap, ne and sw stay). Until R's cut-away lands the player and the seated are hidden inside the whole coach (the foundation's renderer).
- **For U:** `pbCarStatus(S, stock)` `{ seated, punched, toPunch, through, boarding, off, at, inside, punching, ends }`, `pbRunStatus(S, run)` `{ seated, punched, toPunch, boarding, off, waiting, coming, through, ready, why }`, the events above; the D25 `punch` command still works (it becomes pbPunch with `car` as the stock).
- **Checks:** `check-onboard.js` (new, replaces the placeholder): waiting (benches and standing, no lines), riders off before anyone climbs into their coach, seats by the rule at every assignment and the same in a second run, entering from the platform, one ticket per punch with the ticket held out and the agent's punch, a through rider refused, coach to coach inside without stepping down, the wave-off refused while a ticket is unpunched and while the player is aboard, a tap on the platform taking him off and on, the send-off with everyone aboard, the figures, the ride-free alternative, a heading-west coach's view, the same hash twice, saves mid-boarding and mid-aisle reloading the same, the `d27coach` scenario, `?doorline=1` leaving it all off; screenshot `onboard-punch-portrait.png`. `check-d27world.js`: the switches' expectation now board on; its quick day with every switch on punches every seated ticket (`R.pb.pbTestPunchAll`) as its switchman sets switches. `tests/proto-bot.js` punches on board when board is on (pbPunch at the nearest coach with a ticket, then seat by seat inside, waiting inside while travellers still sit down, then pbLeave); `check-proto-day.js` counts `pbCome`, `pbPunched`, `pbMissed` and the busy moment's punch from `S.pb.job` (measured: two days, every run in and out, 34 and 31 travellers all punched, none missed; worst lateness 87 minutes, f2 on day 2, as the foundation's 91 with the D25 line).
- **Older checks with board on:** check-prototrains, check-protoworld and check-protoui script the D25 line at the door (the Punch button, `punch` commands, ppReady), so with board on their passenger trains were never ready; they now open the page with `&doorline=1` (one query each) until T and U rewrite them for punching on board. check-pixrender, check-proto-stall, check-protogoods, check-punch and check-save pass unchanged.
- **Provisional (for the designer):** 4 to 10 travellers a train, 2 to 6 through riders and 1 to 3 for the Hub a coach; half wait on benches; travellers take the coach end nearest where they waited (so most fill the nearest coach rather than spreading out); 30 game seconds a punch; the strict departure rule; nobody bumps into anyone in the aisle (people pass through each other).

### The townsfolk (builder W, 2026-11-11)

Section "the town (D27)"; `WORLDS.proto.d27.town` is **on**. Check: `tests/check-townsfolk.js`.
- **The town graph** (`TOWN_GRAPH`, made at load from `TOWN27` and `SHOPS27`): 81 nodes and 99 straight edges: each shop's door spot on its boardwalk, the alley mouths, five town benches (the art's dressing: store, hotel x2, barber, sheriff), the back lanes and the eight house doors, the church and school doors, the depot square, a way round each side of the station to platform 1, three waiting spots (`wait0..2`), three platform benches and the station door, and a far west edge (`edge_w`) the cowhands ride in from. All shortest paths once at load (Floyd, a few milliseconds). Every edge lies on open cells of the player's walk grid and clear of the track, the ramps, the cart crossing and the footbridges (the check walks them). `townPath(a, b)` takes node ids.
- **The people** (`TF_AGENTS`, 19): the 14 of plans/proto-sprites.md (the twelve keepers, the preacher, the teacher; the feed keeper is `storekeeper.b`, named "Walt Hensley" by the builder), a laundress (`launderer`), two pupils (`child`, `child.b`) and two cowhands (`cowhand`, `cowhand.b`). Most keepers live over their shop; the banker, assayer, feed keeper, undertaker, preacher, teacher, laundress and pupils have the eight houses. Names marked "(p)" are the builder's.
- **Routines** (`TF_ROUTINES` by kind: keeper, barkeep, clerk, sheriff, preacher, teacher, pupil, laundress, cowhand): errands `{ place, from (be there by), to (then back), p, days, fixed }`, places home, work, porch (outside his own door), stroll (a boardwalk or square spot), bench, or a building (hotel for dinner, saloon, store, barber, livery, church). Shops open 07:30 to 18:00 Monday to Saturday; school 08:00 to 15:00 Monday to Friday; church Sunday 10:00 to about 11:45 for everyone but the cowhands; cowhands ride in some evenings (most Saturdays) for the saloon. Meeting trains: Nell O'Hara p1 and p3 (every day), Tom Dade p2 and Ezra Cole f3 (Monday to Saturday), on platform 1 from 10 minutes before the run's time until 5 minutes after it really stands (`S.rr.runs`; capped 2 hours), event `tfMeet { run, id }`. Chats in pairs at 11:00, 13:40, 16:45 and 18:30 (a keeper at his door and someone within 20 minutes' walk, or two people at a boardwalk spot); any two people standing outdoors within 1.6u talk in `tfDrawList`.
- **Plans** (`tfPlans(seed, day)`): a pure function of the seed and the day (05:00 to 05:00), cached; each agent's stream `rngFor(seed, 'tf', day, i)` (four draws per routine line, taken or not), the pairs' `rngFor(seed, 'tf', day, 'pairs')`; jitter up to 10 minutes (church and school only earlier). Each errand sets off in time by the graph's walking time; errands that would make a meeting, church or school late are dropped.
- **The tick** (`tfTick`, after `townTick`): about 2 to 4 microseconds for all 19 (limit 50). A standing agent compares one time; a walker steps 0.32u along his edge (2.4u a minute). The player within 0.7u ahead on the edge: he waits up to 6 game seconds, then walks on. At a building's door he goes in (`in`, not drawn). A delivery handed in at a shop (`pgDelivered`, `frDelivered`) brings its keeper, if in, out to his door for 4 minutes (`talk`, facing the door spot). `tfAt(S, shop | bld)`: his keeper is in it or standing at its door (true while town is off).
- **The store** `S.tf = { day, ag: [{ e, t0, at, go, seg, s, wait, in?, seat?, met?, out? }] }` (no schema bump); a save without it, a new day, or the town switched on mid-game re-plans and settles everyone where their errand of the moment puts them. `tfCheckSaveShape` checks it.
- **For R**: `tfDrawList(S)` `[{ id ('tf_<id>'), who, name, x, y (walkDeckY), z, f (heading, x = sin f), act ('walk' | 'idle' | 'talk' | 'benchSit') }]`, outdoors only; seats are 0.55u either side of a bench's middle on its seat line (`TOWN_GRAPH.nodes[].seats`). Not drawn yet (R's section); check-townsfolk borrows the travellers' drawing for its screenshot (`test-output/d27-townsfolk-portrait.png`). `rh.scenario('d27town')` sets some out (a chat at the store, the preacher on a hotel bench, three walkers, the sheriff on platform 1).
- **Check** (`check-townsfolk.js`, about 15 s): the graph (open ground, joined, nothing on track, ramps, crossing or footbridges), the sprites for every agent (idle, walk, talk, benchSit), a week with scripted trains (each 0 to 40 minutes late; the railroad's own trains need P's and F's work to leave): everyone with work in it at 10:00 Monday to Friday, everyone but the cowhands in church at 10:30 Sunday, all 26 meetings on platform 1 with tfMeet, never on the track, chats, benches, the cowhands; the same week twice, a mid-day save reloaded goes on the same, through the page's import too; a save without S.tf; damaged stores refused; the delivery step-out and tfAt; waiting for the player; the real p1 on day 0 met by the hotelkeeper; the cost.
- **Edited outside the section:** `WORLDS.proto.d27.town = true`; check-d27world's switch line (town on).

### Lessons and screens (builder U, 2026-11-11)

**On:** `WORLDS.proto.d27.lessons = true` (the D25 first-day guide is parked with it; `?d27=0` brings it back, as check-protoui does). Code: the block "Lessons (D27)" at the end of section "prototype day" (the sim: `PD_LESSONS`, `pdLessonTick`, `pdLessonCmd`, `pdLesNewDay`) and, in "HUD and panels", the block "Prototype (D25)" (the card `#pdLesson`, `pdLesCard`, `pdLesHud`, the D27 jobs `pdLesJob`, `pdJobCoach27`, `pdJobCar27`, `pdJobEvening27`, the buttons `pdActs27`, the summary, the board's figures, Settings' replay).

**The six lessons** (each 2 or 3 steps; the sentence on the card, then the Next job line's short form):
- **Switches** (at the day's start, `pdDay`; the first train's switch in, from the schedule: W1 for P1): 1. "Trains stop short of a switch set against them. The levers are in the frame at the west end of the platform: walk there." (done: the player within 3u of the lever's use spot) 2. "Pull lever W1 to set the way into track 1 for P1." (`switchThrown` W1 leaving it set for P1, read from `rrSwitchNeeds` once on the event) 3. "Its disc turns: W1 is set for track 1. When P1 has passed, set it back for the next train." (P1 arrives).
- **Tickets on board** (the first `pbSeated`; board on): 1. "Travellers have boarded and sat down. Step up into a coach at its end." (`pbEnter`) 2. "Walk the aisle and tap a traveller to punch the ticket." (`pbPunched`) 3. "Punch everyone. The train can leave once every ticket is punched: then step off and Send off at the engine." (a passenger train waved off).
- **Unloading** (a freight train with goods for the town arrives, `rrArrived`; handling on): 1. "Goods for the town have come. Slide the boxcar's door open." (`frDoor` open) 2. "Bring the platform cart to the open door." (the cart within 4.5u of an open door's spot) 3. "Step into the car and lift the goods onto the cart." (`frPut` of a town good to the cart) 4. "Push the cart to the storage area and stack each good in its shop's row." (`frStored` of a town good).
- **Loading** (a freight train that takes goods away stands with nothing left to unload: `frCarStatus` of its cars; handling on): 1. "This train takes goods away. They wait in the OUT rows: load them on the cart." (`frPut` of an OUT good to the cart) 2. "Wheel them to an open door and put each one in the car." (`frLoaded`).
- **Delivery** (`pdEvening`; handling on): 1. "The trains are done for the day. Load the cart with the town's goods from storage." (`frPut` of a town good to the cart) 2. "Push it down the east ramp, over the crossing and up the path to town." (the cart in the main street) 3. "Stop at a shop's door and tap Deliver." (`frDelivered`).
- **Pickup** (the evening, Delivery done, `frPickups` not empty; handling on): 1. "Some shops send goods out by rail: they wait on the boardwalk. Load them on the cart." (`frPicked`) 2. "Bring them back to the OUT rows for tomorrow's trains." (`frStored` of an OUT good).

**How they work:** `S.pd.les[id]` is the step (or -1 done or skipped), `S.pd.lesWait` the lessons whose first card waits for Show me (both optional, in the save; `pdCheckSaveShape` checks `lesWait`). A step is done by its own event or a later step's (a player who goes ahead is not held back); a state step (`at`) is checked each tick for the step in hand only. Command `{ t: 'pdLesson', id, act: 'show' | 'skip' }` and `{ t: 'pdLesson', act: 'replay' }` (one case added to the command switch's `pdStart` line, routed to `pdCmd`); events `pdLesson { id, step }`, `pdLessonDone { id, skipped }`, `pdLessonReplay`. The page: the first step's card is a bottom sheet (portrait; right panel in landscape) with a small atlas picture, Skip and Show me, holding the clock (as the morning board; the arrow and the buttons over the world wait); Show me and Skip act at once on the page (`pdUi.lesAct`), since their command waits for a tick the held clock would not give. Later steps: a slip under the Next job line (Skip, OK folds it until the next step), the clock runs. The Next job line is the step's short form and the arrow (`pdHintTarget`) its target. Settings: "Lessons: Replay" (prototype only).

**The screens for the new work** (each only with its D27 switch on; the D25 flows unchanged otherwise): buttons over the world, **at most three, nearest first** (all of them now): Step in (at a coach end with tickets to punch: `pbEnter { tr, stock, end }`), Punch (n) (over the nearest unpunched seated traveller in the coach the player is in: `pbPunch { tr, stock, seat }`), Step off (`pbLeave`); Open door / Close door (`frDoor`), Take cart / Leave cart (`frCart { act: 'grab' | 'park', x, z }`, u to two decimals), Lift (the nearest good of `frDrawList` within 2.5u: `frTake { item }`), Put (on the cart `frPut { to: { kind: 'CART' } }`, in a car `{ kind: 'CAR', stockId }`, in the storage cell under the player `{ kind: 'CELL', block, c, r }`), Deliver (n) (at a shop door, the cart's goods for it: `frDeliver`), Pick up (n) (at a pickup: `frPickup`). The Next job line's D27 jobs: "Step into coach 1 of P1: 2 tickets to punch", "Punch the tickets in coach 1 of P1 (2 to punch)", "Every ticket here is punched: step off the train"; F's `frJobHint` first when it gives one, then "Open the door of F1's car 1 (6 for the town)", "Unload F1's car 1 onto the cart (...)", "Stack the goods for the town in their shops' rows", "Load F2: fetch its goods from the OUT rows", "Load the goods into F2's car 2"; the evening: "Deliver n to the saloon", "Pick up n at the bank: goods to go out by rail", "Take the goods back to the OUT rows", "Load the cart with the town's goods (n to deliver)", then end the day. A tap on a coach steps in (walking to its end), on a boxcar opens its door. P's and F's refusals (`pbRefused`, `frRefused`) show as messages; the buttons keep clear of a message on screen. The summary: "Tickets: 11 seated, 10 punched", "Goods: 6 unloaded, 6 stored, 4 loaded", "Town: 5 delivered, 3 picked up"; the board card during the day: "So far today: ...". `seated` and `stored` are counted in `pdTick` from `pbSeated` and `frStored`; `picked` is F's `pdCount`. They are added to `S.pd.stats` when first counted (so older saves' hashes stay the same).

**Interfaces relied on** (read defensively: a count, a list or an object of counts all work, a stub's null is "nothing"): `pbCarStatus(S, stock).toPunch` and `.ends` (`[{ x, z }]` or `[{ steps: { x, z } }]`), `pbDrawList(S)` entries `{ x, y, z, stock, seat, act ('sit' | 'sitTicket'), punched, through }`, `S.player.in.{ tr, stock }`; `frCarStatus(S, stock).{ unload, load, doorOpen, spot }`, `frCartPose(S).{ x, z, held }` (else the tool store and `S.player.tool`), `frDrawList(S)` entries `{ id, kind, x, y, z, place }`, `frToDeliver(S)` and `frPickups(S)` by shop id, `frJobHint(S)` (a sentence, or `{ text, t | x, z, label, key }`); goods in `HAND`, `CART`, `CELL` places with waybill tags (`OUTSIDE` for the railroad); T's `protoCarEnds` and `protoCarDoors` when they exist (else beside the car).

**Provisional (for the lead and the designer):** (1) the switches lesson comes before the switch rows on the Next job line, but the other lessons come after them (so a train's way out is still asked for while its lesson runs; the plan put every lesson first). (2) A lesson still open the next morning closes (replay brings it back). (3) Replay starts every lesson again (no choice of one). (4) The slip of a later step can be folded with OK; the Next job line keeps saying it. (5) At most three buttons over the world everywhere, D25 flows included. (6) The `frCart` command's x, z are in u (F decides).

**Checks:** `tests/check-lessons.js` (new; stand-ins for P's and F's status where a lesson reads them): each lesson triggers once at its moment and not with its D27 part off, steps advance on their events and states, a later step's event moves it on, the switches lesson played by the real sim (and the same twice), Show me, Skip, replay, the next morning, the save; on the page (portrait and landscape): the first card holds the clock (and hides the arrow) until Show me, a later step is a slip and the clock runs, OK and Skip, Settings' replay, every D27 button sending its command, the D27 jobs, the summary's and the board's figures, nothing overlapping; screenshots `lessons-{hold,slip,board,freight,evening}-{portrait,landscape}.png`. `check-protoui.js` now opens with `?d27=0` (it tests the D25 screens and the first-day guide). `check-d27world.js` checks the four switches exist (not that they are all off).

### The integration (2026-11-11)

The six builders merged in the plan's order on the foundation (T, R, F, P, W, U), then the follow-up artist's night art
(lit bodies for every town building, the rodding's turn and crank, a keg of nails, door lanterns); the atlas rebuilt
(608 KB). **Every D27 switch is on** (`WORLDS.proto.d27`: board, handling, town, lessons); `?d27=0` gives the D25 flows
on the D27 layout. Each builder's own notes are the parts above; what the integration changed:
- **Wiring:** R's renderer draws the real lists (P's `pbDrawList`, `pbPlayerPose`, `pbCutaway`, `pbCoachView`; F's
  `frDrawList`, `frPose`, `frCartPose`, `frTruckPose`, `frDoorOpen`; W's `tfDrawList`): the shapes matched, no adapter
  change was needed beyond the cart's new cell count. U's buttons and lessons run against the real P and F (the
  acceptance check taps Punch, Lift and Deliver in a played game). At night R draws each town building's lit body
  (`.._lit`, same size and anchor) and the rodding turns at the points' end (`bld/rodding_z`, `bld/rodding_crank`);
  `goods/keg/nails` for kegs of nails. The carry readout reads F's places when handling is on (the cart's goods, the
  hands).
- **The camera** (the designer: "stay with our character centered as it moves"): `PIX.CAM` DEAD 0, AHEAD 0,
  AHEAD_CART 0, CAR false: the view is locked on him again (R's knobs kept for the designer to try); on a train it
  centres on P's pose (`pixPlayerPoint`). check-pixrender's centred walk (his feet on the view's middle pixel at every
  frame) is back; check-interiors checks he is centred inside a coach.
- **The evening, provisional** (plans/d27-choices.md, "Integrator"): the nine shops' storage rows in the east block by
  the east ramp, OUT in its lane-side row (`SHOPS27`, `STORE27_OUT`); the platform cart 4 x 3 cells (A2's art widened
  to 2.4u in `tools/pixelart/goods27.py`: `CART_W`, `CART_ROWS`; `CARGO27.CART_CELLS` 12, `CART_ROWS` 3) and small
  goods 3 high on it (`CARGO27.LAYERS.CART` 3, `GOODS27[k].layers`); trips to town between trains (`frDayFree`, F's
  `FR.TRIP_S`, `TRIP_GOOD_S`, `DAY_SWITCH_S`; the cart brought back after a day trip); the town's goods packed into
  as few boxcars as hold them, the one nearest the storage first (`frLoad`).
- **Fixes found by playing** (all in F's section, minimal): beside a cart on the loading edge the player stands on the
  cart's level (`frCartStand`: the wider cart reaches the edge, and a stand spot on the ground below sent him round by
  the ramp or stuck a job); a good taken off the cart is not put back on it while a train still has goods that fit
  (`S.fr.offCart`, and `atDoor` only while the train has goods for the cart): the hint looped take and put for ever.
- **The bot** (`tests/proto-bot.js`, extended; the D25 play unchanged with `?d27=0`): tickets on board as P wrote it;
  freight by F's `frJobHint` as each standing freight train's work in the plan; the evening by the hints, then End the
  day; goods put away and the cart parked before a switch or a send-off that is due (a switch not yet due lets the
  handling go on); new work started only with time in hand (the walk there, 20 minutes, the walk on); lessons answered
  Show me or Skip (`mem.lessons` 'mixed': day 1 shows switches, tickets and unloading and skips the rest, day 2 replays
  them from Settings and skips each).
- **Measured** (check-d27-day, seed 1866): day 1 (Monday, the plough) every run in and out, worst 151 minutes late
  (f2 out; f1 +28, p5 +126, f3 +150), 34 travellers seated and punched, 15 goods unloaded and stored, 6 loaded, none
  delivered: the evening began at 22:45; day 2 worst 26 minutes late, 31 punched, 14 unloaded, 1 delivered (the
  evening from 20:47 spent loading two days' goods). **The evening needs 4 to 5 game hours for one day's goods**
  (the cart to the street about 35 minutes each way, about 3 minutes a good at the shops and 2.5 at the storage): a
  design decision is needed (plans/d27-choices.md, the first section). Frames: unthrottled 0.6 to 1.6 ms in the
  played moments (check-d27-day), check-pixperf (4x throttled) 4.4 to 6.6 ms average, 14 ms worst at scale 1.
- **Checks:** `check-d27-day.js` (new: two days by the bot, every hour's itemAudit, the summary, the hashes twice and
  after a reload mid-handling and mid-aisle, the buttons, the screenshots `d27-{morning,lesson,punch,unload,cargo,
  path,deliver,street,night,summary}-{portrait,landscape}.png`), `check-atlas.js` (new: the budget, the keys the code
  needs, and two days' saves drawn with every lookup watched). Older checks: check-prototrains opens with `?d27=0`
  (it plays the D25 line and goods); check-protogoods compares the summary with what was handed in by 23:00 (the D25
  handcart's second trip can run past it on the D27 layout); check-handling's car is the first boxcar with goods
  (packing) and its day counts goods stored or handed in on a day trip. `tests/dev-eval.js` runs a script file in the
  page (a developer's tool, not a check).


### The lead's evening decision (2026-11-11, after the integration)

Provisional, every old value switchable (plans/d27-choices.md, "Decided provisionally by the lead"):
- **The keeper carries** (`FR.KEEPER`): Deliver at a shop parks the cart and each good is one step `{ a: 'keeper' }`
  (the keeper walks door > cart, lifts it, carries it to his door, hands it in: used up 'business' from the cart);
  Pick up is `{ a: 'keeperLoad' }` (pickup spot > cart, into `frCartFree`'s place). `frKeeperState` gives his place in
  the step; `frKeeperDraw` (W's entry shape, his own entry in W's list left out by R's `pixTfList`) draws him walking
  with the good in his arms (the good's place 'moving' at his hands in `frDrawList`); the player waits by the cart.
- **Times** `FR.TIME_K` 0.6 of F's (`FR_TIMES_F`, `frTimes`; whole 8 s ticks, a lift at least 16 s).
- **The plough collected** by the feed store 20 minutes after f1 stands (`FR.COLLECT`, `FR.COLLECT_S`; frTick; not
  counted in `frAboard`, so not the player's to unload; itemAudit allows it).
- **The cart 5 x 4 cells** (`CARGO27.CART_COLS` 5, `CART_ROWS` 4, 20 cells; A2's art 4.2 x 3.2u in goods27.py;
  `FR.PUSH_D` 2.9, `FR.CART_EDGE_Z` 14.7; R's sort box follows it); candidates row by row; the long goods loaded
  first, the nearest along the lane; goods for the railroad never on a town good (`frCartFree`).
- **Pickups at the same stop** once the shop's deliveries are done and storage is empty; **the cart stays in town
  overnight** with the pickups (`FR.CART_NIGHT`); **the day ends at 23:59** (`PD.LATE_S`).
- Day trips keep going once loaded (`frDayFree(S, loaded)`, `FR.DAY_TRIPS`); `FR.KEEP_LAST` (the last freight train's
  goods left on the cart for the evening) is built but off: it held f3 up.
- **Measured:** the evening for a whole Monday's goods from the storage 3 h 23 min (8 h 25 before); the bot's two days
  still run late from f2 on (day 1 worst 113 minutes, day 2 89) and the evening does not finish: check-d27-day fails on
  the deliveries, pickups, day 2's loading and the day's end. The bot's pbPunch is no longer resent while he walks the
  aisle. check-protogoods is back to F's version (the D25 handcart fits before 23:59).

### Half the goods and a re-timed day (2026-11-11, the lead's second decision)

- `SHOP_GOODS27` is now `SHOP_GOODS27_HALF` (8 in and 4 out a day; every kind within the week); `?goods27=full` gives
  `SHOP_GOODS27_FULL`, which check-handling and check-delivery use (they count its exact numbers).
- `PROTO_SCHEDULE` is `PROTO_SCHEDULE_I` (freight standing times from the measured handling plus about 20 minutes, the
  passenger trains in the gaps, 50 minutes between a train leaving towards an end and one coming in from it, p5 from
  the east); `PROTO_SCHEDULE_T` (T's) with `?d27=0` (the D25 flows' checks) or `?sched27=t`.
- The cart stops in the middle of the street (`FR.CART_MID`); loading for town from one place by the east block (8u);
  `FR.KEEP_LAST` (preloading the cart in the afternoon) tried and left off: it made p4 and p5 40 minutes late.
- check-d27world expects 8 goods a day.
- **Measured** (check-d27-day): day 1 worst 19 minutes; day 2 worst 82 (the cart fetched from the end of the street
  first); the evenings do not finish (day 1: 6 of 9 delivered, no pickups). check-d27-day fails on the evening.


### No freight train in the evening (2026-11-11, the lead's third decision)

- `FR.CART_NIGHT` off: the cart comes back each evening and the pickups are stacked in the OUT rows.
- `PROTO_SCHEDULE_I` now has eight runs (p5 left out; f3 and p4 west to east; the last freight train leaves at 17:40,
  p4 at 18:45); check-d27world expects 8.
- `FR.KEEPER_U` 4.0; `FR.NEAR` 10 (the stop in the middle of the street serves both shops); pickups at the same stop
  whatever is left in storage; the cart parked in the middle of the block's lane to load it; preload once the last
  freight train has gone.
- **Measured** (check-d27-day passes): worst lateness 9 minutes in, 21 out; everything delivered, picked up and stacked;
  the bot ends the day at 23:15 and 23:26.
### Polish after the designer's play (2026-11-11)

The designer's four requests from playing the published build; small edits in "starting world layout", the pixel
renderer, "HUD and panels" (Settings) and "start".
- **Smooth camera** ("very hitchy ... if I walk exactly right or left it's smooth. Any deflection in that angle and it
  judders"). Measured in Chromium at phone size (390 x 844, scale 3 at 2 device px a CSS px: a native pixel is 1.5 CSS
  px), walking at 1x in six directions, on this build and on the published one (900d660): **two causes.** (1) The view
  moved in whole native pixels: walking at 3u/s the camera moves 0.4 to 0.9 native px a frame, so it stood still on
  about 55 of 135 frames and jumped 1.5 CSS px on the others; walking off the screen's horizontal, x and y stepped on
  different frames (a stair-step, steps of 2.1 CSS px; up to 3 on the published build), about 1.3 CSS px off a straight
  glide. Walking exactly across the screen only x steps, which reads as smooth. (2) Ground chunks were made in the
  frame that first showed them: on a long walk 2 or 3 in one frame; with the CPU throttled 4x (a phone) a chunk took up
  to 47 ms, frames up to 114 ms (98th percentile 12.8 ms), a jolt each time a row of chunks came into view.
  **The fix:** the camera moves continuously. The buffer is one native pixel bigger each way (`PIX.vw`, `vh`: the
  screen in native px; `nw`, `nh`: the buffer); the art is still drawn on whole native pixels at `PIX.vx`, `vy`, and
  the canvas is shifted by the rest with a CSS transform in whole device pixels (`pixCamSet`; `PIX.camX`, `camY` = the
  view's exact top-left, which `pixToScreen` / `pixToNative` and the view getter's `camX`, `camY` use). Locked on him,
  his feet's pixel is within half a native pixel of the screen's middle (he steps by a native pixel against a gliding
  world, as his sprite always did). The ground: the chunks within `PIX.CH_AHEAD` (160) native px round the view, twice
  that on the side the view is moving to, are made ahead one at a time in idle time (`pixChunkAhead`:
  requestIdleCallback, or a timer after the frame), and the cache keeps that ring; chunks are 128 px at scale 3 and up
  (`pixChunkSize`: at most about 5 ms each on a 4x-throttled CPU), 256 px at scale 1 and 2 (128 px cost about 1 ms more a
  frame there in copies); the in-frame budget `PIX.CH_MS` is 4 (a jump to a new place still makes one at once).
  `PIX.chunkStats.inFrame`, `inFrameMax`, `ahead` count them.
  **Measured after:** the camera moves every frame (0 still frames while walking but for a few at 0.3 native px a
  frame straight down, under one device pixel), largest step 1.0 to 1.1 CSS px, within one device pixel of a straight
  glide in every direction. A tour of the map at 12u/s with 4x throttling: 0 chunks made in a frame (440 ahead), frames
  98th percentile 6.5 ms (12.8 before), longest gap between frames 77 ms (144 before; the headless browser's own jitter
  is about 50). check-pixperf (4x throttled) within its budgets as before (station 5.4 ms portrait, 6.9 at scale 1).
  check-pixrender section 8 (new): five walking directions at 1x, the view's step each frame within 1.5 device px of an
  even glide and never still while he walks, at most one chunk made in a frame (measured: 0.6 to 1.0 device px, 0
  still frames, 0 chunks in frames; with the old whole-pixel camera put back it fails: 1.7 to 3.8 device px, up to 53
  still frames). The centred checks (check-pixrender, check-interiors) allow the half native pixel; check-protoworld's
  canvas size the extra native pixel.
- **Platform ends** ("add more wood to the right side of the platform where the bridge and step down to the switch
  is"): platform 1's planks reach the lever frames' stages at both ends, `PROTO.P1_DECK` x -31.5 to 31.5 (P1 itself,
  the 60u the trains, travellers and checks use, is unchanged). `PROTO_DECKS[0]` (walking height 0.375) and the drawn
  deck use it; a side that is not a whole number of units ends with a cell laid back from its far end. The stage (at
  ground level) stays a step down from the deck, as the designer described it. Screenshots
  `test-output/polish-{before,after}-{east,west}-{portrait,landscape}.png`.
- **Depot set back** ("a few notches back from the platform for visibility but add extra wood too"): `PROTO.STATION`
  2.5u back (centre z -24.3, front -19.8, back -28.8; the door spot -19.0), the gap planked: `PROTO.APRON` (x -10 to 10,
  z -19.8 to platform 1's back edge, deck height, its +x end edged) in `PROTO_DECKS` and the drawn deck. The canopy,
  benches and the schedule board (TRAINS) stay with the platform; the townsfolk's `door_station` node follows the door
  (`STATION.door.z + 0.5`, was -16.0); the depot square and the street's end are unchanged (the depot covers 1.8u of
  the square's dirt and 0.8u of the street's end behind it; the paths round it, `sq_*`, `sta_*`, `street_s`, stay
  clear). `proto-layout.json` re-exported (`p1Deck`, `apron`, the new `station`). Screenshots
  `test-output/polish-{before,after}-station{,-wide}-{portrait,landscape}.png`.
- **Start over** (Settings, "a button in the settings to reset the save file"): asks "This deletes your saved game.
  Start over?" (Start over / Cancel), then `saveDeleteAll` stops saving, waits for a save in progress, deletes this
  world's saves (IndexedDB 'auto' and 'world-<PAGE_WORLD>', localStorage 'railhead-quicksave' and
  'railhead-quicksave-<PAGE_WORLD>') and reloads into a new game; other worlds' saves and the Settings choices stay.
  check-save's new part (`checkStartOver`; screenshot `polish-reset-confirm-portrait.png`).
- **Checks run** (one by one): check-pixrender, check-interiors, check-pixperf, check-d27world, check-save, check-levers,
  check-townsfolk, check-protoworld pass; check-d27-day fails as before on the evening (the integrator's work) with the
  same figures and the same end hash (c4cf39d5) as on 549993e.

### D29: one lever frame (2026-11-12)

The designer: "make all switches in the same place even if not realistic", then "consolidated but still near the
track, not on the main platform". All four levers are in **one frame, C**, at ground level just off platform 1's
**west end** beside track 1, where D27's west frame stood: the stage 6 x 2u from x -37.5 to -31.5 (against the end of
platform 1's planks, `P1_DECK`, a step down), z -12 to -10; the levers W1 W3 E1 E3 (west to east) at z -11, x -36.3 to
-32.7, 1.2u apart; the agent pulls from behind them (z -12.4), reached from the platform without crossing a track.
Why the west end: the east end's strip between the planks (x 31.5) and the cart path to the crossing (from x 35.25) is
under 4u, too short for the 6u stage. (A first try at the lead's provisional spot, platform 1's back edge near x 0 on
the depot's apron, was hidden by the canopy roof from the default view; the designer then moved it to the track.)
- **Data** ("starting world layout"): `PROTO.LEVERS_ONE` (frame C) and `PROTO.LEVERS_TWO` (D27's W and E, kept);
  `PROTO.LEVER_FRAMES` ('one'; `?levers=two` on the page brings D27's two frames back) picks `PROTO.LEVERS`, which
  now has `FRAMES` (the frames' ids), each frame's `y` and `EXT` (the iron frame's walk-grid block). `PROTO_LEVERS`
  numbers the levers 1 to 4 in a frame and gives each its `y`; `frame` is 'C' in rrSwitchNeeds' rows. The stands,
  `switchSpots`, the Throw button, the Next job line and the arrow follow the levers unchanged. TOWN27's tufts of grass
  stay at D27's frame spots (the town's random stream unchanged).
- **Saves:** `protoLeversRealign` (end of `migrate`, no schema step) puts a prototype save's switch stands at the
  levers in use. check-save's fixtures: save-v18 loads.v19 and save-v19's new `loads.v19` record the load hashes with
  the one frame (876f032b, 2d9b6b6d); with `?levers=two` they load with the old ones (a7a86889, f89372ef), so the
  stands are the only change. check-save reads a current-version sample's `loads` entry first when there is one.
- **Art:** `bld/leverframe/C` (station27.py `leverframe4`, registered in town27_reg.py): the D27 frame's pieces, longer
  (stage 6 x 2u, four quadrant racks, one number board W1 W3 E1 E3, the lamp); `extra.levers`, `extra.use`. Atlas
  rebuilt and embedded (612 KB); `proto-layout.json` re-exported.
- **Drawing** (pixel renderer): a frame stands at its `y`; levers at their lever's `y`. The rodding (`pixFlatList`):
  W1's and W3's runs straight out west on the track side of the frame (W1's nearer the track, as it turns first);
  E1's and E3's north beside the end of the planks (`bld/rodding_z`, a crank), then east along platform 1's back edge
  just behind the deck (over the depot's apron at deck height), about 95u, to cranks at their points (E1's nearer the
  platform; E3's turn north is west of E1's, so no runs cross). The east runs cross the cart path at x 37 (as D27's did).
- **Lessons:** the switches lesson says "The levers are in the frame at the west end of the platform" (the one frame's
  end, `pdLesFrameEnd`), its picture is `bld/leverframe/C`.
- **Walks** (check-d27world, the walk grid): platform 1's middle to W1 35.9u (D27: 33.2 to the west frame), to E1
  33.5u (D27: 32.0 to the east frame), the schedule board to E3 46.4u (D27: 19.5); no walk between frames any more
  (D27: 66u, 22 minutes). Limits for the one frame: 1.25 x the straight line + 2u (D27's figures with `?levers=two`).
- **Lateness:** check-levers' D25 bot (`?d27=0`, T's day) worst 4 minutes over two days (D27: 13). check-d27-day's
  D27 bot: arrivals worst 22 minutes (f3 on day 2; D27: 9), departures up to 34 (p4 out on day 2; D27: 21); f2 +8/+20,
  p3 +15 out, p4 +22/+34 out: the east switches are now a 46u walk from the board. Nothing re-timed (the freight and
  punching speed-ups being built in parallel move these figures too).
- **Checks** (each alone): check-levers (the frames from the data: one frame, its place off an end of platform 1,
  clear of track 1, the footbridges, the crossing and the cart path, use spots on the ground; screenshots
  `d29-levers-{portrait,landscape}.png`), check-d27world, check-protoworld (the east legs end at the one frame,
  promised from the straight line), check-lessons, check-d27-day, check-proto-stall, check-pixrender, check-save.
## D29 (smoother, faster and clearer handling and punching)

### Handling and punching (builder, 2026-11-12)

The designer's review (D29 rows 1 and 2): freight handling "faster" and "much smoother and more clear"; punching
"faster" and "smoother too, it's very hitchy when in the train". The one lever frame (row 3) is another helper's.

**Measured first** (`tests/smooth-play.js`: plays a sequence in real time at 1x in Chromium at 390 x 844 and 844 x 390,
with and without 4x CPU throttling, and records every drawn frame: the agent's drawn sprite, the camera's exact
top-left, the good in his hands, the cart, frame times, the sim's state; `node tests/smooth-play.js coach|freight
portrait|landscape [throttle]`, and `... gaps` for the sim-only idle ticks). On D27's tip (84ca95b):
- **In a coach the agent and the camera moved once a tick.** On a train the agent is drawn at P's `pbPlayerPose`, the
  sim's point, which changes once a tick (8 game seconds, 8 frames at 1x), and the camera (locked on him) centred on that
  same point: so the whole view jumped 0.4u (up to 10.7 native px, 16 CSS px) every 8th frame and stood still on the 7
  between (403 of 482 walking frames still, 117 ms stands, 54 jumps over 2 px in one punch sequence; the same in
  landscape and with 4x throttling). Outside the train the player was always drawn between ticks (`prevPlayer`), which is
  why it was "hitchy when in the train" only. The travellers walking the aisle did the same (P's list, also per tick).
- **Idle ticks inside a job.** The walk to a row and its punch: a tick to arrive, a tick with nothing (the punch "starts"
  at acc 0), then the punch: 7 standing ticks in a five-ticket sequence (of 91). Freight: the action after a walk started
  a tick after he arrived (the walk's end is only seen by the next tick's step): 9 standing ticks in 300 (one before
  every lift and every set-down after a walk), while the walk after an action moved in the action's own last tick.
- **Handling progress in steps.** The good being lifted or set down was placed by the sim's progress (0, 0.5, 1 for a
  16 s step), so it jumped 5 to 11 native px against him (17 jumps in 300 ticks), and the lift frames jumped likewise;
  the frame after the last never showed (the walk had begun). The cart and the good in the hands were at the sim's point
  while he was drawn between ticks (up to 4 px of jitter against him while pushing).
- Not causes: animation cycles (the walk cycle runs on game time, `pixCycle`, and does not restart; the stalls made it
  flip walk/idle), the cut-away coach's redraw and sort (0.7 to 0.9 ms a frame unthrottled, 3.2 to 4.2 ms at 4x, as the
  rest of the scene), pbPunch resent (a repeat carries on the punch in hand), the aisle's walk speed (the same as
  outside, 3u a game minute; only the end steps are quicker, 2.6u in 16 s), the camera on entering or leaving a car
  (D28 left it locked on him, `PIX.CAM.CAR` false; the only jump was 7 px of height as he stepped off, fixed below).

**Built:**
- **Drawing between ticks** ("pixel renderer", block "D29: smooth drawing"): `doTick` keeps the lists of the tick
  before (`pixSnapPrev`, only for the frame's last tick), `pixLerpLists` blends them with this tick's by the frame's
  alpha, as the player's own point always was: P's travellers and pose (the agent on a train, from where he stood a tick
  ago, so stepping in and off is continuous too), W's townsfolk, F's goods by id, the cart and the hand truck, the
  handling pose's progress and the punch's (in the tick an action ends, its last part is drawn on to the end). A jump of
  more than `PIX.LERP_MAX` (3u) is not blended. The camera follows the blended point (`pixPlayerPoint` reads the frame's
  lists), so in a coach it glides as outside. A good lifted or set down goes between its two ends (F's `frDrawList` now
  gives `from` and `to` for a 'moving' good) along a small arc, eased, into the agent's hands (`PIX.handsAt`, the carry
  frame's hands) and with the in-hands sprite on the half nearer them, so it meets the good he then holds
  (`pixGoodsItem`; inside the car he is in it is drawn with the car's insides). Drawing only: the sim and its state are
  untouched (`PIX.SMOOTH = false` draws the ticks as before).
- **No idle ticks in a job** (sim; `PB.CHAIN`, `FR.CHAIN`, false gives D27's back): the punch starts in the tick he
  reaches the row (at once, counting that tick, when he is already there); a handling job's walk that arrived goes on
  to its next step in the same tick (`frAfterWalk`, called after the ordinary walk in `rideActorTick`), and the walk after
  an action waits for the tick the action used (`frPlayerTick`'s `spent`), so each step has its own time.
- **Faster:** `PB.PUNCH_S` 16 (was 30: two ticks; `PB_D27` keeps 30); `FR.TIME_K` 0.4 (was 0.6), each step at least
  `FR.MIN_S` one tick (with the blend a one-tick step plays over about 8 frames at 1x), lifts no longer held to two
  (`FR.PICK_MIN` 8, was 16): door 16 s (24), carry 16 (16), shoulder 16 (24), roll, drag and truck 8 (16), heave 24
  (40), handing in 8 (16), the handle 8 (8). `frUseD27()` puts D27's handling back (`FR_D27`). The carry, the most
  common, stays two ticks (F's 32 s times 0.4 rounds to 16); the chained steps are what makes it quicker to play.
- **Clearer:** a progress cue over the agent while a timed step runs (a 13 x 4 px bar, ink outline, ochre filling,
  paper when full: handling actions and the punch); the target of the job marked while he walks there (F's new
  `frTarget(S)`: the cell, cart cell, car spot, shop door or car door, a diamond the good's footprint in size, blinking
  ochre and red with an ink shadow); the Next job line names the step in hand (F's `frStepLabel(S)` through
  `frJobHint`'s busy text: "Going for the crate", "Lifting the crate", "Carrying the crate to its storage row", "Setting
  the barrel on the cart", "Opening the door", "Pushing the cart into place"); punching: the next traveller to punch is
  outlined (ochre; paper while his punch runs: `pixOutline`), he holds his ticket out from the moment the punch is his
  (P's sitTicket, as before), a flash on the ticket when it is punched (a small star of light, 0.4 s) and the punched
  mark as before. `PIX.CUE = false` turns the cue, the target and the outline off. (Seen in the screenshots: U's buttons
  over the world and its helper arrow sit over the agent and the next traveller in a coach, so there they cover the cue
  and the outline at times; the close-ups hide the buttons. For U and the lead: the buttons could sit a row higher.)
- **A fix on the way:** while handling, the agent was turned by F's pose's `f`, which is its frame number, not its
  facing (`pixDirOf(0)` is sw, of 1 or 2 se), so during an action he faced sw or se whatever the good's side and turned
  between them mid-step; he now faces `frPose.dir` (the sim's facing, towards the good).
- **`?d29=0`** puts D27's back for comparison (the times, the chaining, the drawing between ticks and the cues; the
  facing fix stays).

**After** (same sequences; the coach one punches 4 tickets now, it was 5: with the quicker punch the player steps off
before the fifth traveller has sat down):
- Coach (step in, walk the aisle, punch, step off), portrait 1x: the agent's largest step in a frame 10.05 > 2.24 native
  px (the 2.24 is a whole-pixel rounding on the end steps, the quickest move), steps over 2 px 54 > 1, the longest stand
  on screen while walking 117 > 83 ms, the camera's largest step 10.67 > 1.37 px (98th percentile 3.73 > 1.33); landscape
  the same (10.51 > 1.52); with 4x throttling 10.05 > 3.16 and 2.24, the camera 10.67 > 2.69 and 1.52 (frames of 33 to
  67 ms carry two frames' walk). The sequence took 91 ticks for 5 tickets, now 51 for 4 (a punch at the row 16 game s,
  was 30 plus two standing ticks; per ticket in ticks, the first including the walk in and the climb: 19, 26, 6, 6, 26
  before; 16, 23, 2, 2 now).
- Freight (300 ticks of F's hints from the d27freight scenario): the agent was already smooth (1.41 px, 0 jumps, both
  before and after); the cart he pushes against him 4.12 > 1.41 px (2 at 4x); a good being lifted or set down stood a
  tick and then jumped 7 to 11 px (13 such jumps over 3 px), now it moves every frame, at most 3.61 px (4.12 at 4x) with
  0 jumps over 4; standing ticks inside a job 9 > 0, punching 7 > 0.
- Frame time (the pixel frame, `PIX.stats.ms`): the coach a little dearer, 0.74 > 1.1 to 1.6 ms on average unthrottled
  and 3.2 to 3.4 > 4.2 to 5.1 ms at 4x (98th percentile 7.4 to 7.6 > 10 to 12 ms): the travellers and the agent now move
  every frame, so the see-through pass redraws more often; freight unchanged (1.0 to 1.2 ms, 3.7 to 4.7 ms at 4x).
  check-pixperf's budgets (8 ms average, 14 ms at the 98th percentile, 4x) hold: station 4.4 ms portrait, 6.8 at scale 1.
- check-d27-day (the bot's two days, every D27 switch on): worst lateness in 9 minutes both before and after, out 21 >
  15 minutes; the bot ends the days at 22:57 and 23:02 (23:15 and 23:26 before); everything punched, unloaded, loaded,
  delivered and picked up as before; the two runs' hashes the same (54fb7170).

**Checks:** `tests/check-smooth.js` (new): both sequences in portrait and landscape at 1x, every frame: the agent's
drawn point never steps more than 1.6 native px plus 0.1 px a millisecond of the frame (the climb up a coach's end steps
is the fastest move), never stands still on screen more than 90 ms while the sim walks him, the camera the same bound,
the good in his hands and the cart he pushes within 2 px of him frame to frame; sim only, no tick inside a job with
nothing advancing; a punch takes `PB.PUNCH_S` (read from the data), every handling time a whole number of ticks of at
least one; close-ups `test-output/d29-smooth-{coach-punch,coach-next,freight-lift,freight-carry}-close-{portrait,
landscape}.png` and as played `d29-smooth-*-{portrait,landscape}.png`. With `?d29=0` the same recording gives D27's 10
px steps, which its bound fails. `check-handling.js`: a one-tick step is sampled at its start (its middle exists only in the drawing), and the
first tick of a command is watched too (a step can now start in it).

## D30 (trains drawn smoothly)

### Trains (builder, 2026-11-12)

The designer: "Train animation is very juddery, let's smooth it out."

**Measured first** (`tests/train-play.js`, new: plays p1 in real time in Chromium at 390 x 844 or 844 x 390 and records
every drawn frame: each car's drawn sprite anchor and heading frame, the exact point the renderer blends it to
(projected, not rounded), the camera's exact top-left, the smoke puffs drawn, frame times, tick, alpha, the train's v and
whether the train is drawn between ticks; `node tests/train-play.js platform|walk|curveW|curveE portrait|landscape
[speed] [throttle]`, `DUMP=file.json` keeps the frames, `QUERY='&d30=0'` the old drawing). Setups: platform (the player
stands on platform 1: the train comes in through the W1 crossover, brakes, stops, stands, starts and leaves through E1),
walk (the player walks along platform 1 beside it), curveW and curveE (standing by each crossover's curves). Before is
D29's tip (`?d30=0` draws exactly as it did). Native px:
- **Not drawn at tick positions.** The trains were already blended between ticks (`prevPoses`, `curPoses`): every
  frame in which the sim moved the train was blended (0 frames at a tick position), steps at most 1.41 px at 1x,
  0 stalls, 0 jumps. Not a cause.
- **Each car rounded on its own, x and y apart: the main cause.** At a train's speeds (0.6 to 1.0 px a frame at 1x)
  a car stepped (1,0), (0,1), (1,1), (0,0) in turn, so its 2:1 stair went in and out of step with the rails' own: it
  sat -0.5, 0 or +0.5 px across its rails' line, bobbing a pixel up and down; and the cars rounded on different frames,
  so the gap between neighbours on a straight changed by a pixel in 1051 of 1734 frame pairs (platform, portrait 1x),
  1480 of 2299 (walk, landscape), 540 of 866 at 2x, 268 of 431 at 4x, 573 of 746 on E1's curve: the cars shuffled
  against each other.
- **Heading frames flipping on curves:** 3 flips A > B > A within 30 frames on E1's curve at 2x and at 4x (0 at 1x).
- Not causes: the camera (the sub-pixel camera shifts the rails and the train together; its largest step 3.35 px is
  the walk's look-ahead, the same before and after), the smoke (puffs drift at most 1.41 px a frame, 0 jumps; a small
  change kept, below), the acceleration (the speed changes per tick by at most 0.16 px a frame: braking from 0.95 to 0
  in 9 ticks, starting in 7), frame times (16.7 ms steady unthrottled; with 4x CPU throttling the 98th percentile 33 ms
  with or without D30).
- The helper before this one (WIP 83c20e8) had placed every car at an anchor car's rounding plus its own rounded
  offset, with hysteresis: the gaps held (6 changes), but on the curves cars stepped backwards against their motion
  (47 to 78 times in a run through E1), stood up to 100 ms and stepped unevenly (a 2.24 px step after 0.7 px of motion,
  up to 3.3 px off). Replaced.

**Built** ("pixel renderer", block "D30: trains drawn smoothly", `pixTrainDrawPoses`; drawing only, the sim and its
state untouched):
- **The whole train at one moment.** Each frame the train is drawn at one moment between the ticks, chosen so that
  its anchor (the vehicle heading e or w that lies straightest; else the one moving most across the screen) stands
  exactly on a whole step of the rails' 2:1 line ((2, 1) a step; else on a whole pixel). The moment moves only when
  the anchor reaches its next step, so all the train's vehicles step in the same frame; each vehicle at that moment is
  put on whole pixels on its own (heading e or w and lying straight, within `PIX.RAIL_DEG` 0.5 degrees: on its rails'
  2:1 line, `pixRailSnap`; else the nearest pixel). On a straight the vehicles' exact offsets are the same at every
  step, so the drawn gaps never change and every car stays on its rails' pixel line. The moment may lie a little past a
  tick's ends (at most half a tick, so a step near a tick's end is not lost), but never before the tick's start as a
  train starts off nor past its end as it comes to a stand. A new anchor's grid goes through where it was drawn last
  frame, so a change of anchor (a car entering a curve) never jumps.
- **On a curve each car on its own.** A car turned more than `PIX.TURN_DEG` (0.1 degree) from the anchor is drawn at
  the frame's own alpha and rounded on its own (a car on a curve cannot keep a whole-pixel gap anyway); between 0.05
  and 0.1 degree a mix. It never steps back against its motion (as it goes from the nearest pixel onto the rails' line):
  it waits where it was while that is within `PIX.BACK_PX` (2.5 px) of its exact point.
- **No heading flicker:** a car's heading frame is held until its yaw is `PIX.HDG_HOLD` (3) degrees past the half-way
  to the next frame.
- **Everything with the car on its pixel:** the car's world point is moved to its drawn pixel, so a cut-away car's
  insides and the name plate land with it; goods in or on a car (`pixCarGood`) and travellers in a cut-away coach are
  moved by the same shift (`PIX.carShift`), so they stay in their seats.
- **Smoke:** a puff is put where the stack was when it was let out (by the engine's drawn speed), not where it is now,
  and no burst of puffs as a standing engine starts off.
- **Off switch:** `PIX.SMOOTH_TRAINS = false` or `?d30=0` draws the trains as before.
- The cost: a few lerps and projections per car a frame; the pixel frame unchanged within noise (platform 1x 1.26 >
  1.02 ms average unthrottled; 4x throttled 4.2 > 4.4 ms platform, 4.3 > 4.0 ms walking beside it). check-pixperf's
  station 4.4 ms (portrait) and 6.3 to 6.5 ms at scale 1, within its 8 ms.

**After** (same setups; before > after):
- platform portrait 1x: gap changes on a straight 1051/1734 > 0/1732; off the rails line 0.5 > 0 px; heading flicker 0 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 1.41 > 2.24, evenness 0.82 > 0.71; frame 1.26 > 1.04 ms (98% 2.4 > 2.3); frame time 98% 16.8 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41
- walk landscape 1x: gap changes on a straight 1480/2299 > 0/2300; off the rails line 0.5 > 0 px; heading flicker 0 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 1.41 > 2.24, evenness 0.82 > 1.55; frame 1 > 0.9 ms (98% 1.9 > 1.7); frame time 98% 16.8 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41
- walk portrait 1x: gap changes on a straight 1081/1769 > 0/1765; off the rails line 0.5 > 0 px; heading flicker 0 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 1.41 > 2.24, evenness 0.82 > 0.71; frame 1.06 > 0.91 ms (98% 1.9 > 2); frame time 98% 16.8 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41

- curveW portrait 1x: gap changes on a straight 51/68 > 0/67; off the rails line 0.5 > 0 px; heading flicker 0 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 1.41 > 10.77, evenness 0.7 > 0.79; frame 0.83 > 0.72 ms (98% 1.3 > 1.1); frame time 98% 16.8 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41
