- curveE landscape 1x: gap changes on a straight 573/746 > 0/748; off the rails line 0 > 0 px; heading flicker 0 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 2.24 > 2.83, evenness 1.03 > 1.59; frame 0.61 > 0.64 ms (98% 1 > 1.1); frame time 98% 16.8 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41
- curveE portrait 1x: gap changes on a straight 629/814 > 0/813; off the rails line 0 > 0 px; heading flicker 0 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 2.24 > 2.83, evenness 1.03 > 1.59; frame 0.72 > 0.66 ms (98% 1.5 > 1.1); frame time 98% 16.8 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41
- platform portrait 2x: gap changes on a straight 540/866 > 0/865; off the rails line 0.5 > 0 px; heading flicker 0 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 2.24 > 2.24, evenness 1.05 > 1.59; frame 1.01 > 1.15 ms (98% 2 > 2.1); frame time 98% 16.8 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41
- platform portrait 4x: gap changes on a straight 268/431 > 0/432; off the rails line 0.5 > 0 px; heading flicker 0 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 4.47 > 4.47, evenness 0.9 > 2.1; frame 1.05 > 1.3 ms (98% 1.8 > 3); frame time 98% 16.8 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41
- curveE landscape 2x: gap changes on a straight 286/373 > 0/373; off the rails line 0 > 0 px; heading flicker 3 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 4.47 > 4.47, evenness 1.06 > 1.8; frame 0.59 > 0.68 ms (98% 0.9 > 1.1); frame time 98% 16.8 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41
- curveE landscape 4x: gap changes on a straight 144/186 > 0/187; off the rails line 0 > 0 px; heading flicker 3 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 8.06 > 8.94, evenness 1.1 > 2.24; frame 0.55 > 0.7 ms (98% 0.8 > 1.1); frame time 98% 16.8 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41
- platform portrait 1x, CPU 4x slower: gap changes on a straight 977/1638 > 0/1732; off the rails line 0.5 > 0 px; heading flicker 0 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 4.47 > 2.24, evenness 0.82 > 0.71; frame 4.22 > 2.94 ms (98% 8.9 > 6.4); frame time 98% 33.3 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41
- walk landscape 1x, CPU 4x slower: gap changes on a straight 1452/2237 > 0/2293; off the rails line 0.5 > 0 px; heading flicker 0 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 3.16 > 2.24, evenness 1.06 > 1.55; frame 4.27 > 2.95 ms (98% 9.5 > 6.4); frame time 98% 16.8 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41
- curveW portrait 1x, CPU 4x slower: gap changes on a straight 51/68 > 0/67; off the rails line 0.5 > 0 px; heading flicker 0 > 0; steps back 0 > 0; stalls 0 > 0; jumps 0 > 0; largest step 2 > 2.24, evenness 0.7 > 0.79; frame 2.78 > 2.17 ms (98% 5.2 > 4.1); frame time 98% 16.8 > 16.8 ms; smoke step 1.41 > 1.41

("Evenness": the largest difference between a car's drawn step and its exact motion since its last step; a rail step is
2.24 px. curveW 1x's 10.77 px step is one 233 ms frame of the test machine, the exact point moved as far. The pixel
frame's cost is within run-to-run noise; at 4x CPU throttling the D30 runs happened to be faster.)

A judgement call: a car on a straight now moves in whole steps of the rails, (2, 1), every 2 to 3 frames at 1x, where it
moved a pixel nearly every frame before; drawn at most 2.2 px from its exact point (0.7 before). That is the only way
to keep its 2:1 edge in step with the rails' and the cars' spacing fixed; the steps are as even as the speed allows (each
within 0.8 of a step of the exact motion since the last, at 1x). Not changed, for the lead: when the player rides a
moving train the camera follows his blended point, so the car he is in steps against it by up to a step (as each car
did by a pixel before); following the car's drawn point instead would make the world step. A camera decision.

**Checks:** `tests/check-smooth.js` has a train part (`checkTrains`; `SMOOTH_ONLY=train` runs only it): platform 1x
portrait, walk 1x landscape, curveW 1x portrait, curveE 2x landscape, every frame: the train blended whenever the sim
moves it; no car's drawn step more than its exact step plus one rail step (2.3 px); no car standing still while drawn
more than a step behind its exact point; no step backwards against its motion; even steps (each within 1.8 px of the
exact motion since the car's last step, 2.3 at 2x); on a straight the gap between neighbours never changes (at most 1
in 100 frames) and every car heading e or w on track 1 on its rails' pixel line; no heading flicker (A > B > A within 30
frames); smoke puffs at most 2 px a frame; and with `?d30=0` the gaps change again (the bound bites). Screenshots
`test-output/d30-train-{platform,walk,curveW,curveE}-{portrait,landscape}.png`.

## D32 (line building)

Plan: `plans/d32-tech.md` (the lead's decisions at its end override its body) and `plans/d32-journey.md` (the designer's journey along the line). Each owner writes its own part below.

### The foundation (2026-11-13)

**What it is.** A second place inside the prototype's one game: the country a new line is built through, 2,520 m (3,600u) from the junction to the far town. It is **off unless dev mode turns it on**: a new game has no `S.dv`, `S.ln` or `S.cw` at all, so with the line off nothing in the sim or the hashes changes (the schema stays 19, no fixture was re-recorded; `save-v19.json` loads to its recorded hash). The one thing that changes for everyone is the slower clock (below).

**How to turn it on.** Settings, the row "Dev mode" (kept in the browser under `railhead-dev`, not in the save), or `?dev=1` on the page. A "Dev" button then opens the panel: Rail crew 0 to 30, the three kinds of hired worker (greyed: Town's), "Ride out as a rail worker" (greyed: Play's), "Look at the line" / "Back to the station" (Foundation's stand-in for riding out: it switches the view only), "Skip to 05:00 tomorrow", and a status block (day of the line, metres spiked, each front, supplies, the work train, men by stage, the head count). Every choice is a command (`dvSet { k, v }`, `dvSkip`), so it is saved and replayable. Sizes take effect at the next 05:00; a crew chosen before 06:20 on a day the line is not yet on starts at once. Other owners add buttons with `dvAddRow(id, label, onTap, state)`; when Play adds one, Foundation's two stand-in rows go.

**URL parameters.** `?dev=1` (dev mode on), `?clock=7.5` (the old clock rate), `?pk=1` (the player's old pace and action times).

**The line (section "the line (D32)").** Its own frame: x along the line from the junction (1 m = `PROTO.M` u), z across (track at z 0), flat. No track graph: the track is six fronts in metres (`S.ln.f`: cleared, bed, rock, ties, rails, spiked), each kept `LINE.GAP` behind the one ahead, the clearers at most 60 m ahead of the diggers. `lnWork(S, stage, wu)` is the only way a front moves (it spends work at `lnCost`, stops at the gap, the supplies and the end, takes supplies, hands back what it could not use); `lnOpenWork`, `lnSpot`, `lnFronts`, `lnHours`, `lnTrain`, `lnOn`, `lnSetView` are the plan's 11.4 signatures. Supplies (`S.ln.sup`) are topped up when the work train reaches the railhead (the first day a double load so the dump opens full). Hours: work 07:00 to 12:00 and 13:00 to 18:00, every day (no Sunday rest). `LN_TEST` (`__railhead.ln`) exposes it all to the checks.

**The country: the journey.** `LINE.SEGS` rows are `[from m, to m, cost kind, region]`. The nine regions of the designer's journey lie over the plan's metres of each cost kind, so the work total is exactly the plan's 192,800 wu: home prairie 250 m, creek bottoms 300 (the trestle at 536 to 552), the hills and the cut 350 (the cut 745 to 825), the big woods 400, the rocks 200, the river run 400, the canyon 150 (dug as rock), the pass 200 (as woods), the far valley 210. The lengths are provisional (the canyon is short because it shares the plan's 350 m of rock with the rocks; lengthening it means changing the cost table's totals). The same country in every game; the seed moves only the scatter. Nothing is stored: `lnTile(seed, i, j)` gives the ground tile of any cell, `lnScatter(seed, ci, cj)` the standing things of any 8u cell (thick: grass tufts, flowers, stones, bushes, stumps, the odd skull and prairie dog mound, trails as tiles), from whole-number hashes, never the game's random stream.

**The natural borders (superseded 2026-11-13 by "The wild country" below: the borders are now straight, 9.5u north and 11u south, and the art is the wilds artists' own layout; what follows is the Foundation's first version).** The corridor is narrow: the far (north) border lies 8.5 to 11.5u from the line, the near (south) one 11 to 14u (room for the right of way of 5u each side, the gangs, the carriers and the dump beside the cars at z 6), wandering along the line (`lnBorder`). `lnWalkable(seed, x, z, f2)` is the collision for Play and Crew: inside both borders, between the strip's ends, not in water (the creek only on the trestle's deck once the bed's front has passed). At both ends the borders close in to a wooded cutting the width of the roadbed. Beyond the borders and the ends the country goes on for ever (drawn, never walked), so no edge of the world exists to be seen. Each region's border is a list of belt rows in `LINE.REGIONS` (`far`: tall walls with ranks behind; `near`: low things, tall ones at least 9u back; `marks`: landmarks at fixed metres; `water`: the creek alongside, the river, the canyon's river below the rim, the mill stream). **Each belt thing is asked for by the wilds artists' key** (`wild/far/<region>/<row>/<1..3>`, `wild/near/...`, `wild/mark/<name>`, ground `tile/wild/<region>/<1..4>`) **and carries a batch-one stand-in (`alt`)** drawn until that key exists. The row names after the region are Foundation's guesses: when the artists' keys land, match the rows of `LINE.REGIONS` to them (that one table; nothing else). Landmarks with no stand-in (the trapper's cabin, the waterfall, the water tower) are not drawn yet. `proto-layout.json` (v 3, block `d32`) lists every row for the artists.

**The work train (section "the line (D32)", `wt*`).** DEVIATION from the plan's body, by its own fallback (section 12): it is a **scripted run, not a railroad (D24) train**. A third kind of run inside `S.rr.runs` touches about twenty places that read "not passenger" as "freight" (the freight handling, the job buttons, the board, the bots), in code another builder was changing. So `wtTick` moves it by the clock: at the station on track 2 from 05:50 (crew car x -40 to -27, engine 3 to 21), away east 06:20, at the railhead about 06:45 to 06:55, standing behind the spiked front and moving up in hops, away 18:20, backing in from the east 19:05, standing 19:15 to 19:35, backing away west. No block, lever, board row or railroad run changes (check-worktrain compares every railroad train event with the line off). Because the railroad's trains do not see it, it keeps out of their way itself: in the evening it waits for a clear road (`wtRoadClear`; its times shift by the wait), and a train for its side cannot be waved off while it moves (`wtBlocks`, a refusal with the reason). Known limits: the player can walk through it at the station (it is not stamped on the walk grid); a train stuck coming in at the east switch keeps it waiting up to three hours; with `?sched27=t` (the D25 flows' day) f3 is at the station in the evening and may be passed through. `PROTO_SCHEDULE_WORK` holds its two visits for the board and the export. When the clock jumps (the next morning, the dev skip) `lnSkipTo` plays the line's own ticks through the gap, so the evening's work is not lost.

**Stand-ins for the other owners** (each in its own section, to be replaced whole): Crew (`cwTick` spends 0.8 wu a man a working minute, shared by the work open to each stage, no men simulated or drawn; `cwDrawList`, `cpItems`, `cwGang` empty; `hcCount` counts the rail men by number, home once the train is back; `cwSimDays` plays the line alone), Play (`lpTick`, `lpCmd`, `lpTap`, `lpPose`, `lpHud`: nothing; with no player on the line the view follows a look point moved by a one-finger drag and the bar's buttons), Town (`hwTick`, `hwDrawList`, `tn*`: nothing). Hooks already placed for them: the tick order (`wtTick`, `cwTick`, `awLeverTick`, `hwTick`, `tnTick` after `tfTick`; `lpTick` after `pbTick`), the commands (`lpRide`, `lpBack`, `lpWalk`, `lpJoin`, `lpLeave`, `lgDone` to `lpCmd`), the save shape checks, the renderer's lists (the station's townsfolk list takes `cwDrawList(S, 'station')` and `hwDrawList(S)`; the camp's `cpItems`; on the line `cwDrawList(S, 'line')` and `lpPose`, drawn between ticks), the input (`lpTap(x, z, hit)` when the line is in view), the page class `line`, the summary's two lines (head count, away), the place kind `CREW { who }` for goods.

**Away.** `S.player.away = 'line'` (Play sets it) makes `applyCommand` refuse the station player's commands (`awRefuse`), and a relief man throws each lever a train needs 10 game minutes after it is needed (`awLeverTick`): measured, the first train comes in 10 minutes late.

**The clock.** `WORLDS.proto.clock = 5` ticks a real second (was 7.5; `clockRate(T)`; other worlds unchanged): a game minute is 1.5 real seconds, a day 36 minutes. A tick is still 8 game seconds, so the sim and the timetable are untouched. The player keeps his real speed: `PROTO.PLAYER_K = 1.5` multiplies his walk and tool pace (`playerWalkSpeed`). His action times are divided by 1.5 in whole ticks but never below D29's two ticks, so in practice only a heave (24 to 16 s) and a lever throw (4 ticks to 3) shorten; a lift, a put and a punch stay two ticks, which is 0.4 real seconds where it was 0.27 (the tick is the limit). Because a tick's walk is now longer than a walk grid cell, the player's tick of walking is taken in sub-steps of at most 0.41u (`PLAYER_SUB_U`; at the old pace one step, as before).

**Drawing (block "The line's view (D32)" at the end of the pixel renderer).** `pixFrame` hands the frame to `pixLnFrame` while `S.ln.view === 'line'`. Ground in chunks with their own cache (`PIX.lnChunks`): tiles, the roadbed up to the fronts (the artist's cleared, bed and rock tiles with their edges, ties and rails per pixel, rusty until spiked, the trestle's deck and bents), then every small thing, every border belt and everything beyond the borders baked in. A chunk is redrawn when the fronts within its stretch have moved (at most one a frame). A chunk in view is always made at once, so no blank ground is ever shown. Only the tall things inside the corridor, the work train, the supply piles, the men and the player are sorted items. At the station the work train, the plank walk and the camp are added by `pixLnStationItems`. Measured in check-linerender: the big woods at scale 1 with the train and 30 stand-in men, 69 to 125 sorted things, 0.5 to 0.7 ms a frame (budget 8); a chunk about 2 ms (the first of a kind up to 30 ms).

**The atlas.** `build.py` now writes the index packed (format v 2: keys front-coded, numbers as base-64 digits, each different `extra` once); the block unpacks itself when the page loads, so the page sees the same `PIX_ATLAS`. With the artist's batch one merged it is 628 KB (it would be 767 unpacked). check-atlas measures the block as written; its budget is 1,000 KB (the designer lifted the 700).

**Provisional choices (Foundation).** The region lengths above; the corridor's widths; the muster at x -46 to -40, z -16 to -10 (between the water tower and the lever frame; the plan's place is where D29 put the lever frame) and the plank walk at x -39.3; the camp at the plan's rectangle (checked clear); the work train as a scripted run and its evening wait; the stand-in crew's even shares (its fronts spread 30 to 40 m apart, wider than the plan's 6 to 9 m: the foreman is Crew's); the first day's double supplies; the loads on the cars (full going out, a little while standing, none going home).

**Checks.** `check-d32world.js`, `check-worktrain.js`, `check-linerender.js` (screenshots `test-output/d32-*.png`); run-all.js has their lines and one per other owner's check, skipped with a note until its file lands. Changed for the clock: check-smooth (one diagonal pixel step allowed in the 'walk' train case at 1x: at 5 ticks a second the exact motion in that frame is smaller, so the same step counts), check-d27-day (31 game hours audited, was 35: the bot's days end sooner), check-atlas (the packed block, budget 1,000 KB).
## D31 (play-test fixes)

The designer's play-test of 2026-11-13, on a phone by touch (design-doc D31): five fixes, one builder. Switches:
**`?d31=0`** (or `D31.TAP`, `D31.PACE`, `D31.SOFT` in the page; `__railhead.d31`) parks the taps, the pace and the soft
patch together and gives D29's play back; the big cart is parked in the art tool (below).

### 1. The bug: "the first freight that comes in, I can't lift the goods out"

**Reproduced** by real taps (Chromium, 390 x 844, a fresh default game with the lessons on, the bot playing the morning
up to f1 at 08:25): Show me, a tap on the boxcar (its door opens), and then **no Lift button, ever**. **Cause:** the
Lift button was offered only for a good within 2.5u of the player (`PDUI.REACH`), and the player cannot walk into a car
by hand (the walk grid stops at the door; only a handling job walks him in). Since the day's goods were halved, f1's
three goods stand at the car's two ends (the cars are filled from the far walls: 4.5u and 5.4u from the door spot), so
none was ever in reach; a tap on the open car only said "3 to unload onto the cart". With D27's fuller cars a good
happened to stand near the door, which is why it did not show before. Not causes: the lesson card, the layout, the
door (it opened), an empty car.

**Fixed** by item 2: goods no longer need to be in reach, he walks in himself. `tests/check-d31.js` plays it by taps
(the Lift button at the open door, a tap on a good, the queue) in portrait and landscape.

### 2. One tap per good

- **The sim** (section "freight handling (D27)", block "D31: one tap per good"): a new command **`{ t: 'frAuto', item }`**.
  The job (`S.fr.job.k === 'auto'`, `q`: the goods tapped, in order; `src`: where the one in hand came from) is planned
  a part at a time by `frAutoSteps` as each part ends (`frJobEnd`, like the keeper's deliver loop): the good in the
  hands to its place, else the boxcar's door (`frDoorSteps`, the old frDoor's steps), else the lift (`frTakeSteps`), so
  every step is the existing animated one and `FR.CHAIN` keeps them without a standing tick. A frAuto while that job
  runs joins `q` (event `frQueued`); a walk by hand (or any other command) stops the job and its queue, as any job.
  A tap on a stack takes its top good; tapped again while that one waits, the next one down (`frStackUp`).
- **Where a good goes** (`frAutoTo`; the old commands are unchanged, the bot still plays by `frJobHint`): out of a
  car: onto the cart when it stands within `FR.AUTO_CART` (8u) of that car's door and takes it (not hand-truck goods),
  else to its shop's storage row. Goods for the railroad: into the standing freight train that still needs goods (its
  door opened first), or onto the cart when the cart stands by them and not by the car; with no such train they stay
  (a refusal says so) and, off the cart, go to the OUT rows. Out of storage, goods for the town go on the cart
  (wherever it is). Off the cart: to storage; but at its shop's door the tap is `frDeliver`, at a shop's pickup spot
  `frPickup` (the keeper carries, as before); in town otherwise a refusal ("Wheel the cart to its shop's door first"),
  so nothing is carried by hand from town to the station.
- **The page** ("HUD and panels", `pdGoodsTap`, `pdTapOn`): a tap on a good's own pixels, or within 16 CSS px of them
  (`PDUI.TAP_PX`; beside a good only when it can be moved now), sends frAuto; a tap on the cart or the hand truck takes
  its handle (he walks to it); a tap on an open boxcar takes the next good out of it. Buttons: **Lift** now shows for
  the nearest movable good within 7u (not 2.5u) when it is work in hand, and reads "... next" while he works (it
  queues); **Set down** (with where: on the cart, in its storage row, in the car) replaces the three Put buttons;
  Open door, Close door, Take cart, Leave cart, Deliver, Pick up are as they were. **What can be tapped is marked**
  (`frTapList`, worked out once a tick; `pixWorkOverlays`): a small ochre arrow bobs over every movable good within 16u
  of the player; a good waiting in the queue carries a paper square. No button is placed over a marked good (the Lift
  and Take cart buttons used to cover the goods they were about; `pdActsHud`). The D25 `carryTap` (which answered a
  tap near the cart with "Platform cart left at its spot") is not used while the taps are on.
- **Lessons** (`PD_LESSONS`): the unloading, loading, delivery and pickup sentences say what to tap (the D27 sentences
  are kept for `D31.TAP` false). The steps' events are unchanged, so a player who taps goods straight to storage
  passes the cart steps by the later step's event.
- `D31.TAP` false (or `?d31=0`): the Lift / Put buttons in reach as D29 (check-lessons checks them that way), no taps
  on goods, no marks. The frAuto command itself stays in the sim.

### 3. The small cart

- **Art** (`tools/pixelart/goods27.py`, `sprites_carts.py`; rebuilt and embedded, atlas 609 KB of 700): the bed 3.4 x
  1.6u (was 4.2 x 3.2u), 4 x 2 cells (was 5 x 4): the picture 49 x 32 px (was about twice that each way). The dray's
  numbers are parked as `CART_D27` in goods27.py (put them in the two lines under it, rebuild, embed, and set `CARGO27`
  back from `CARGO27_D27` and `FR.PUSH_D` 2.9, `FR.CART_EDGE_Z` 14.7); it is not a URL switch, since both carts'
  pictures would have to be in the atlas.
- **Sim:** `CARGO27` (cells 8, rows 2, cols 4, 4 layers), `FR.PUSH_D` 2.5 and `FR.CART_EDGE_Z` 15.1 (F's numbers from
  before the dray), the renderer's sort box from the cells. Parking spots, the keeper's and the player's stands are
  worked out from the rows and columns, so they followed.
- **A full day on it** (`FR.CART_MIX`, false gives the storage's rule back): on the cart a good sits on whatever goods
  are under all of it (a crate on the lumber, the bars across the crates), up to 4 high; goods that take nothing on
  top (a barrel, the mail, a stove, furniture: `GOODS27.layers` 1) still take nothing. `frOnTop`, `frStackUp`, the
  never-bury rule (`frCartFree`) and `itemAudit` read what is under or over any part of a good on the cart
  (`frOver`); a good's height on the cart is the tallest pile under it (`frCartUp`, given to the renderer as `up`).
  The evening hint loads the goods delivered last first, so the others can go on top. Measured with the bot: Monday's 8
  and Tuesday's 9 goods for the town leave the platform in one trip each (the small cart with the storage's stacking
  rule took 4 goods a trip, 7 or 8 with mixed stacking before the load order and the barrel rule).
- **Old saves:** a save from before (its goods on the dray's cells) has them put into storage on its first tick
  (`S.fr.c31` marks a game with the small cart's places; set by `frTick`, so a load's hash is unchanged and no fixture
  was re-recorded).

### 4. Walking 40% faster

`WORLDS.proto.pace` times `D31.PACE` (1.4): 4.2u a game minute walking, 2.94 with the cart (3.0 and 2.1 before); inside
a car too. **A bug this uncovered and fixed:** a tick's move is now 0.56u, longer than a walk cell (0.5u), and the
level check (`walkStepMove`, the foot of a ramp or a flight) knew only a step to a neighbouring cell: the bot stood
"blocked" at the east ramp for the rest of day 1. `playerModeTick` now checks a longer move piece by piece (`stepOk`),
and a tap-to-walk move along the corners of its path, not the straight line (only when the step is longer than a
cell: other worlds and `?d31=0` are bit for bit as before). check-d27-day after (with the small cart): worst lateness
in 5 minutes, out 4 (9 and 15 before); the bot ends both days at 22:06 (22:57 and 23:02 before); everything punched,
unloaded, loaded, delivered and picked up; the two runs' hashes equal (the check prints them). Its "hours audited"
floor went from 34 to 32, since the days end sooner. check-smooth holds (the camera follows the drawn point as before).

### 5. The soft see-through patch

The part of a roof or a car over a person was a rectangle of checker (the person's box and 2 px). Now (`D31.SOFT`,
`pixSoftMask`, in `pixBlit`'s see-through branch): the covering sprite is rubbed out through a round mask, an ellipse
7 px wider than the person each way (`PIX.SEE_SOFT`), 82% clear in the middle (`PIX.SEE_A`) and fading to nothing at
its edge. Same cost shape as before (one cached canvas per sprite and patch position, `PIXA.part`; the mask cached by
size), and the checker is no longer built for it; check-pixperf's budgets hold. The box a tap passes through to the
person behind (`see`, the person's box and 2 px) is unchanged. The cut-away coach, the whole-sprite dither
(`opt.dither`) and the night lights are untouched. A soft pixel is a real half-clear pixel, the one place the pixel
view blends (the designer asked for a blend).

### Judgement calls (for the lead and the designer)

1. The cart is 4 x 2 cells, 3.4 x 1.6u (2.4 m by the scale): F's first cart. Half of each side would be 3 x 2 cells,
   but lumber and bar iron are 4 cells long and would overhang; 3.4u is "a little longer than a man" (2.5u).
2. The load on the cart is stacked mixed, 4 high, so a day fits in one trip; a barrel can stand on crates. The piles
   are drawn at their true heights, but it is a tall load on a small cart. The stacking prefers going up before using
   free floor (F's rule, kept), which can look odd (a barrel on the bars while a floor cell is free).
3. A good out of a car goes on the cart only when the cart stands within 8u of that door; else straight to storage by
   hand. On day 1 the cart happens to stand 4.5u from f1's third car, so the first goods go on the cart.
4. A tap beside a marked good (within 16 CSS px) takes the good, so walking right next to marked goods needs a tap a
   little further off. A tap on the ground still stops the queue.
5. The marks are small (7 px arrows). They show within 16u only, to keep the storage from filling with arrows.
6. The big cart cannot be switched back from the URL (art); the other three parts can (`?d31=0`).
7. The pace applies to pushing the cart too (the same 1.4).

**Checks:** `tests/check-d31.js` (new, in `npm test`): by real taps in portrait and landscape: the lesson card, Show me,
the last steps to f1's car by taps (the long walk over is one walk command), a tap on the boxcar opens it, the Lift
button and the marks at the open door, one tap on a good (the job starts, he steps in), two more taps queue, all three
reach the cart (or storage when the cart is not by), a tap on a good on the cart takes it to storage, a tap on the
ground stops the job, Set down, a tap on the cart's handle takes it; by commands: several frAuto in one tick queue,
the door is opened by the job, cart then storage; the cart's cells and picture size; the soft patch has partly clear
pixels; the pace is 1.4 times `?d31=0`'s and that switch parks the taps and the soft patch. Screenshots
`test-output/d31-{door,lift,queue,cart,soft}-{portrait,landscape}.png`. `D31_ONLY=portrait|landscape` and
`D31_DEBUG=1` (prints what each tap hit; skips the command part) for working on it. `check-lessons.js` turns `D31.TAP`
off for its Lift and Put buttons (the parked step-by-step ones); `check-handling.js` expects a barrel on a crate on the
cart to be taken (it was refused).

### The crew, the camp and the head count (Crew, 2026-11-13)

**What it is (section "the crew (D32)", prefixes `cw`, `CW`, `cp`, `hc`, store `S.cw`).** Foundation's stand-in crew is gone: 5 to 30 rail workers are simulated one by one, each with a name (`cwName`, 36 names shuffled by the seed), one of the three navvy looks, a skill (0.9 to 1.1), stamina, a tool and a place. `S.cw` is only made when a crew is taken on, so with the line off nothing changes. The store and a man's fields are listed at the head of the section.

**A man's day.** In his cot until 05:05, breakfast at his seat in the camp's yard, out by the gate to the muster (places are taken in the order the men arrive and boarded in that order), over the plank walk and aboard by about 06:10. At the railhead the men step off one after another, take their stage's tool beside the supply boxcar (a minute), walk out along the walkers' lane beside the track, and work. Stamina falls 1.6 a minute at work (more digging and spiking, less clearing prairie); at 20 a man puts his tool down (it is drawn lying beside him), walks to the verge and sits for seven minutes, one time in three by way of the water barrel for a drink; two men resting side by side turn to each other. At 12:00 everyone walks to the dinner spot and eats by the fire until 12:45. In the evening each man leaves in time to be aboard by about 18:15 (earlier when he is far, or when the way leads over the trestle), hands his tool up at the boxcar and boards. At the station: off by the plank walk, back to the camp, supper to 20:15, by the fire (each man goes once to the barrel), to his cot from 21:00, all asleep by about 21:40. A man who misses the morning train spends the day in camp; a man not aboard when the train leaves the railhead sits there until morning (`st: 'left'`) and joins his gang again when the train comes.

**The work.** A man in `'work'` in working hours gives `skill / the crew's mean skill x CW.K` wu a minute to his stage through `lnWork` (the crew never writes `S.ln.f`). Carrying trips, shuffling along as the front moves and waiting for a load all count as work, so the pace depends only on minutes worked. **`CW.K` is 1.5** (the plan's 1.0 assumed about 480 worked minutes a man a day; with real walking to and from the train, tools, rests and gangs waiting on the gang ahead a man works about 300 to 340, so the rate is higher to hold the plan's days). Measured (`cwSimDays`): **30 men 14 working days on five seeds, 5 men 86 to 88 days (6.1 to 6.3 times), 10 men 40, 15 men 32, 20 men 22, 25 men 17**. The result wanders by about half a day with any small change (the sim is deterministic but sensitive), which is why K sits where 30 men finish in the middle of day 14.

**The six gangs.** Clearers stand in the brush just ahead of the cleared front with brush hooks and axes; diggers three abreast behind the bed's front with picks and shovels; the rock gang is barrow men (load at the rock heap, push out full along a lane, tip at the front, back empty) and up to six rakers; tie men work in pairs (they queue at the tie pile, carry a tie 3u apart on the near shoulder, set it across the bed, walk back); rail men in gangs of four (two when only two are left) lift a rail at the stack with tongs, carry it up and set it down; spikers stand either side of the rails with mauls. A lone tie or rail man (a small crew) is a setter who works at the front. The piles are the crew's own (`cwProps`): the rock heap on the near side, the tie pile and rail stack on the far side, each brought up a few metres at a time as its front moves, and the dinner spot (fire and kettle, water barrel, tool rack and the tool row where a man swaps tools on a change of stage) on the near side behind the ties' front. The work train's own dump beside its cars is Foundation's and is untouched.

**The foreman (`cwAssign`).** When the train arrives, at 12:40, every half hour, and five minutes after a gang has stood wholly idle. The men go one by one to the stage with the fewest men for what its front costs a metre here (so the slowest front is as fast as can be and the six fronts move together); then a gang with under a quarter of an hour's work open lends men to the stage with the most work waiting (the cut, the trestle, a stage that has fallen behind; this is also how a small crew's men follow the work from stage to stage); ties and rails get an even number; a gang one man over or under is left alone; whoever can stays with his gang and his tool. The clearers keep at most 12 m ahead of the diggers (24 m for a crew under 12), so the site is 60 to 110u long. Play's `cwBoost(S, stage, x, k, until)` (x in line units) and `cwGang(S, stage)` are as plan 11.4.

**Walking.** `cwStep`: straight walks, by way points at the station, along a lane beside the track on the line; a man goes round another in his way, waits, and after most of a minute squeezes past. On the line every step is checked against `lnWalkable` (asked only near a border, an end or the creek). At the creek everything narrows onto the trestle's deck (`cwFz`); nobody crosses before the deck is built, the far bank is cleared from this side, and the men cross in three files.

**The camp (`cpItems`).** Inside `PROTO.CAMP`: a tent to every four men in two rows (the row by the yard first), a cot each, the cook tent at the west end, the table and benches, the fire with the kettle, the barrel, the tool rack, the wash line. Tents are lit while men are up in them at night, the fire burns from supper until the camp sleeps and at breakfast. With the player inside the camp's rectangle the tents' near walls and roofs are drawn see-through and the men are seen in their cots.

**The head count.** `hcRoll` lists the townsfolk (home when indoors; taken as home when the station's ticks are not being played, as in the jump to the next morning) and the rail men; `hcCount` gives `{ total, home, out: [{ id, name, where }] }`; `hcTick` at 22:00 keeps it in `S.ln.hc` (with the names of those out) and pushes `hcDone`. A rail man counts as home at the station; out when on the line ("the line at 281 m") or still aboard the work train away from the station. In an unplayed game the sheriff is usually still out at 22:00, and the crew is reported aboard when the work train has had to wait for a clear road.

**Drawing.** `cwDrawList(S, 'line' | 'station')` gives the townsfolk's shape plus `anim` and `frame` (the navvies' own animations, by the tick), `name`, `stage`, `sta`, `st`, and `held` (the tool on the shoulder, the barrow at the grip mark) and `prop` (the carried rail, the tie being set, the tool on the ground); on the line the id is the man's number, at the station `'cw<n>'`. `cwPixDress` draws `held` and `prop` with the man.

**Shared lines touched (all small).** In the renderer: the station's townsfolk loop honours a named `anim` and `frame`; the line's scene calls `cwPixDress` for each man and adds `cwProps`; `pixGlow` and `pixNight` let `camp/fire/<n>` glow at night. In Foundation's checks: check-d32world and check-worktrain expected a head count of exactly 30 (now the 30 rail men and the townsfolk), check-linerender counts redrawn chunks frame by frame inside the page (the gangs are now close together, so several chunks go stale at once).

**Judgement calls.** The noon meal is at the dinner spot in the middle of the work site, not at the train (at 2.4u a minute the train is up to an hour's walk from the clearers). Tools are swapped at the tool row there after the morning. The lone setters, the three files over the trestle, K 1.5 and the clearers' lead are described above.

**Known limits.** The men do not walk round the work train's cars or the player (they pass through); two walkers can overlap for a moment when one squeezes past (about 50 to 100 such man-ticks in a day of 190,000). The day at the creek is slow (about 150 m) because most of the crew moves to the trestle and back. Cost: `cwTick` 0.02 to 0.04 ms a tick with 30 men.

**Checks.** `check-crew.js` (every man's day in order, no two standing within 0.5u, the stage's tool, `lnWalkable`, the six gangs and their animations, the meal, the same twice and after a save in mid morning, the pace windows on two seeds, the tick's cost, a screenshot `d32-crew-site-portrait.png`) and `check-camp.js` (tents by crew size, a cot each, the camp's day, lit and dark, a man left on the line reported and back next morning, the townsfolk in the roll in the real game, screenshots `d32-camp-{supper,night,asleep}-portrait.png`).

### The wild country: the artists' art in the line's view (Wilds builder, 2026-11-13)

This replaces the Foundation's "natural borders" paragraph above where they differ: the wandering borders, the belt rows with guessed names and the stand-ins are gone; the two wilds artists' art is drawn as they laid it out (`plans/pixel/d32/far/README.md`, `plans/pixel/d32/near/README.md`).

**The layout (one table: `LINE.WILD`, `LINE.RUNS`, `LINE.MARKS` in "the line (D32)").** Both borders are straight lines along the track, as the art is.
- **Regions** begin on a multiple of 16u (`LINE.RUNS`: the plan's metres moved to the nearest one: creek 448u, hills 864, woods 1376, rocks 1936, river 2224, canyon 2800, pass 3008, valley 3296). West of 0 the prairie goes on, east of the end the valley. The cost kinds (`LINE.SEGS`) are untouched.
- **Ground**: `lnTileWild` gives `tile/wild/<region>/<1..4>` by a hash of the cell; over the 12u before the next region begins the blend tiles `tile/wild/blend/<a>-<b>/<1..4>`, the share rising with jitter by cell. Tiles are only drawn where they can show (south of 14u behind the far wall's front line). The creek's crossing at the trestle keeps batch one's water and bank tiles (`lnCreekAt`). South of the near edge those tiles are carried on over the edge piece, cell by cell, until they meet the water the art draws alongside (`pixLnCreekJoin`), so the crossing and the creek join; north it runs in under the far wall's trees.
- **Far side (north)**: modules 16u long in rows (`lnWildFar(seed, row, n)`). Row 0 is rank 1 with its front line at z -9.5 (`LINE.BORDER.N`); row 1 is rank 2, 14u behind; rows 2 and up are rank 3, 30u behind and every 20u further north **for ever**, so the top of any screen is covered at any zoom and with the view dragged as far north as it goes. Each row's grid is shifted along x (0, 5, 9, then 7 more a row), so seams never line up. Variants by `lnVar` (never the same twice running). The last two modules before a region's end are the artist's `to_<next>/1` and `/2`.
- **Near side (south)**: three bands measured from the near edge at z 11 (`LINE.BORDER.S`): edge (8u pieces), mid and far (16u pieces), `lnWildNear(seed, band, n)`; the last 16u before a region's end is the artist's blend piece in each band; the region's own ground and batch one's small scatter go on beyond 58u. Drawn edge, mid, far (the nearer band wins).
- **Landmarks**. Edge pieces in place of a normal one (`LINE.WILD.EDGE_MARKS`, by the slot's x): the beaver dam (470 m), the ferry landing (1,848 m), the canyon's shelf (six pieces, 2,027 to 2,061 m), the mill (2,402 m, its wheel turning). Standing alone (`LINE.MARKS`): at the far wall's foot the lone cottonwood (180 m), a grave (236 m), the giant pine (1,150 m), the trapper's cabin (1,270 m), the balanced rock (1,420 m), the waterfall (1,700 m, animated), the arch (1,990 m); on the near side the wagon wreck (122 m), the far cabin with smoke (268 m), the far town's water tower and roofs (2,492 and 2,514 m, far down the screen: seen at the furthest zoom); in the corridor the summit board (2,210 m, a sorted thing with the artist's box) and the rock cut (1,478 to 1,506 m: five 8u pieces of the two faces, sorted things; the low rise lies across the line until the diggers are through).
- **Wildlife** (`lnLife(seed, cell)`, 48u cells, by the seed; about 30 beasts on the whole line): prairie dogs and far-off buffalo on the prairie, ducks on the creek and the river (the renderer puts each on a wet pixel of the piece there, or leaves it out), deer and a buck at the wood's edge, a fox in the hills, the rocks and the valley, and the hawk's shadow crossing every 50 seconds over open country. All of it stands outside the ground a man can walk.

**The corridor.** `lnBorder(seed, x, side)` returns `{ d, pad, end, narrow }`: the far wall's front line 9.5u north (a walker stops 1u short: its foot is ragged), the near edge 11u south (0.4u short). `lnWalkable` uses it, so the player and the men are stopped by the drawn border. Narrow places: on the canyon's shelf the near border is 5u from the line; in the rock cut both are 3u. Crew's `cwOk`, `cwFz` (the funnel it already had for the trestle) and `cwGeo` (the piles and the dinner spot keep to open ground before a narrow place) know both; the work train's supply piles are drawn before a narrow place too. At the strip's two ends batch one's pines still close in to a cutting (inside the straight borders). **A judgement call**: the near artist drew for a near edge at z 9; it is at z 11 here, because Crew's dinner spot (fire at z 9.2, seats to 10.4), the heap at 6.3 and the train's piles at 5 to 7.5 already fill the ground out to 10.4, and moving another owner's work site was not this job. So the shelf leaves 5u on the near side, not 3. Changing `LINE.BORDER.S` moves the whole near side; the dinner spot would have to move first.

**Drawing (block "The line's view (D32)").** Everything static is baked into the line's chunks: tiles and the roadbed, then `pixLnFarDraw` (the rows whose pictures reach the chunk, furthest first, then the landmarks at the wall's foot), the small things in the corridor, `pixLnNearDraw` (the bands and the near landmarks), the small things beyond. **Moving water**: a chunk that holds water gets two see-through overlays (the water's frames 1 and 2), made in idle time a step at a time by drawing the near side with each frame and keeping only the pixels that differ from the baked chunk (`pixLnOverlays`); one is drawn over the chunk each frame, changing every 0.45 s (`PIX.LN_WATER_S`), so a tree of the next band or a landmark in front of the water stays in front. A chunk's overlays are kept when its roadbed is redrawn. **Each frame** (`pixLnLive`, over the ground and under everything in the corridor): the mill's wheel, the waterfall's water, the cabin's smoke, the beasts, the hawk. These run on real seconds (`PIX.lnT`), not game time, so they stay calm at 4x speed. The big sheets of the regions near the view are decoded ahead (`pixLnWarmSheets`).

**Rebuilding the art without the long redraw.** `python3 tools/pixelart/build.py` then `python3 tools/pixelart/embed.py`, about two minutes (the near side redraws in 70 s; everything else is quick). The far side's 138 modules (80 minutes to redraw) come from two caches: the artist's one in the system's temp folder, and, new, **the committed sheets themselves**: `tools/pixelart/wild_far_cache.json` records the hash of the drawing code each module in `out/sheet-wild_far_*.png` was drawn by, and while that code is unchanged `sprites_wild_far.py` cuts the module back out of the sheet (`_from_sheet`; `WILD_NO_TMP=1` forces this path: 0.5 s for all of them). Change a region's drawing code and only that region's modules redraw. Both artists had named a file `wild_regions.py`; the far side's is now `wild_far_regions.py`. `build.py` packs a sheet wider than 512 px when it would be taller than the packed index holds (4,095).

**Sizes and speed (measured 2026-11-13, headless Chromium, phone size).** `index.html` is 5.91 MB (was 2.52); the atlas block 3,935 KB (was 628; check-atlas's budget is now 4,500). Load on a 4x-throttled CPU: the atlas ready at 1.9 to 2.8 s over five loads (the build before: 1.7 to 2.0 s), the first frame about 0.1 s later; unthrottled 0.85 s (was 0.6). Not slow, but the file is over twice the size to download. Frame at 4x CPU with the train and 30 men: 1.3 to 2.5 ms on average in the river run, the creek, the canyon and the woods at the default zoom and the furthest (95th centile under 6 ms; budget 8). A chunk takes 1 to 11 ms at 4x, a water overlay step about 4 ms, all in idle time: walking (4.2 u/s) and riding (25 u/s) built no chunk inside a frame. Memory: a far-side sheet is about 8 MB decoded and a region uses two or three; the browser decodes them when first drawn.

**Checks.** check-linerender (extended: each region's own art in the frame, the joins' transition modules and blend pieces, every landmark drawn, no background pixel at 84 views a orientation with the chunks themselves filled with the marker colour, no near-side picture reaching up past the near edge, the water moving at a calm rate, wildlife drawn, the river's frame time, a walk with no chunk built in a frame; screenshots `test-output/d32-wild-<region or join>-<default|far>-<orient>.png`), check-d32world (the layout: straight borders, every key asked for is in the atlas, all 16 + 24 + 32 transition modules, blend pieces and blend tiles used, landmarks in their regions and off the walkable ground, nothing scattered under the art, wildlife sparing and never in the way).

**Known, small.** check-pixperf's station case at scale 1 sits at its budget (7.2 to 8.3 ms against 8 with 4x throttling) and failed one run in two here; the station's drawing is untouched. The creek's crossing is batch one's square-cornered tile water between two pieces of drawn creek. A pinhole of three pixels inside one far transition module shows the ground's fill colour (the check allows 12 px a view). The rock cut's north face has a grass top on the rocks' bare ground, and the rise across it is batch one's grassy one. Beyond the near side's far band in the canyon and the pass the level ground resumes (it reads as the far rim). The track still begins from nothing at x 0 inside the west end's pines (Foundation's).

### Play, stage 1: riding out, walking the line (2026-11-13)

**What it is.** The player can go out to the line in person and come back. Section "on the line (D32)" (the sim: `S.lp`, `lpTick`, `lpCmd`, `lpPose`, `lpFade`) and the block "Line (D32)" at the end of dev mode in "HUD and panels" (the page: the dev panel's buttons, the line's bar, the fades, `lpTap`, the Look card). Nothing changes until the player rides out: a game without `S.lp` plays and hashes as before.

**Riding out.** The dev panel's "Ride out as a rail worker": while the work train is coming in or standing (before 06:20) he walks to the muster by the water tower (the station's own walk, `P.mode 'to'`), boards over the plank walk to the crew car's steps once the train stands, and is then out of sight; the station's view rides with the crew car (`lpStationFocus`, a hook in `pixPlayerPoint`) and fades as the train leaves the map; the view goes to the line, dark (a "Riding out along the line" screen) until the train is 120u from its stop, then fades in on the crew car braking in; when it stops he steps down beside the crew car's steps. If the train has gone (or does not run today), the same at once. "Ride out now" goes at once from anywhere, and "Skip the ride" in the bar does the same mid-ride. From the Ride out command `S.player.away` is 'line': the station's player takes no commands (Foundation's away rules: the relief lever man, the day's figures) and, while he is on the line, stands parked at the muster.

**Coming back.** "Ride home 18:20" in the bar: he walks to the crew car's steps and boards; at 18:20 the view watches the train pull away for 2 game minutes, fades to dark ("Riding home along the line"), comes back at the station as the train backs in (19:05 or later, by Foundation's wait for a clear road), and at 19:15 he steps off at the steps and walks the plank walk to the muster, his own man again. "Back now" (the bar, the dev panel) is at once: a quick fade, at the muster. If the train leaves without him he stays on the line ("Back now" brings him home).

**On the line.** He is drawn as at the station (the agent sprite, walking and standing), the camera centred on him between native pixels as at the station (`pixFocus(alpha)` blends him between ticks; a drag looks away and eases back once he walks; "Centre" in the bar when he is looking away). A tap walks him: straight to the point if he can stand there, else to the last point along the way he can stand on (the natural borders, the water and the strip's ends stop him; there are no invisible edges in open ground), round the nearer end of the work train when it is in the way (the train is one box, its cars end to end; if it moves up onto him he steps aside). The walk is in sub-steps of 0.41u at the station's pace, checked with `lnWalkable`. All of it is in the sim (`lpWalk { x, z }` in hundredths), so a save on the line or mid-ride loads into the same moment (every visual, the fades too, is worked out from the state: `lpFade(S, sod)`).

**The Look card.** A tap on a man (his drawn pixels, `pixPick`'s `{ k: 'cw', id }` from `cwDrawList(S, 'line')`) opens his card: name, stage (and his gang's size), what he is doing, stamina; the same man again, or the card's Gang button, shows his gang: men, front, at work or at the meal, the gang's stamina, the names. Another tap closes the card without walking. The card reads Crew's men through the signed interfaces: `cwGang(S, stage)` for the gangs and `cwDrawList` for the man; **for the card's fields Crew can add `cwLook(S, id) -> { name, stage, doing, stam (0..1) }`**, else the card takes `name`, `stage`, `doing` (or `act`, put into words) and `stam` (or `stamina`) from the draw list's item. With Foundation's stand-in crew there are no men to tap (check-lineplay stands four in through `cwDrawList` and `cwGang`).

**The bar.** `#lpBar` (class `lnHud`, so it stays up with the page class `line`): on the line in person it replaces Foundation's look-point bar (`lpHud()` answers true); at the station it shows while he walks to the muster and boards (below the Next job line). Text: the metres spiked and the day, what the men and the train are doing; buttons (44px and more): Ride home, Back now, Skip the ride, Stay here (at the muster), Centre. Portrait: under the clock; a wide screen: beside it. The dev panel's rows: Ride out as a rail worker, Ride out now, Come back with the evening train, Back to the station now, and Foundation's "Look at the line" kept (row id `look`, the look point and its bar), each shown only when it applies.

**Shared lines touched (smallest edits).** `pixAgentItem`: the station's agent is not drawn while he is in the crew car (`lpHideAgent`). `pixPlayerPoint`: the station's view follows the crew car while he rides through (`lpStationFocus`). `pixFocus(alpha)` and the camera lines of `pixLnFrame` (Foundation's block): the player blended between ticks, the camera between native pixels on him, the look away eased back as he walks and not clamped against him while he rides in the cutting west of the junction. `handleEvent`: `lpRefused` shows its reason as a toast. `run-all.js`: the check's line.

**Provisional choices (Play).** The fades (one real second; dark between the station's edge and the train's last 120u, and from 2 minutes after 18:20 until it backs in); he steps down 3.4u south of the track at the crew car's steps; a tap on a man opens his card rather than walking; riding out is refused while he carries something or is in a coach; the join-in games (`lpJoin`, `lpLeave`, `lgDone`) still do nothing (stage 2).

**Check.** `check-lineplay.js`, in portrait and landscape by real taps: out with the train (muster, plank walk, hidden, the view on the crew car, dark, fade in, stepped down), export/import mid-ride and play on to the same hash, a real page reload on the line, the camera on him and gliding, a walk by touch, round the train to its far side (never inside it), stopped at the near border on walkable ground, the Look card and the gang's card, Back now, the look point kept, a ride when the train has gone, Ride out now, the bar's buttons thumb-sized on the screen, home with the evening train (dark, the station as it backs in, off at the muster, his own man again), the station's day went on (trains counted, levers thrown by the relief man), the same ride twice in the sim gives the same hash, no console errors. Screenshots `lineplay-*.png`.

### Town, stage 2: hired station workers and the away rules (2026-11-13)

**What it is (section "hired workers (D32)", prefixes `hw`, `HW`, store `S.hw`; its head comment is the contract).** Dev mode's three greyed rows are live (they turn on by themselves once `HW` exists): ticket punchers 0 to 3, cargo loaders 0 to 4, delivery men 0 to 2, hired **at once** (`dvSet` sets the count; `hwSync` in the next tick adds or lets go; a man let go puts what he holds into storage). Each is a simulated man with a name (`HW.NAMES`, shuffled by the seed) and a home (the hotel, until stage 3), drawn in the station agent's uniform with the agent's animations (walk, carry, lift, door, push, handOver, climb, punch: provisional, until the artist draws hands of their own). `S.hw` exists only once someone is hired, so with nobody hired the game plays and hashes exactly as before (check-d27-day passes unchanged).
- **Punchers** take the lowest coach of a standing passenger train with seated travellers not punched that no other puncher works (the player's coach last), walk to its nearer usable end, climb in, walk the aisle and punch seat by seat through **`pbPunchBy(S, stock, seat, who, ev)`** (new, in P's section: P's marks and counts, `pbPunched` with `by`), 1.5 times the player's punch; never the traveller the player is punching. They wait in the aisle while travellers are still coming to that coach, then climb down. The coach a puncher works in is drawn cut away (`hwCutaway`, `hwPbList` in P's draw shape) and the traveller holds his ticket out to him. A passenger train is not ready while a puncher is aboard.
- **Loaders** take goods for the town out of a standing freight train into their storage rows, then goods for the railroad from the OUT rows into a train that still takes goods (never more than it needs, counting what the others carry to it), by F's spot rules (`frStoreSpot`, `frPutDest`, `frSpotOf`) and F's times times 1.3, by hand (a hand-truck good is heaved), opening a shut door first; their own walker: the walk grid's static path (`hwPath`: `walkAStar` with no train stamped, so it is the same after a reload), straight lines inside a car.
- **Delivery men** from 17:30 load their own cart (a second platform cart, drawn with `pixToolItem`, not in `S.tools`) with up to 8 goods for the town in the street's order, push it by the cart grid's path to each shop, carry each good to the door and hand it in (the keeper steps out, `frDelivered`), load the shops' pickups on the way and bring them back to the OUT rows; no new trip after 22:30 (what is left goes back into storage).
- **The day:** to work from the hotel at 06:00; home from 20:30 (punchers and loaders, when nothing is in hand and no train stands), the delivery man when his cart is empty at its spot after 22:30; at home they are not drawn and the head count (`hcRoll` gets `hwRoll`) has them at the hotel.

**Sharing the work (the D31 queue and its claim).** D31 landed the player's own queue (`S.fr.job`: the goods tapped, `J.q`, the good in hand, each step's good), not a shared job list, so the claim is kept this way: the player's job is his claim (`hwPlayerIds`); a man's claim is the good his job names (`m.job.id`); a man takes only a good nobody holds or claims, the oldest first; the player always has first call (a good his job names is let go by the man in the same tick, and again at the end of the man's lift, so the player's pick never meets a good that has gone). A good in a man's hands or on his cart is in Foundation's place `CREW { who: 'hw<id>' }` (`'hw<id>c<slot>'` on the cart), one place every tick, counted by `itemAudit`; the player's tap on it is refused ("A station hand has that"). Measured with the bot playing and 2/3/1 hired: the two shared the day (the player punched and stored, the hands unloaded, punched and loaded), never one good claimed by both, the audit clean every hour.

**While the player is away (`hwAwayTick`, provisional choices for the designer).** A standing train leaves by itself when its work is done and its time has come, or at the latest 10 minutes (`HW.GRACE_S`) after its time, its time moved on by however late it came in. Unpunched travellers ride free and those not aboard yet are left behind: both counted missed (P's `pbOnDepart`). Goods for the town not unloaded leave with the train (used up `soldOutside` and marked `left: 1` in the item log, which F's audit accepts). Goods for the railroad not loaded wait in the OUT rows for the next day; goods not delivered wait in storage. Trains that come in late while he is away are counted. All of it goes into Foundation's `S.ln.awStats` (levers, late, missed, left), so the summary's line reads e.g. "While you were on the line: 18 levers thrown late by the relief man, 8 trains late, 28 travellers missed, 8 goods left aboard." The hands' own day is a new summary line, "Station hands: 2 punchers, 3 loaders, 1 delivery man: 28 tickets punched, 8 goods unloaded, 4 goods loaded, 8 goods delivered, 5 pickups fetched." Measured (seed 1866, day 1, away all day): with 2/3/1 hired every train left with its work done (none by the grace rule), nothing missed or left aboard, everything delivered and fetched; with nobody hired all 7 stopping trains left by the grace rule, 28 of 28 travellers missed, 8 of 8 goods left aboard, 8 trains late, 18 levers late, nothing delivered but the plough the feed store collects itself.

**Shared lines touched (all small, each marked "D32 (Town)").** P: `pbPunchBy` added after `pbReady`; `pbWhyNotReady` "The ticket puncher is still aboard"; `pbDrawList` the ticket held out to a puncher. F: `frCmd`'s frAuto refuses a good in a CREW place; `frAuditDay` accepts business from CREW and a `soldOutside` marked left. Crew: `hcRoll` adds `hwRoll`. U: `pdSummary` adds `sum.hw`, the summary card its line "Station hands". R: `pixLists` adds `hwPbList` to the travellers and `hwCutaway` to the cut-away cars, `pixActAnim` knows 'punch', `pixScene` adds `hwPixItems` (the carts and the goods in hand) before the player. `run-all.js`: the check's line.

**Judgement calls.** The hands wear the agent's uniform (no art of their own yet); loaders carry by hand and never use the hand truck or the platform cart; the delivery man has his own cart; deliveries in the evening only; the "oldest first" order is by the good's id, unloading before loading; while away a train with its work done leaves at its time, not earlier; the summary's away line shows only when the line is running (Foundation's condition), the hands' line whenever someone is hired.

**Known limits.** The hands' paths ignore trains (a static grid, for exact reloads): a hand can walk through a train standing on a crossing; they pass through each other and the player; a loader's chosen cell can be taken by the player's put first, then his own put is planned again (the player's frAuto put can likewise be refused once by a cell a hand just filled). In the town the delivery man's cart can stand behind a hitched horse. Cost: a whole day with 6 hands about 0.9 s of sim in the browser (with the townsfolk and everything else), `hwPath` a few hundred microseconds a walk.

**Check.** `check-hired.js` (in `run-all.js`): a day away with 2 punchers, 3 loaders and 1 delivery man (everything punched, unloaded, loaded, delivered and fetched, none by the grace rule, the hands home at 23:30 and back at 06:30, the head count, the summary line, the audit every hour, the same day twice and a mid-morning save played on to the same hash); the same day with nobody hired (the away counts); the player at home played by the bot with the same hands (never one good claimed by both, the audit, everything done); a tap on a good in a hand's keeping refused; a loader let go; screenshots `test-output/hired-{punch,load,deliver}-{portrait,landscape}[-zoom].png` (a puncher in the cut-away coach, loaders in a boxcar, the delivery man at a shop door); no console errors.
### Play, stage 2: joining in, the six games, the line's lessons (2026-11-13)

**Joining in.** Walking up to a gang (the stretch each gang works: within 12u behind its front, the clearers in the 5u of brush ahead of theirs; `lgNearStage`) brings up a thumb-sized "Join the diggers" button under him. A tap sends `lpJoin { stage }`; he walks to his place (`lgSpot`: just behind the front on his side of the track, beside the rail for the spikers, in the brush for the clearers; `lgPlace` moves it beside the work train when the train stands on it) and is drawn in work clothes with the navvies' frames, his tool or load in his hands (Crew's way of drawing it), a small bar over his head with his last q and a warm glow at his feet while he lifts the men. He shuffles up as the front moves. The game panel (bottom of the screen in portrait; at the right under the line's bar in landscape, so Ride home and Back now stay in reach) has the gang's name, his last result and worth (and "lifting n men"), five pips of his last results, Step out, the game, and a one-line hint. Step out works as the thumb lifts from it (on pointerup, so the other thumb can still be on the tie: a two-finger touch gives no click; not on the press, or the tap's click would fall through to what was under the panel): the round is dropped and nothing is sent, so nothing is left in the air; walking away, Back now, Ride home, the meal and the end of the day also end it (`lgLeft { why }`). Not joining is always fine: the men work on without him. The first card of a lesson and Settings hold the game.

**The six games** (block "Joining in (D32)" in "HUD and panels"; a logical box 360 x 220 scaled to fit; each round a few seconds, repeating while he stays; random choices from the game's seed and the round number). Timing scores are full within 90 ms of the beat, nothing at 330 ms. Good is q 75 and over, fair 45 to 74, poor under 45. (0) Clearing: a dashed chalk line across the clump; swipe along it, quick and straight: three cuts fell a clump (four, from the left and the right in turn, for a trunk in the woods); measured: each swipe's angle and offset from the line, and the pace; chips fly, a white slash, the clump shrinks and falls. (1) Digging: a ring closes on the mark four times (a 760 ms beat, the two navvies beside him swing on it); tap as it meets the mark, then flick up to throw the earth into the barrow; measured: each tap's error, the flick's; the hole deepens, earth flies, the screen jolts harder on a good swing, "Missed" on a missed beat. (2) Laying rock: the barrow rolls in; tap to tip as its nose is over the chalk cross; then drag side to side to rake the stones level between two pegs (each pass brings the bed towards the level the load makes, and pulls the spill back in; a "level" bar fills); measured: the tip's distance from the cross (35%) and how level the bed is (65%) when level enough or after 4 s. (3) Ties, with a partner: press and hold as his "Hup!" ring fills, keep your end (a dot) level with his as he walks it up, let go on "Down!" on the chalk mark; measured: the lift's timing, keeping in step (a line between the ends, green when close), the let-go's timing, the distance from the mark; dust where it lands. Lift your thumb early and the tie is set down there (a poor round). (4) Rails, with the gang of four: "One... two... Lift!" — press and hold on Lift, drag to keep the rail on the gauge line (a top-down strip above), let go on "Down!"; measured: the lift's timing, the gauge error at the drop, the let-go's timing; sparks at both ends, "Clang!". (5) Spiking, turn about with a partner: he strikes, then you, three times each on a 620 ms beat; tap as the ring closes on the spike head; measured: each blow's error; a blow off the beat bends the spike (the round is poor at once); sparks, a ring, the spike sinks. Each result goes to the sim as one command, `lgDone { stage, q }`; the touches never do.

**The numbers (section "on the line (D32)", `LG`).** One accepted `lgDone` is one finished action; it is worth the time since his last action (at most 4 game minutes) at a man's pace (`CW.K`) times `LG.worth(q)` = 0.5 + 0.75 q / 100 men: 1.25 men at q 100, 0.875 at 50, 0.5 at 0; it goes through `lnWork` (work beyond what is open to the stage comes back, and the panel says he is waiting on the gang ahead). Actions closer than 8 ticks (64 game seconds, about one real second at 1x) are refused, and he must be at his place, so a flood of commands can never be worth more than one man at 1.25 working the same time (the check measures it). The lift: while his last three q average 60 or more, `cwBoost` makes the men of his gang within 12 m work at 1 + 0.15 x the average / 100 (at most 1.15), until 23 ticks (about 3 game minutes) after his last action; poor rounds let it lapse. He does not tire. At 2x and 4x a round takes the same real seconds but more game time passes, so he is worth less beside the men (the 4-minute cap); judgement call, see below.

**Lessons for the line** (`LG_LESSONS`, added to `PD_LESSONS`, so they are U's cards, Show me, Skip, OK, kept in `S.pd.les`, closed the next morning, replayed from Settings): `lnRide` (Out on the line: the six gangs, walk up to one, Join or watch; the first time he is set down on the line), `lnClear`, `lnDig`, `lnRock`, `lnTies`, `lnRails`, `lnSpike` (how to play each, then "good, fair, poor; work well and the men near you work faster; Step out whenever you like"; the first time he joins that gang, done after his third action), `lnLeave` (Stepping out: nothing is left half done; the first time he steps out or walks away), `lnHome` (Coming home: six o'clock, Ride home or Back now; done when he is home). None has an arrow (their places are on the line). The card is shown on the line (the class `lnHud`).

**Shared lines touched (smallest edits).** `pixLnScene` (Foundation's line scene): his pose's own `who`, `anim` and `frame` when he works with a gang, and `lgPixDress` for what he holds and the bar over his head. `PD_LESSONS.push(...LG_LESSONS)` after U's list, and `pdCheckSaveShape`'s lesson-waiting test reads the ids from `PD_LESSONS` (the line's are allowed). `pdLesCan` (U's): a station lesson does not open while he is away on the line (it waits for its next moment at the station), and a line lesson (`line: true`) opens only there; before, a freight's unloading lesson came up on the line and held his game. `check-lessons.js` (U's): counts the station's six lessons, not the whole list. `check-lineplay.js` (mine): puts the line's first lesson aside before walking. `lpWalkTick` (mine): a walk ends within a rounding error of its end (he could stand a hair from it for ever).

**Provisional choices (Play, stage 2).** The worth (0.5 to 1.25 men), the lift (1.15 at most, within 12 m, from an average of 60), the 4-minute cap and the one-second spacing; the beats (760 ms digging, 620 ms spiking) and the timing window (90 to 330 ms); the rake's 4 s and its level 0.9; the clearers' place 1.8u into the brush; the player is not drawn in his own ties game (his end is the dot).

**Check.** `check-linegames.js`, portrait and landscape, by Chromium touch events (taps, swipes, press-hold-drag-release, a second thumb on Step out): the Join button beside each gang, thumb-sized and on the screen; each game at good, fair and poor timing gives that word (portrait; landscape plays good) and the sim takes it; three good rounds lift the diggers near him (`cwBoost`, the panel says so), poor rounds let it lapse; Step out mid-carry leaves no round sent and him empty-handed; the whole session's ticks replayed from its start give the same hash; a flood of `lgDone` one a tick is taken at most once in 8 ticks and is worth no more than one man at 1.25; no console errors. Screenshots `linegames-<game>-<orient>.png` mid-round. `check-linelessons.js`: in the sim each line lesson opens at its moment, steps on, finishes, never opens twice, comes back after replay, and a mid-day state as JSON plays on to the same hash; on the page the first card holds the game on the line, Show me lets it run, the game panel waits under the first card of the spikers' lesson, the card is inside the screen, Settings has the Lessons row on the line. Screenshots `linelessons-*.png`.

### D32 stage 3: the bigger town (sim) (Town, 2026-11-14)

Plan 8.1 (homes, roles, a daily round) and 8.4 (a simple economy you can see). The rooms (8.2, 8.3: walking inside, the
fading walls, the shelves indoors) and the far town are the Rooms builder's; people still go in at a door and are not
drawn, as in D27. Code: section "the bigger town (D32)" (after "hired workers (D32)"; its head comment is the contract)
and TOWN27's block of rows "D32 stage 3 (Town)" in "starting world layout".

**The switch.** `?tn=0` (`TN_OFF`, `TN.on`) parks all of it: the D27 town of 19 people, the 8 D27 houses, no `S.tn`, no
economy, W's routines (`check-townsfolk.js` now opens with it: it tests W's town). Nothing is deleted.

**What exists.**
- **Houses** (TOWN27 rows, `tn: 'cabin' | 'frame' | 'two'`): ten on 8u lots. Five between the D27 houses on the back
  lanes (house9 at (-35, -50); house10 to house13 at x 35, z -50, -70, -90, -112), doors on lanes W and E as theirs. Five on
  a third lane west of the town (`TOWN27.tnLanes`: lane `W2` at x -43, joined to lane W at the alley z -73 and at its
  north end z -116; house14 to house18 at x -51, z -66 to -114, facing it), north of the camp (its z0 is -58; house14
  ends at -61.75). Clear of every footprint, the camp, the muster, the wash, the line's country (east of x 80) and the
  wood; solid nature on the new lots and the lane is taken away (only with tn on). The lane and its joins are dirt on the
  ground (`tnLaneAt`, one line in `pixCellTile`). Each has a door node in the town graph (W's `townGraphBuild` reads
  `tnLanes`). **Art**: the renderer asks for `bld/house_<tn>/<face>`, then `bld/house_<tn>` (the artist's keys, by key,
  once they have a box), else the D27 design as a stand-in (`tnHouseKey`; cabin design 3, frame 1, two-room 2).
- **People**: `TN_PEOPLE`, 22 more in `TF_AGENTS` (41 townsfolk): Martha Pratt and Josie Turner (the keepers' wives),
  Gus Lindqvist (the smith's hand), the Abbotts (Will, store clerk; Mary; Ada), the Marshes (Edwin, bank clerk; Lucy;
  Jimmy), Hattie Bell (hotel cook), the Diaz (Rosa, hotel maid; Ana), Billy Ortega (stable boy), Lottie Shaw (barmaid),
  Sam Ives (printer's devil), Pete Hensley, Otis and Ruth Fenn (carpenter for the undertaker; bath woman at the
  barber's), Dan Pike (feed hand), Joe Haskins (livery hand), Ned and Nora Quinn (assay clerk; keeps house). Six pupils.
  Every townsperson has `{ role, home, work }` (`TN_ROLES` for W's 19; with tn on Tommy Pratt lives over the store with
  his parents and the cowhands' work is the range, `'edge'`). The hired hands (`S.hw`) lodge at the hotel. 26
  households (the people of one home), each with at least one earner.
- **The round** (`TN_ROUTINES` by kind: keeper, barkeep, barmaid, clerk, sheriff, preacher, teacher, pupil, laundress,
  cowhand, homemaker, hand, cook; W's planner with two hooks, `tnRoutine` and `tnPlace`): home, work, the noon meal
  (the hotel's table or home, `meal`), an errand (a shop by the draw), the saloon or the porch in the evening
  (`evening`), **home by 21:30** (every evening errand ends by 21:10 plus up to 10 minutes' jitter); church on Sunday;
  the pupils at school Monday to Friday.
- **The rail men** (Crew's men, Crew's own walking `cwGo` / `cwStep`): Saturday at 20:20 a third of the men at the camp's
  fire walk by the town graph to the saloon (each to his own spot by its door, rows of five), are inside to 21:15 (each
  one custom) and walk back; Sunday from 19:45 a quarter walk to the church for evening prayers to 20:50. Back at the
  gate they are handed to Crew walking to their cots (`'toBed'`). While out `m.st` is `'town'` (indoors `pl` 0), which
  Crew's tick leaves alone, and the head count says "the town". The line's pace is untouched (check-bigtown compares the
  fronts after a week with `?tn=0`: the same to the millimetre).
- **The economy** (`S.tn`, settled each morning at 05:00 by `tnRoll`, called first in W's `tfTick` so the day's plans see
  it; `tnTick` counts the day's events):
  - stock 0 to `TN.CAP` 12 for the nine shops that take goods by rail (`TN.USE`: store 3, saloon 3, hotel 2, smithy 2,
    livery 2, newspaper 2, bank 1, undertaker 1, feed 1 a day while open); a good handed in at a door (`frDelivered`,
    `pgDelivered`: the player's, the hired delivery man's and the shop's own collection) adds 3, a plough, piano,
    furniture or stove 6; a new town starts with two days' use;
  - open or shut: open while it had stock at 05:00, or on its first morning at 0 ("empty shelves"); shut on the second
    morning at 0. So goods missed from day 0 give empty shelves on day 2 and every shop shut on day 3 (days counted from
    0 as the sim does: the clock's Day 1 is day 0). The barber, the sheriff, the assay office, the church and the school
    keep no stock and are always open. The hotel and the saloon also stay open on yesterday's travellers' custom;
  - pay: per household 2 coins a day for each earner whose workplace was open, less 1 upkeep, a purse of 0 to 20
    (starts at 8); mood content, worried after 2 days without pay, unhappy after 4;
  - what it does to the round: a shut shop's keeper stands at its door all day (so he is there to take a delivery, W's
    `tfAt`), its other people idle on their porches or at the saloon's door; an errand to a shut shop ends at its door;
    unhappy households' grown-ups stand about the depot square's ends and the street's foot 18:30 to 20:45;
  - travellers' custom: each traveller who steps off (`pbOff`, `ppOff`) has a 1 in 3 chance (`rngFor(seed, 'tn',
    'cust', run, id)`) to walk from platform 1's west end to the hotel or the saloon (half each, shut or not: the custom
    is what keeps them open), stay an hour (1 in 10 of the hotel's overnight, to 07:00) and walk back; each a custom.
    The hired hands lodging at the hotel are a night's custom each. At most 16 visitors at once.
- **Visible signs**: a "Closed" board standing in the street at each shut shop's steps (`tnPixItems`: `ui/closed_card`
  when the atlas has it, else a paper card with the word in the pixel font; what stands in front turns see-through); the
  idle on porches and at the saloon's door; the unhappy on the square in the evening; travellers walking in
  (`tnDrawList`, added to the townsfolk's list in `pixLists`); the **Look card** (a tap on a townsperson, a rail man, a
  hired hand, a traveller, a home or a shop in the pixel view: role, home, work, household, purse, mood; a shop's stock,
  open or shut, today's travellers; `tnTap` in the input after `pdTap`, the card is D1's `#lookCard` through
  `lookCardOf`'s new kind `'tn'`); the dev panel's town line (`tnDevLine`: shops open and which are empty, households paid
  yesterday, moods, travellers today).
- **For Rooms**: `tnShop(S, id)` `{ stock (null for a shop that keeps none), open, custom }`, `tnHome(S, who)` `{ pay,
  mood (0 content, 1 worried, 2 unhappy) }` by home building or member id; `tnRoomOpen()` stays his.

**The tables.** `TN` (every number above), `TN_PEOPLE`, `TN_ROLES`, `TN_ROUTINES`, `TN_ERRANDS`, `TN_SQUARE`, TOWN27's
rows and `tnLanes`; households are worked out from `TF_AGENTS` (`tnHouseholds`).

**The store** `S.tn = { day, key, shop: { [id]: { s, z, o, c, cy } }, home: { [home]: { p, u, lp } }, vis, vn, cr, tot }`
(the head comment lists every field). Optional, schema 19 unchanged: a new game gets it at its first tick, a save
without it at its next 05:00 (its townsfolk are re-planned at once by W's rule, without the economy until then);
`tnCheckSaveShape` refuses a damaged one. The day's plans are cached by seed, day and `S.tn.key` (the shut shops and
unhappy homes), so the planner stays a pure function of the state.

**Shared lines touched (each marked "D32 Town" or "D32 (Town)").** TOWN27's block of rows; W's `townGraphBuild` (the new
lane), `tfPlans` (`tnd`: the routine and place hooks, the cache key), `tfPlanDay`, `tfTick` (`tnRoll` first),
`tfDrawList`; `BLD_LAYOUTS.proto` (the houses' `tn`); the renderer (`tnHouseKey` for a house27's key, the lit-window
test knows `house_`, `tnPixItems` after `hwPixItems`, `tnDrawList` in `pixLists`, `tnLaneAt` in `pixCellTile`); Crew's
`hcRoll` (a man in town); `lookCardOf` and `onTap` (the Look card); `dvStatusText` (the town line); `__railhead.tn`.

**How to test.** `node tests/run-one.js check-bigtown` (the people, houses and graph, a week with a crew of 30: the
round at 10:00, the noon meal, Sunday church, everyone at home at every 22:00 head count, nobody on a blocked cell, the
rail men's Saturday and Sunday, the same week twice and after a save, the line's fronts equal to `?tn=0`; screenshots
`bigtown-{lanes,street}-portrait.png`) and `node tests/run-one.js check-towneconomy` (a week of full deliveries, a week
of none, travellers' custom, saves and damaged stores, the Look cards and the dev line by real taps; screenshots
`towneconomy-{closed,look,square}-portrait.png`). Both use scripted trains (as check-townsfolk) and hand in the day's
goods at 18:00 through `TN_TEST.tnFeed`, so they take seconds.

**Speed.** Measured with check-pixperf (4x-throttled CPU, best of three windows, average ms; its scenes add 7 sorted things with the town on: the new houses at the furthest zoom). Before this work: the station at the furthest zoom 7.74 portrait, 9.76 landscape; the default zoom 5.8, 5.4; the street at dusk 2.56, 3.2 (default) and 4.61, 5.79 (furthest). The same build with `?tn=0`: 6.61, 8.81; 5.49, 5.37; 2.6, 2.92 and 5.29, 6.0. With the town on, two runs: the station at the furthest zoom 9.45 and 6.87 portrait, 8.6 and 7.67 landscape (its own budget is 10); the default zoom 6.18 and 4.46, 5.01 and 6.15; the street at dusk 4.39 and 3.76, 4.14 and 3.55 (default), 5.43 and 5.02, 6.96 and 7.53 (furthest). All within the budgets; the run-to-run noise (up to 3 ms here) is larger than the difference. The sim side (unthrottled): `tfDrawList` with 41 people 18 microseconds (2 with 19), `tnDrawList` 0.1, `tnPixItems` 0.6; a week of the town and a crew of 30 about 1.3 s of sim. What costs is drawing more people outdoors: at 19:45 on a weekday about 15 of the 41 are outdoors (1 of the 19 before).

**Provisional choices for the designer.**
1. The ten houses' places (five between the D27 houses, five on a third lane west of the town, north of the camp; no
   third lane east, where the dry wash is) and kinds (four cabins, four frame houses, two two-room houses), with D27
   house designs standing in until the artist's art lands.
2. The 22 new people, their names (all the builder's), roles, homes and families; Tommy Pratt moved in with his
   parents over the store; three households living over shops gained a member.
3. The routines' times (shops 07:30, clerks 09:00, hands 07:00, the cook 06:00 every day, the barmaid 16:00 to 21:15,
   home by 21:30; the sheriff's saloon round now ends at 21:15 and the cowhands ride home by 21:20).
4. Stock: 3 a good (6 a big one), the daily use by shop, a cap of 12, two days' stock at the start.
5. Open or shut: one morning of empty shelves, shut on the second; the barber, sheriff and assay office keep no stock.
6. Pay 2 coins an earner a day, upkeep 1, a purse 0 to 20 starting at 8; the preacher, the teacher and the sheriff are
   always paid (the town and the county pay them).
7. A shut shop's keeper waits at its door all day (so a delivery can still be made); its hands idle at home or at the
   saloon's door.
8. Travellers: 1 in 3 into town, half to the hotel and half to the saloon, an hour, 1 in 10 of the hotel's overnight;
